He kissed Charlotte good night and walked in the streets until morning. Hugo could see no solution. Charlotte was so trusting, so good to him. He could not imagine how she would receive any suggestion that she go to New York and get a job, while he returned to college, that he see her during vacations, that he send money to her. But he knew that a hot fire dwelt within her and that her fury would rise, her grief, and that he would be made very miserable and ashamed. She chided him at breakfast for his walk in the dark. She laughed and kissed him and pushed him bodily to his work. He looked back as he walked down to the curb. She was leaning out of the window. She waved her hand. He rounded the corner with wretched, leaden steps. The morning, concerned with the petty business of receipts, refurbishings, cleaning, went slowly. When he returned for lunch it was with the decision to tell her the truth about his life and its requirements and to let her decide.

===Chinese===
他吻别了夏洛特，在街上一直走到天亮。雨果想不出任何解决办法。夏洛特这样信任他，对他这样好。他无法想象她会怎样接受任何一种提议——让她去纽约找个活儿，他回学校去，放假时来看她，寄钱给她。可他知道她胸中住着一团烈火，知道她的怒气会窜起来，她的悲恸会涌上来，知道他会被弄得痛苦不堪、无地自容。早饭时她嗔怪他在黑夜里走了那么久。她笑着吻他，把他连人带身子推出门去做工。他走到路边又回头看。她正探身窗外。她朝他挥手。他迈着凄惶而沉重的步子拐过街角。那个上午在收账、修葺、清扫的琐碎营生里慢慢过去。他回来吃午饭时，已经拿定主意，要把他生活的真相和它的要求告诉她，让她自己来决定。

===Original===
She did not come to the door to kiss him. (She had imagined that lonely return.) She did not answer his brave and cheerful hail. (She had let the sound of it ring upon her ear a thousand times.) She was gone. (She knew he would sit down and cry.) Then, stumbling, he found the two notes. But he already understood.

===Chinese===
她没有到门口来吻他。（她早已想象过那样孤单的归来。）她没有应答他勇敢而欢快的一声招呼。（她已让那声音在她耳畔响过一千遍。）她走了。（她知道他会坐下来哭。）然后，他跌跌撞撞地，找到了那两张字条。可他其实早已明白了。

===Original===
The message from Valentine Mitchel was reckless, impetuous. “Dear Hugo—Charlotte and I have fallen in love with each other and I’ve run away with her. I almost wish you’d come after us and kill me. I hate myself for betraying you. But I love her, so I cannot help it. I’ve learned to see in her what you first saw in her. Goodbye, good luck.”

===Chinese===
瓦伦丁·米切尔的字条写得鲁莽冲动。“亲爱的雨果——夏洛特和我相爱了，我带她私奔了。我几乎希望你追上来杀了我。我恨自己背叛了你。可我爱她，无法自已。我已学会在她身上看见你最初在她身上看见的东西。再见，祝好运。”

===Original===
Hugo put it down. Charlotte would be good to him. In a way, he didn’t deserve her. And when he was famous, some day, perhaps she would leave him, too. He hesitated to read her note. “Goodbye, darling, I do not love you any more. C.”

It was ludicrous, transparent, pitiful, and heroic. Hugo saw all those qualities. “Goodbye, darling, I do not love you any more.” She had written it under Valentine’s eyes. But she was shrewd enough to placate her new lover while she told her sad little story to her old. She would want him to feel bad. Well, God knew, he did. Hugo looked at the room. He sobbed. He bolted into the street, tears streaming down his cheeks; he drew his savings from the bank—seven hundred and eighty-four dollars and sixty-four cents; he rushed to the haunted house, flung his clothes into a bag; he sat drearily on a subway for an hour. He paced the smooth floor of a station. He swung aboard a train. He came to Webster, his head high, feeling a great pride in Charlotte and in his love for her, walking in glad strides over the familiar soil.

===Chinese===
雨果把它放下。夏洛特会待他好的。从某种意义上说，是他配不上她。等他有朝一日出了名，也许她照样会离开他。他迟迟不肯读她的字条。“再见，亲爱的，我不再爱你了。夏。”

这字条可笑、透明、可怜，而又英勇。所有这些质地雨果都看见了。“再见，亲爱的，我不再爱你了。”她是在瓦伦丁的眼皮底下写的。可她精明得很，一边安抚好新情人，一边把这则小小的伤心故事讲给旧情人听。她会希望他难受。唉，天知道，他确实难受。雨果看了看这间屋子。他呜咽起来。他夺门冲到街上，泪水顺着两颊奔流；他从银行取出了存款——七百八十四美元六十四美分；他冲回那幢魂牵梦萦的屋子，把衣服胡乱塞进一只提包；他蔫蔫地在地铁上坐了一个钟头。他在一座车站光滑的地面上踱步。他一步跨上一列火车。他回到韦伯斯特，昂着头，为夏洛特、也为他对她的爱感到巨大的骄傲，迈着欢快的步子走过那片熟悉的土地。

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
Hugo sat alone and marvelled at the exquisite torment of his Weltschmertz. Far away, across the campus, he heard singing. Against the square segment of sky visible from the bay window of his room he could see the light of the great fire they had built to celebrate victory—his victory. The light leaped into the darkness above like a great golden ghost in some fantastic ascension, and beneath it, he knew, a thousand students were dancing. They were druid priests at a rite to the god of football. His fingers struggled through his black hair. The day was fresh in his mind—the bellowing stands, the taut, almost frightened faces of the eleven men who faced him, the smack and flight of the brown oval, the lumbering sound of men running, the sucking of the breath of men and their sharp, painful fall to earth.

In his mind was a sharp picture of himself and the eyes that watched him as he broke away time and again, with infantile ease, to carry that precious ball. He let them make a touchdown that he could have averted. He made one himself. Then another. The bell on Webster Hall was booming its paean of victory. He stiffened under the steady monody. He remembered again. Lefty barking signals with a strange agony in his voice. Lefty pounding on his shoulder. “Go in there, Hugo, and give it to them. I can’t.” Lefty pleading. And the captain, Jerry Painter, cursing in open jealousy of Hugo, vying hopelessly with Hugo Danner, the man who was a god.

It was not fair. Not right. The old and early glory was ebbing from it. When he put down the ball, safely across the goal for the winning touchdown, he saw three of the men on the opposing team lie down and weep. There he stood, pretending to pant, feigning physical distress, making himself a hero at the expense of innocent victims. Jackstraws for a giant. There was no triumph in that. He could not go on.

===Chinese===
雨果独自坐着，惊叹于自己的「世界苦」（Weltschmerz）那精微的煎熬。远处，校园的另一头，传来歌声。从他房间的凸窗望得见的那一方天空上，他能看见他们为庆祝胜利——他一个人的胜利——点起的那堆巨火的光。火光跃入上空的黑暗，像一道巨大的金色幽魂在进行某种奇幻的飞升；他知道，火光底下，一千名学生正在跳舞。他们如同正在向橄榄球之神举行祭典的德鲁伊祭司。他的手指在黑发间挣扎。这一天犹在眼前——咆哮的看台，面对他的那十一个人绷紧的、近乎惊惶的脸，棕色椭圆球的拍击与飞出，众人奔跑时沉重的脚步声，人的喘息的抽吸，以及他们锐利而痛苦的倒地。

他脑海里有一幅鲜明的画面：他自己，以及他一次又一次以婴孩般轻易的身手甩开众人、抱着那颗宝贵的球时，注视着他的那些眼睛。有一个达阵他本可以阻止，他却放对方达了阵。他自己达了一个阵。然后又一个。韦伯斯特堂（Webster Hall）上的钟正轰鸣着胜利的赞歌。他在那单调不息的钟声下绷紧了身子。他又回想起来。左撇子喊战术口令时嗓音里那种奇异的痛楚。左撇子捶着他的肩膀。「进去吧，雨果，给他们点厉害的。我办不到。」左撇子在恳求。还有队长杰里·佩因特（Jerry Painter），公然出于对雨果的嫉妒而咒骂，绝望地同雨果·丹纳——那个如同神明的人——一较高下。

这不公平。不正当。那古老而最初的荣光正从中消退。当他抱着球安全越过球门线、放下制胜的一记达阵时，他看见对方队里有三个人躺倒在地哭泣。他就站在那里，假装喘息，佯装体力不支，靠牺牲无辜的受害者把自己造就成英雄。在巨人眼里，他们不过是挑棍游戏里的小细棒。这里面没有任何胜利可言。他无法再继续下去了。

===Original===
Afterwards they had made him speak, and the breathless words that had once come so easily moved heavily through his mind. Yet he had carried his advantage beyond the point of turning back. He could not say that the opponents of Webster might as well attempt to hold back a Juggernaut, to throw down a siege-gun, to outrace light, as to lay their hands on him to check his intent. Webster had been good to him. He loved Webster and it deserved his best. His best! He peered again into the celebrating night and wondered what that awful best would be.

He desired passionately to be able to give that—to cover the earth, making men glad and bringing a revolution into their lives, to work himself into a fury and to fatigue his incredible sinews, to end with the feeling of a race well run, a task nobly executed. And, for a year, that ambition had seemed in some small way to be approaching fruition. Now it was turned to ashes. It was not with the muscles of men that his goal was to be attained. They could not oppose him.

As he sat gloomy and distressed, he wondered for what reason there burned in him that wish to do great deeds. Humanity itself was too selfish and too ignorant to care. It could boil in its tiny prejudices for centuries to come and never know that there could be a difference. Moreover, who was he to grind his soul and beat his thoughts for the benefit of people who would never know and never care? What honour, when he was dead, to lie beneath a slab on which was punily graven some note of mighty accomplishment? Why could he not content himself with the food he ate, the sunshine, with wind in trees, and cold water, and a woman? It was that sad and silly command within to transcend his vegetable self that made him human. He tried to think about it bitterly: fool man, grown suddenly more conscious than the other beasts—how quickly he had become vain because of it and how that vanity led him forever onward! Or was it vanity—when his aching soul proclaimed that he would gladly achieve and die without other recognition or acclaim than that which rose within himself? Martyrs were made of such stuff. And was not that, perhaps, an even more exaggerated vanity? It was so pitiful to be a man and nothing more. Hugo bowed his head and let his body tremble with strange agony. Perhaps, he thought, even the agony was a selfish pleasure to him. Then he should be ashamed. He felt shame and then thought that the feeling rose from a wish for it and foundered angrily in the confusion of his introspection. He knew only and knew but dimly that he would lift himself up again and go on, searching for some universal foe to match against his strength. So pitiful to be a man! So Christ must have felt in Gethsemane.

===Chinese===
事后他们让他讲话，那些曾经脱口而出的气喘吁吁的言辞，此刻在他心里沉重地移行。然而他已把自己的优势带到了无可回头的地方。他无法说出这样的话：韦伯斯特的对手们想伸手拦住他去实现他的意图，还不如去设法拦住一辆横冲直撞的贾格纳特神车（Juggernaut），去掀翻一门攻城炮，去跑赢光速。韦伯斯特待他不薄。他爱韦伯斯特，它配得上他的全力以赴。他的全力以赴！他又凝望那欢庆的夜，不知道那可怕的全力以赴将会是什么。

他热切地渴望能够献出那份力量——遍布大地，让人们欢喜，给他们的生活带来一场革命，让自己干到狂热，让那不可思议的筋骨疲惫不堪，最终怀着一场漂亮的赛跑、一件高贵地完成了的工作的心情收场。而有一年之久，那志向似乎曾以某种微小的方式接近于结出果实。如今它化成了灰烬。他的目标并非要凭人类的肌肉去达成。他们根本无法与他抗衡。

他阴郁而苦闷地坐着，不明白自己心中究竟为何燃烧着建立伟业的愿望。人类本身太自私、太无知，不会在乎。它可以在此后数百年里在自己狭小的偏见中翻滚沸腾，永远不知道事情本可以两样。再说，他是谁，要为那些永远不会知晓、也永远不会在乎的人磨砺灵魂、锤打思想？等他死了，躺在一块石板底下，板上寒酸地刻着几行丰功伟绩的记录，那算什么荣耀？他为什么不能以入口的饭菜、阳光、林间的风、清凉的水和一个女人自满自足？正是那超越自身草木之性的悲哀而愚蠢的内在命令，使他成为人。他试图刻薄地想这件事：愚蠢的人，忽然变得比其他兽类更有自觉——他因此而多么快地虚荣起来，那虚荣又怎样永远引着他向前！抑或那并不是虚荣——当他疼痛的灵魂宣称，他甘愿建树功业然后死去，不求自身之内升起的认可与喝彩之外的任何承认？殉道者就是由这种材料做成的。而那，或许不还是一种更加夸张的虚荣么？做一个人，仅仅一个人，实在可怜。雨果低下头，任由身体因奇异的苦楚而颤抖。他想，或许连这苦楚于他也是一种自私的快乐。那么他该羞愧。他感到了羞愧，随即又想到这种感觉源于对羞愧的期求，便在自省的混乱中愠怒地沉浮。他只是隐约地知道一件事：他会重新振作起来，继续走下去，寻找某个足以与他之力相匹敌的普遍仇敌。做人真可怜！基督在客西马尼园（Gethsemane）里，想必也是这般滋味。

===Original===
“Hey, Hugo!”

“Yeah?”

“What the hell did you come over here for?”

“To be alone.”

“Is that a hint?” Lefty entered the room. “They want you over at the bonfire. We’ve been looking all over for you.”

“All right. I’ll go. But, honest to God, I’ve had enough of this business for today.”

===Chinese===
“嘿，雨果！”

“什么事？”

“你到底跑这儿来干什么？”

“想一个人待着。”

“这是在下逐客令吗？”左撇子走进房间。“篝火那边要你去呢。我们到处找你。”

“好吧。我去。可是老天在上，这事儿我今天就受够了。”

===Original===
Lefty slapped Hugo’s shoulders. “The great must pay for their celebrity. Come on, you sap.”

“All right.”

“What’s the matter? Anything the matter?”

“No. Nothing’s the matter. Only—it’s sort of sad to be—” Hugo checked himself.

“Sad? Good God, man, you’re going stale.”

“Maybe that’s it.” Hugo had a sudden fancy. “Do you suppose I could be let out of next week’s game?”

===Chinese===
左撇子拍拍雨果的肩膀。“伟人就得为名声付出代价。走吧，你这个呆子。”

“好吧。”

“怎么了？有什么不对劲？”

“没有。没什么。只是——做人总有点悲哀——”雨果把话收住了。

“悲哀？老天爷，伙计，你这是待得发霉了。”

“也许真是这么回事。”雨果忽然起了一个念头。“你看下周的比赛能不能免了我？”

===Original===
“What for? My God—”

Hugo pursued the idea. “It’s the last game. I can sit on the lines. You fellows all play good ball. You can probably win. If you can’t—then I’ll play. If you only knew, Lefty, how tired I get sometimes—”

“Tired! Why don’t you say something about it? You can lay off practice for three or four days.”

“Not that. Tired in the head, not the body. Tired of crashing through and always getting away with it. Oh, I’m not conceited. But I know they can’t stop me. You know it. It’s a gift of mine—and a curse. How about it? Let’s start next week without me.”

===Chinese===
“为什么？我的天——”

雨果顺着这念头说下去。“那是最后一场了。我可以坐在场边。你们几个都打得不错。多半能赢。要是赢不了——那我再上。你要是知道就好了，左撇子，我有时候有多累——”

“累！你怎么不早说？你可以歇练三四天。”

“不是那种累。是脑子里累，不是身子。是冲垮对手、屡屡得手冲烦了。哦，我不是自负。可我知道他们拦不住我。你也知道。这是我的天赋——也是个诅咒。怎么样？下周开场就别带我了吧。”

===Original===
The night ended at last. A new day came. The bell on Webster Hall stopped booming. Woodie, the coach, came to see Hugo between classes. “Lefty says you want us to start without you next week. What’s the big idea?”

“I don’t know. I thought the other birds would like a shot at Yale without me. They can do it.”

Mr. Woodman eyed his player. “That’s pretty generous of you, Hugo. Is there any other reason?”

“Not—that I can explain.”

===Chinese===
夜终于过去了。新的一天来了。韦伯斯特堂的钟不再轰鸣。教练伍迪趁着课间来看雨果。“左撇子说，你想让我们下周不带你就开场。到底打的什么主意？”

“我也说不上来。我想其他家伙会乐意少了我在场跟耶鲁较量一番。他们办得到。”

伍德曼先生打量着他的球员。“你这话可真大方，雨果。还有别的原因吗？”

“有——只是我解释不了。”

===Original===
“I see.” The coach offered Hugo a cigarette after he had helped himself. “Take it. It’ll do you good.”

“Thanks.”

“Listen, Hugo. I want to ask you a question. But, first, I want you to promise you’ll give me a plain answer.”

“I’ll try.”

“That won’t do.”

“Well—I can’t promise.”

===Chinese===
“明白了。”教练自己先取了一支烟，然后递给雨果一支。“拿着。对你有好处。”

“谢谢。”

“听着，雨果。我想问你一个问题。不过首先，你得答应给我一句实话。”

“我尽量。”

“那可不行。”

“这个——我答应不了。”

===Original===
Woodman sighed. “I’ll ask it anyway. You can answer or not—just as you wish.” He was silent. He inhaled his cigarette and blew the smoke through his nostrils. His eyes rested on Hugo with an expression of intense interest, beneath which was a softer light of something not unlike sympathy. “I’ll have to tell you something, first, Hugo. When you went away last summer, I took a trip to Colorado.”

Hugo started, and Woodman continued: “To Indian Creek. I met your father and your mother. I told them that I knew you. I did my best to gain their confidence. You see, Hugo, I’ve watched you with a more skilful eye than most people. I’ve seen you do things, a few little things, that weren’t—well—that weren’t—”

Hugo’s throat was dry. “Natural?”

“That’s the best word, I guess. You were never like my other boys, in any case. So I thought I’d find out what I could. I must admit that my efforts with your father were a failure. Aside from the fact that he is an able biology teacher and that he had a number of queer theories years ago, I learned nothing. But I did find out what those theories were. Do you want me to stop?”

===Chinese===
伍德曼叹了口气。“不管怎样我都要问。你答不答随意。”他沉默了片刻。他吸了口烟，从鼻孔里喷出烟来。他的目光落在雨果身上，带着一种极为关切的神情，那底下还透着一点柔和的光，很像同情。“我得先告诉你一件事，雨果。去年夏天你离开以后，我去了一趟科罗拉多。”

雨果一惊，伍德曼继续说：“去了印第安溪。我见了你父亲和你母亲。我告诉他们我认识你。我尽了全力去博得他们的信任。你要明白，雨果，我观察你，用的是比多数人更内行的眼光。我见过你做一些事，几件小事，那些事不那么——嗯——不那么——”

雨果喉咙发干。“自然？”

“我想这个词最贴切了。反正你从来都不像我这边的其他孩子。所以我想不妨去探个究竟。我得承认，我在你父亲身上的努力失败了。除了知道他是个有本事的生物学教师、多年前有过一整套古怪的理论之外，我一无所获。不过那些理论究竟是什么，我倒真打听到了。要我停下来吗？”

===Original===
A peculiar, almost hopeful expression was on Hugo’s face. “No,” he answered.

“Well, they had to do with the biochemistry of cellular structure, didn’t they? And with the production of energy in cells? And then—I talked to lots of people. I heard about Samson.”

“Samson!” Hugo echoed, as if the dead had spoken.

“Samson—the cat.”

Hugo was as pale as chalk. His eyes burned darkly. He felt that his universe was slipping from beneath him. “You know, then,” he said.

===Chinese===
雨果脸上有一种奇特的、近乎期盼的神情。“不，”他答道。

“是这样，那些理论和细胞结构的生物化学有关，对不对？和细胞里的能量产生有关？后来——我跟许多人聊过。我听说了参孙的事。”

“参孙！”雨果复述了一遍，仿佛是死者在开口说话。

“参孙——那只猫。”

雨果白得像粉笔。他的眼睛发黑地燃烧着。他感到自己的整个世界正从脚下滑走。“这么说，你知道了，”他说。

===Original===
“I don’t know, Hugo. I merely guessed. I was going to ask. Now I shall not. Perhaps I do know. But I had another question, son—”

“Yes?” Hugo looked at Woodman and felt then the reason for his success as a coach, as a leader and master of youth. He understood it.

“Well, I wondered if you thought it was worth while to talk to your father and discover—”

“What he did?” Hugo suggested hoarsely.

===Chinese===
“我并不知道，雨果。我只是猜测。我本打算问的。现在不问了。也许我确实知道。不过我还有另一个问题，孩子——”

“什么问题？”雨果望着伍德曼，此时才领悟到他身为教练、身为青年的领袖与导师之所以成功的缘由。他明白了。

“是这样，我在想，你是否觉得值得去同你父亲谈谈，弄清楚——”

“弄清楚他做了什么？”雨果嘶哑地接了一句。

===Original===
Woodman put his hand on Hugo’s knee. “What he did, son. You ought to know by this time what it means. I’ve been watching you. I don’t want your head to swell, but you’re a great boy, Hugo. Not only in beef. You have a brain and an imagination and a sense of moral responsibility. You’ll come out better than the rest—you would even without your—your particular talent. And I thought you might think that the rest of humanity would profit—”

Hugo jumped to his feet. “No. A thousand times no. For the love of Christ—no! You don’t know or understand, you can’t conceive, Woodie, what it means to have it. You don’t have the faintest idea of its amount—what it tempts you with—what they did to me and I did to myself to beat it—if I have beaten it.” He laughed. “Listen, Woodie. Anything I want is mine. Anything I desire I can take. No one can hinder. And sometimes I sweat all night for fear some day I shall lose my temper. There’s a desire in me to break and destroy and wreck that—oh, hell—”

Woodman waited. Then he spoke quietly. “You’re sure, Hugo, that the desire to be the only one—like that—has nothing to do with it?”

Hugo’s sole response was to look into Woodman’s eyes, a look so pregnant with meaning, so tortured, so humble, that the coach swore softly. Then he held out his hand. “Well, Hugo, that’s all. You’ve been damn swell about it. The way I hoped you would be. And I think my answer is plain. One thing. As long as I live, I promise on my oath I’ll never give you away or support any rumor that hurts your secret.”

===Chinese===
伍德曼把手放在雨果的膝上。“他做了什么，孩子。到如今你早该知道那意味着什么了。我一直在观察你。我不想叫你飘飘然，可你是个了不起的孩子，雨果。不只是块头上。你有头脑，有想象力，还有道德责任感。你会比其余的人走得更好——即便没有你那——你那项特殊的天赋，你也会。而我在想，你或许会认为，其余的人类将从中受益——”

雨果霍地跳起来。“不。一千个不。看在基督的分上——不行！你不知道也不明白，你想象不到，伍迪，拥有它意味着什么。你丝毫不了解它的分量——它拿什么诱惑你——他们对我做过什么，我又对自己做过什么来战胜它——如果我算是战胜了的话。”他笑了一声。“听着，伍迪。我想要的任何东西都是我的。我渴望的任何东西我都能拿到。没有人拦得住。有时候我整夜出汗，就怕哪一天我会发脾气。我心里有一种要砸烂、要毁坏、要把一切砸个稀烂的欲望，那——哦，见鬼——”

伍德曼等着。然后他平静地说：“你确定吗，雨果——那种想成为独一无二——像那样独一无二——的欲望，同这事毫无关系？”

雨果唯一的回应是直视伍德曼的眼睛，那目光意味如此深长、如此痛苦、如此谦卑，教练不由轻声咒骂了一句。然后他伸出手。“好了，雨果，就到这儿。你在这件事上真他妈够意思。正像我希望的那样。我想我的回答也清楚了。还有一件事。只要我活一天，我凭誓言保证，绝不出卖你，也绝不助长任何伤害你秘密的流言。”

===Original===
Even Hugo was stirred to a consciousness of the strength of the other man’s grip.

Saturday. A shrill whistle. The thump of leather against leather. The roar of the stadium.

Hugo leaned forward. He watched his fellows from the bench. They rushed across the field. Lefty caught the ball. Eddie Carter interfered with the first man, Bimbo Gaines with the second. The third slammed Lefty against the earth. Three downs. Eight yards. A kick. New Haven brought the ball to its twenty-one-yard line. The men in helmets formed again. A coughing voice. Pandemonium. Again in line. The voice. The riot of figures suddenly still. Again. A kick. Lefty with the ball, and Bimbo Gaines leading him, his big body a shield. Down. A break and a run for twenty-eight yards. Must have been Chuck. Good old Chuck. He’d be playing the game of his life. Graduation next spring. Four, seven, eleven, thirty-two, fifty-five. Hugo anticipated the spreading of the players. He looked where the ball would be thrown. He watched Minton, the end, spring forward, saw him falter, saw the opposing quarterback run in, saw Lefty thrown, saw the ball received by the enemy and moved up, saw the opposing back spilled nastily. His heart beat faster.

===Chinese===
连雨果也察觉到对方握手时那股力气之大。

星期六。一声尖利的哨响。皮与皮相撞的闷响。体育场的吼声。

雨果俯身向前。他坐在长凳上望着伙伴们。他们冲过场地。左撇子接到球。埃迪·卡特（Eddie Carter）拦住了第一个人，宾博·盖恩斯（Bimbo Gaines）拦住了第二个。第三个把左撇子撞倒在地。三档进攻。八码。一记踢球。纽黑文把球带到本方二十一码线。戴头盔的队员们重新列队。一声嘶哑的喊号。乱哄哄一片。再次列队。那喊号声。乱作一团的人影骤然凝住。又是一声。一记踢球。左撇子持球，宾博·盖恩斯在前面开路，用他高大的身躯作盾牌。倒地。一次突破，一次二十八码的奔袭。准是查克。好一个老查克。他今天准是在打他一生中最漂亮的一场。明年春天就毕业了。四号，七号，十一号，三十二号，五十五号。雨果预判着球员们的散开跑位。他望向球将被抛去的地方。他看着边锋明顿（Minton）跃出，见他一个踉跄，见对方的四分卫冲入，见左撇子被撞倒，见球落入对方手中向前推进，又见对方一名后卫被狠狠撞翻。他的心跳加快了。

===Original===
No score at the end of the first half. The third quarter witnessed the crossing of Webster’s goal. Struggling grimly, gamely, against a team that was their superior without Hugo, against a team heartened by the knowledge that Hugo was not facing it, Webster’s players were being beaten. The goal was not kicked. It made the score six to nothing against Webster. Hugo saw the captain rip off his headgear and throw it angrily on the ground. He understood all that was going on in the minds of his team in a clear, although remote, way. They went out to show that they could play the game without Hugo Danner. And they were not showing what they had hoped to show. A few minutes later their opponents made a second touchdown.

Thirteen to nothing. Mr. Woodman moved beside Hugo. “They can’t do it—and I don’t altogether blame them. They’ve depended on you too much. It’s too bad. We all have.”

Hugo nodded. “Shall I go in?”

The coach watched the next play. “I guess you better.”

===Chinese===
上半场结束，双方均无得分。第三节，韦伯斯特的球门线被攻破了。韦伯斯特的球员们苦苦地、拼死地奋战，对抗一支即便没有雨果也强于他们的队伍，对抗一支因得知雨果没有上场而士气大振的队伍，他们正被打败。附加分没有踢进。比分变成六比零，韦伯斯特落后。雨果看见队长猛地扯下头盔，怒气冲冲地摔在地上。虽然隔着一层距离，他仍旧清楚地懂得队友们心里翻腾着的一切。他们上场是为了证明，没有雨果·丹纳他们照样能打。可他们没能证明想证明的东西。几分钟后，对手又达了一次阵。

十三比零。伍德曼先生走到雨果身旁。“他们办不到——这我也不能全怪他们。他们太依赖你了。太糟糕了。我们都一样。”

雨果点点头。“要我上吗？”

教练看着下一档进攻。“我看你最好上。”

===Original===
When Hugo entered the line, Jerry Painter and Lefty spoke to him in strained tones. “You’ve got to take it over, Hugo—all the way.”

“All right.”

The men lined up. A tense silence had fallen on the Yale line. They knew what was going to happen. The signals were called, the ball shot back to Lefty, Hugo began to run, the men in front rushed together, and Lefty stuffed the ball into Hugo’s arms. “Go on,” he shouted. The touchdown was made in one play. Hugo saw a narrow hole and scooted into it. A man met his outstretched arm on the other side. Another. Hugo dodged twice. The crescendo roar of the Webster section came to him dimly. He avoided the safety man and ran to the goal. In the pandemonium afterwards, Jerry kicked the goal.

A new kickoff. Hugo felt a hand on his shoulder. “You’ve gotta break this up.” Hugo broke it up. He held Yale almost single-handed. They kicked back. Hugo returned the kick to the middle of the field. He did not dare to do more.

===Chinese===
雨果入场时，杰里·佩因特和左撇子语气紧绷地对他说。“你得把球接过去一路带到头，雨果。”

“好。”

队员们列队。耶鲁的防线上笼罩着紧张的沉默。他们知道将要发生什么。战术口令喊出，球飞传到左撇子手里，雨果起跑，前面的人撞作一团，左撇子把球塞进雨果怀里。“上，”他喊道。一档进攻就完成了达阵。雨果看见一道窄缝，一头钻了进去。一个人在另一头撞上他伸出的手臂。又一个人。雨果闪避了两下。韦伯斯特看台区那愈来愈响的吼声隐约传到他耳中。他避开了安全卫，直奔球门。随后的鼎沸喧嚣里，杰里踢进了附加分。

再次开球。雨果感到一只手落在他肩上。“你得把这波攻势给搅了。”雨果搅了它。他几乎是独自一人挡住了耶鲁。他们往回踢。雨果把球回踢到场地中央。再多的他就不敢做了。

===Original===
Then he stood in his leather helmet, bent, alert, waiting to run again. They called the captain’s signal. He made four yards. Then Lefty’s. He made a first down. Then Jerry’s. Two yards. Six yards. Five yards. Another first down. The stands were insane. Hugo was glad they were not using him—glad until he saw Jerry Painter’s face. It was pale with rage. Blood trickled across it from a small cut. Three tries failed. Hugo spoke to him. “I’ll take it over, Jerry, if you say so.”

Jerry doubled his fist and would have struck him if Hugo had not stepped back. “God damn you, Danner, you come out here in the last few minutes all fresh and make us look like a lot of fools. I tell you, my team and I will take that ball across and not you with your bastard tricks.”

“But, good God, man—”

“You heard me.”

“This is your last down.”

===Chinese===
然后他戴着皮头盔站在那里，弓着身，警觉着，等待再次奔跑。他们喊了队长的战术。推进了四码。然后是左撇子的战术。拿下一次首档。然后是杰里的。两码。六码。五码。又一次首档。看台疯了似的。雨果庆幸他们没有用他——一直庆幸到看见杰里·佩因特的脸。那张脸气得发白。一道小口子流下的血横过脸颊。三次尝试都失败了。雨果对他开口。“要是你发话，我就接过去，杰里。”

杰里攥起拳头，若不是雨果退后一步，他就要动手了。“该死的，丹纳，你偏偏等到最后几分钟才上场，精精神神的，倒显得我们像一群傻子。我告诉你，我的队伍和我自己会把球带过去得分，用不着你和你那些下三滥的把戏。”

“可是，老天爷，伙计——”

“你听见了。”

“这是你们最后一档了。”

===Original===
There was time for nothing more. Lefty called Jerry’s signal, and Jerry failed. The other team took the ball, rushed it twice, and kicked back into the Webster territory. Again the tired, dogged players began a march forward. The ball was not given to Hugo. He did his best, using his body as a ram to open holes in the line, tripping tacklers with his body, fighting within the limits of an appearance of human strength to get his teammates through to victory. And Jerry, still pale and profane, drove the men like slaves. It was useless. If Hugo had dared more, they might have succeeded. But they lost the ball again. It was only in the last few seconds that an exhausted and victorious team relinquished the ball to Webster.

Jerry ordered his own number again. Hugo, cold and somewhat furious at the vanity and injustice of the performance, gritted his teeth. “How about letting me try, Jerry? I can make it. It’s for Webster—not for you.”

“You go to hell.”

Lefty said: “You’re out of your head, Jerry.”

“I said I’d take it.”

===Chinese===
再没有时间多说什么了。左撇子喊了杰里的战术，杰里失败了。对方拿了球，两次冲击，然后把球踢回韦伯斯特半场。疲惫而不屈的球员们又一次开始向前推进。球没有交给雨果。他尽了全力，把身体当作攻城槌在防线上撞开缺口，用身体绊倒拦截者，在看似人类力气的限度之内拼搏，好把队友们送向胜利。而杰里，依旧脸色发白、满口脏话，像驱使奴隶一样驱使着众人。没有用。倘若雨果敢做得更多，他们本可能成功。可他们又丢了球。直到最后几秒，一支精疲力竭而获胜了的球队才把球权让给韦伯斯特。

杰里又点了自己那个号码。雨果对这场逞强与不公又冷又怒，咬紧了牙关。“让我来试试怎么样，杰里？我办得到。这是为了韦伯斯特——不是为了你。”

“你见鬼去吧。”

左撇子说：“你疯了，杰里。”

“我说过我来带球。”

===Original===
For one instant Hugo looked into his eyes. And in that instant the captain saw a dark and flickering fury that filled him with fear. The whistle blew. And then Hugo, to his astonishment, heard his signal. Lefty was disobeying the captain. He felt the ball in his arms. He ran smoothly. Suddenly he saw a dark shadow in the air. The captain hit him on the jaw with all his strength. After that, Hugo did not think lucidly. He was momentarily berserk. He ran into the line raging and upset it like a row of tenpins. He raced into the open. A single man, thirty yards away, stood between him and the goal. The man drew near in an instant. Hugo doubled his arm to slug him. He felt the arm straighten, relented too late, and heard, above the chaos that was loose, a sudden, dreadful snap. The man’s head flew back and he dropped. Hugo ran across the goal. The gun stopped the game. But, before the avalanche fell upon him, Hugo saw his victim lying motionless on the field. What followed was nightmare. The singing and the cheering. The parade. The smashing of the goal posts. The gradual descent of silence. A pause. A shudder. He realized that he had been let down from the shoulders of the students. He saw Woodman, waving his hands, his face a graven mask. The men met in the midst of that turbulence.

“You killed him, Hugo.”

The earth spun and rocked slowly. He was paying his first price for losing his temper. “Killed him?”

“His neck was broken-in three places.”

===Chinese===
有一瞬，雨果直视着他的眼睛。就在那一瞬，队长看见了一种幽暗而闪烁的狂怒，顿时满心恐惧。哨声响了。接着，雨果惊讶地听到了自己的号码。左撇子违抗了队长。他感到球已在怀中。他平稳地跑起来。忽然他看见空中一道黑影。队长用尽全力打在他的下颌上。那之后，雨果便不再能清醒地思考了。他一时狂乱如疯。他狂怒地冲进防线，把它掀得像一排立柱。他奔入空地。一个人，在三十码外，立在他与球门之间。那人顷刻逼近。雨果弯起手臂要去猛击。他感到手臂已经击直，心生不忍已太迟，随即在满场失控的喧嚣之上，听见一声突兀而可怕的脆响。那人的头向后一扬，倒了下去。雨果跑过球门线。终场的枪声让比赛停止了。可是，在人潮雪崩般压到他身上之前，雨果看见他的受害者一动不动地躺在球场上。随后的一切是一场噩梦。歌声与欢呼。游行。球门柱被捣毁。寂静渐渐降临。一阵停顿。一阵战栗。他发觉自己已被从学生们的肩头上放了下来。他看见伍德曼挥着双手，脸像一张雕成的面具。两人在那片骚动的中央相遇了。

“你把他杀了，雨果。”

大地旋转起来，缓缓摇晃。他正在为发脾气付出第一笔代价。“杀了？”

“他的脖子断了——断在三处。”

===Original===
Some of the others heard. They walked away. Presently Hugo was standing alone on the cinders outside the stadium. Lefty came up. “I just heard about it. Tough luck. But don’t let it break you.”

Hugo did not answer. He knew that he was guilty of a sort of murder. In his own eyes it was murder. He had given away for one red moment to the leaping, lusting urge to smash the world. And killed a man. They would never accuse him. They would never talk about it. Only Woodman, perhaps, would guess the thing behind the murder—the demon inside Hugo that was tame, except then, when his captain in jealous and inferior rage had struck him.

It was night. Out of deference to the body of the boy lying in the Webster chapel there was no celebration. Every ounce of glory and joy had been drained from the victory. The students left Hugo to a solitude that was more awful than a thousand scornful tongues. They thought he would feel as they would feel about such an accident. They gave him respect when he needed counsel. As he sat by himself, he thought that he should tell them the truth, all of them, confess a crime and accept the punishment. Hours passed. At midnight Woodman called.

===Chinese===
旁边有几个人听见了。他们走开了。不多时，雨果独自站在体育场外的煤渣地上。左撇子走上前来。“我刚听说。太不走运了。可别让这事把你压垮。”

雨果没有回答。他知道自己犯下了某种谋杀。在他自己眼里，这就是谋杀。有那么狂乱的一刻，他向那跳跃的、狂热的、要砸碎世界的冲动缴了械。于是杀了一个人。他们永远不会指控他。他们永远不会谈论这件事。或许只有伍德曼会猜到这谋杀背后的东西——雨果体内那头驯顺的恶魔，只有那一刻例外，当他的队长怀着嫉妒而自卑的狂怒打了他的时候。

夜来了。出于对停放在韦伯斯特教堂里的那个男孩遗体的敬重，没有任何庆祝。胜利中的每一分荣耀与喜悦都被榨干了。学生们把雨果留在一种比一千条刻毒的舌头更可怕的孤独里。他们以为，对这样一场意外，他的感受会同他们一样。他需要的是有人指点，他们给的却是敬意。他独坐着，心想自己应当把真相告诉他们，告诉所有的人，供认一桩罪，领受惩罚。几个钟头过去。午夜时分，伍德曼来了。

===Original===
“There isn’t much to say, Hugo. I’m sorry, you’re sorry, we’re all sorry. But it occurred to me that you might do something foolish—tell these people all about it, for example.”

“I was going to.”

“Don’t. They’d never understand. You’d be involved in a legal war that would undoubtedly end in your acquittal. But it would drag in all your friends—and your mother and father—particularly him. The papers would go wild. You might, on the other hand, be executed as a menace. You can’t tell.”

“It might be a good thing,” Hugo answered bitterly.

===Chinese===
“没什么好多说的，雨果。我难过，你难过，我们都难过。不过我想到，你也许会做什么蠢事——比如，把这事儿原原本本告诉这些人。”

“我正打算这样。”

“别。他们永远不会理解。你会卷进一场官司，毫无疑问会以无罪开释告终。可那会把你的朋友全都拖下水——还有你母亲和你父亲——尤其是他。报纸会闹翻天。另一方面，你也可能被当作一个祸害处决。谁也说不好。”

“那说不定倒是件好事，”雨果辛酸地答道。

===Original===
“Don’t let me hear you say that, you fool! I tell you, Hugo, if you go into that business, I’ll get up on the stand and say I knew it all the time and I let a man play on my team when I was pretty sure that sooner or later he’d kill someone. Then I’ll go to jail surely.”

“You’re a pretty fine man, Mr. Woodman.”

“Hell!”

“What shall I do?” Hugo’s voice trembled. He suffered as he had not dreamed it was possible to suffer.

“That’s up to you. I’d say, live it down.”

“Live it down! Do you know what that means—in a college?”

===Chinese===
“别让我再听见你这么说，你这个傻子！我告诉你，雨果，你要是趟这摊浑水，我就上证人席去说：我自始至终都知道，我说过一个我确信迟早会杀死人的家伙在我的球队里打球。那我就肯定要蹲监狱了。”

“你真是个好人，伍德曼先生。”

“见鬼！”

“我该怎么办？”雨果的声音发颤。他受着做梦也想不到可能承受的苦。

“那由你决定。要我说，熬过去，让人淡忘。”

“熬过去让人淡忘！你知道那意味着什么吗——在大学里？”

===Original===
“Yes, I think I do, Hugo.”

“You can live down almost anything, except that one thing—murder. It’s too ugly, Woodie.”

“Maybe. Maybe. You’ve got to decide, son. If you decide against trying—and, mind you, you might be justified—I’ve got a brother-in-law who has a ranch in Alberta. A couple of hundred miles from any place. You’d be welcome there.”

Hugo did not reply. He took the coach’s hand and wrung it. Then for an hour the two men sat side by side in the darkness. At last Woodman rose and left. He said only: “Remember that offer. It’s cold and bleak and the work is hard. Good night, Hugo.”

“Good night, Woodie. Thanks for coming up.”

===Chinese===
“是的，我想我知道，雨果。”

“几乎什么事都能熬到人淡忘，唯独那一件事不能——谋杀。它太丑恶了，伍迪。”

“也许。也许。你得自己拿主意，孩子。要是你决定不试了——而且，记住，你也未必没有道理——我有个姐夫在艾伯塔（Alberta）有个牧场。离任何地方都有两百英里。你去那儿是受欢迎的。”

雨果没有作答。他握住教练的手，紧紧一握。随后两个人在黑暗中并肩坐了一个钟头。最后伍德曼起身走了。他只说了一句：“记住那个提议。那儿又冷又荒凉，活儿也重。晚安，雨果。”

“晚安，伍迪。谢谢你跑这一趟。”

===Original===
When the campus was still with the quiet of sleep, Hugo crossed it as swiftly as a spectre. All night he strode remorselessly over country roads. His face was set. His eyes burned. He ignored the trembling of his joints. When the sky faded, he went back. He packed his clothes in two suitcases. With them swinging at his side, he stole out of the Psi Delta house, crossed the campus, stopped. For a long instant he stared at Webster Hall. The first light of morning was just touching it. The debris collected for a fire that was never lighted was strewn around the cannon. He saw the initials he had painted there a year and more ago still faintly legible. A lump rose in his throat.

“Goodbye, Webster,” he said. He lifted the suitcase and vanished. In a few minutes the campus was five miles behind him—six—ten—twenty. When he saw the first early caravan of produce headed toward the market, he slowed to a walk. The sun came over a hill and sparkled on a billion drops of dew. A bird flew singing from his path. Hugo Danner had fled beyond the gates of Webster.

===Chinese===
等校园在睡眠的静寂里沉寂下来，雨果像一道幽灵般飞快地穿过了它。整整一夜，他狠着心在乡间的路上大步疾走。他绷着一张脸。双眼燃烧。他不理会关节的颤抖。天色泛白时，他回去了。他把衣裳装进两只手提箱。提着它们在身侧摇荡，他溜出Psi Delta（ΨΔ）兄弟会的房子，穿过校园，停住了脚步。有长长的一瞬，他凝望着韦伯斯特堂。清晨最初的光正刚刚触到它。为那堆始终没有点燃的篝火收集来的杂物，散在那门大炮四周。他看见一年多前自己在那上面漆下的姓名缩写，仍隐约可辨。一团哽咽涌上他的喉头。

“再见了，韦伯斯特，”他说。他提起提箱，人就不见了。几分钟工夫，校园已落在他身后五英里——六英里——十英里——二十英里。当他看见头一支赶早运往市场的菜车队时，他放慢成步行。太阳翻过一座小山，在亿万颗露珠上闪耀。一只鸟唱着歌从他的路上飞起。雨果·丹纳已经逃出了韦伯斯特的大门。

## X / 第十章

===Original===
A year passed. In the harbour of Cristobal, at the northern end of the locks, waiting for the day to open the great steel jaws that dammed the Pacific from the Atlantic, the *Katrina* pulled at her anchor chain in the gentle swell. A few stars, liquid bright, hung in the tropical sky. A little puff of wind coming occasionally from the south carried the smell of the jungle to the ship. The crew was awakening.

A man with a bucket on a rope went to the rail and hauled up a brimming pail from the warm sea. He splashed his face and hands into it. Then he poured it back and repeated the act of dipping up water.

“Hey!” he said.

Another man joined him. “Here. Swab off your sweat. Look yonder.”

The dorsal fin of a shark rippled momentarily on the surface and dipped beneath it. A third man appeared. He accepted the proffered water and washed himself. His roving eye saw the shark as it rose for the second time. He dried on a towel. The offshore breeze stirred his dark hair. There was a growth of equally dark beard on his tanned jaw and cheek. Steely muscles bulged under his shirt. His forearm, when he picked up the pail, was corded like cable. A smell of coffee issued from the galley, and the smoke of the cook’s fire was wafted on deck for a pungent moment. Two bells sounded. The music went out over the water in clear, humming waves.

The man who had come first from the forecastle leaned his buttocks against the rail. One end of it had been unhooked to permit the discharge of mail. The rail ran, the man fell back, clawing, and then, thinking suddenly of the sharks, he screamed. The third man looked. He saw his fellow-seaman go overboard. He jumped from where he stood, clearing the scuppers and falling through the air before the victim of the slack rail had landed in the water. The two splashes were almost simultaneous. A boatswain, hearing the cry, hastened to the scene. He saw one man lifted clear of the water by the other, who was treading water furiously. He shouted for a rope. He saw the curve and dip of a fin. The first man seized the rope and climbed and was pulled up. The second, his rescuer, dived under water as if aware of something there that required his attention. The men above him could not know that he had felt the rake of teeth across his leg—powerful teeth, which nevertheless did not penetrate his skin. As he dived into the green depths, he saw a body lunge toward him, turn, yawn a white-fringed mouth. He snatched the lower jaw in one hand, and the upper in the other. He exerted his strength. The mouth gaped wider, a tail twelve feet behind it lashed, the thing died with fingers like steel claws tearing at its brain. It floated belly up. The man rose, took the rope, climbed aboard. Other sharks assaulted the dead one.

===Chinese===
一年过去了。在克里斯托瓦尔（Cristobal）的港内，船闸的北端，「卡特琳娜」号（*Katrina*）在轻柔的涌浪中拽着锚链，等候天亮，等候那拦断太平洋与大西洋的巨大钢颚开启。几颗星星如液体的亮光，悬在热带的天空上。偶尔从南面吹来的一阵阵小风，把丛林的气味送上船来。船员们正在醒来。

一个提着系绳水桶的男人走到栏杆边，从温暖的海里拽上来满满一桶水。他把脸和手浸进水里扑洗。然后又把水倒回海中，重复舀水的动作。

「嘿！」他说。

另一个人凑了过来。「来，擦把汗。瞧那边。」

一条鲨鱼的背鳍在水面上掠起一阵涟漪，随即沉了下去。第三个人出现了。他接过递来的水，洗了洗。他那逡巡的目光看见了第二次浮上来的鲨鱼。他用毛巾擦干身子。离岸的风吹动他深色的头发。晒黑的下巴和面颊上生着同样深色的胡茬。钢铁般的肌肉在衬衫下隆起。他提起水桶时，前臂的筋腱如缆绳般虬结。厨房里飘出咖啡的气味，厨灶的烟被送上甲板，呛人地停了一瞬。两响船钟敲响。那乐音化作清朗而嗡鸣的波纹，传向水面之外。

最先从船头楼出来的那个男人把身子靠在栏杆上。栏杆的一端为了缷放邮件被摘开了挂钩。栏杆滑脱，那人向后跌去，双手乱抓，随即猛然想起鲨鱼，便尖叫起来。第三个人望过去。他看见同伴落水。他从立足之处纵身跃起，越过排水口，在那截松脱栏杆的受害者落水之前便已穿空而坠。两声水响几乎同时。一名水手长听到喊声，急忙赶到现场。他看见其中一人被另一人完全托出水面，后者正猛烈地踩水。他大喊要绳子。他看见一面鱼鳍拱起又没入。第一个人抓住绳子攀爬，被拽了上来。第二个人，那位救人者，潜入水下，仿佛察觉水下有什么需要他去料理的东西。在他上方的人们无从知道，他已感到一副牙齿刮过他的腿——强健的牙齿，却终究没能咬穿他的皮肤。他潜入绿色深处时，看见一个庞大的身躯朝他猛扑过来，一转身，张开一张白边如须的巨口。他一手擒住下颚，一手擒住上颚。他使出力气。那张嘴豁得更开，十二英尺开外的一条尾巴抽打着，那东西在如钢爪般撕裂它脑髓的手指下断了气。它肚皮朝上浮起。那人浮出水面，抓住绳子，攀上船。其余的鲨鱼扑向了死去的这一条。

===Original===
The dripping sailor clasped his saviour’s hand. “God Almighty, man, you saved my life. Jesus!”

“That’s four,” Hugo Danner said abstractedly, and then he smiled. “It’s all right. Forget it. I’ve had a lot of experience with sharks.” He had never seen one before in his life. He walked aft, where the men grouped around him.

“How’d you do it?”

“It’s a trick I can’t explain very well,” Hugo said. “You use their rush to break their jaws. It takes a good deal of muscle.”

“Anyway—guy—thanks.”

“Sure.”

===Chinese===
浑身滴水的水手紧紧握住救命恩人的手。「全能的上帝啊，老兄，你救了我的命。老天爷！」

「这是第四个了。」雨果·丹纳心不在焉地说，随后他笑了。「没事。忘了吧。我跟鲨鱼打过很多交道。」他这辈子此前还从未见过一条鲨鱼。他向船尾走去，人们在那里围拢过来。

「你是怎么做到的？」

「那是个我没法解释得很清楚的技巧，」雨果说。「借它们扑冲的劲道来折断它们的颚。这得有相当大的力气。」

「不管怎么说——老兄——多谢了。」

「没什么。」

===Original===
A whistle blew. The ships were lining up in the order of their arrival for admission to the Panama Canal. Gatun loomed in the feeble sun of dawn. The anchor chain rumbled. The *Katrina* edged forward at half speed.

The sea. Blue, green, restless, ghost-ridden, driven in empty quarters by devils riding the wind, secretive, mysterious, making a last gigantic, primeval stand against the conquest of man, hemming and isolating the world, beautiful, horrible, dead god of ten thousand voices, universal incubator, universal grave.

The *Katrina* came to the islands in the South Pacific. Islands that issued from the water like green wreaths and seemed to float on it. The small boats were put out and sections of the cargo were sent to rickety wharves where white men and brown islanders took charge of it and carried it away into the fringe of the lush vegetation. Hugo, looking at those islands, was moved to smile. The place where broken men hid from civilization, where the derelicts of the world gathered to drown their shame in a verdant paradise that had no particular position in the white man’s scheme of the earth.

At one of the smaller islands an accident to the engine forced the *Katrina* to linger for two weeks. It was during those two weeks, in a rather extraordinary manner, that Hugo Danner laid the first foundation of the fortune that he accumulated in his later life. One day, idling away a leave on shore in the shade of a mighty tree, he saw the outriggers of the natives file away for the oyster beds, and, out of pure curiosity, he followed them. For a whole day he watched the men plunge under the surface in search of pearls. The next day he came back and dove with one of them.

===Chinese===
汽笛响了。各船正按抵达的先后次序排成队列，等候进入巴拿马运河。加通（Gatun）在黎明微弱的太阳里隐隐显现。锚链轰隆作响。「卡特琳娜」号以半速缓缓向前蹭动。

大海啊。蓝色的、绿色的、永不安宁的、鬼魅出没的，被乘风疾驰的魔鬼在空旷海域里驱赶着的，深藏秘密的、神秘莫测的，对人类的征服发起最后一场庞大而洪荒的抵抗的，把世界围困并隔绝起来的，美丽的、可怖的，发出万千声音的已死之神，普世的孵化器，普世的坟墓。

「卡特琳娜」号来到了南太平洋的岛屿。那些岛像绿色的花环从水里冒出来，仿佛漂浮在水面上。小艇放了出去，一部分货物被送上摇摇欲坠的码头，白人和棕色皮肤的岛民在那里接了手，把它搬进丰茂植被的边缘。雨果望着那些岛屿，不由得露出微笑。这是破碎之人躲避文明的地方，是被世界遗弃的人们聚集起来、在白人的世界版图上并无特定位置的青翠天堂里溺毙自己耻辱的地方。

在一座较小的岛上，引擎的一次故障迫使「卡特琳娜」号滞留了两周。正是在那两周里，雨果·丹纳以相当不寻常的方式，为他后半生积累的财富奠定了第一块基石。一天，他在岸上休假，在一棵大树的树荫下消磨时光，看见土著们的支腿独木舟鱼贯驶向牡蛎床，出于纯粹的好奇，他跟了上去。整整一天，他看着那些人潜入水下寻找珍珠。第二天他又来了，跟其中一人一起下了水。

===Original===
On the bizarre floor of the ocean, among the colossal fronds of its flora, the two men swam. They were invaders from the brilliance above the surface, shooting like fish, horizontally, through the murk and shadow, and the denizens of that world resented their coming. Great fish shot past them with malevolent eyes, and the vises of giant clams shut swiftly in attempts to trap their moving limbs. Hugo was entranced. He watched the other man as he found the oyster bed and commenced to fill his basket with frantic haste. When his lungs stung and he could bear the agony no longer, he turned and forged toward the upper air. Then they went down again.

Hugo’s blood, designed to take more oxygen from the air, and his greater density fitted him naturally for the work. The pressure did not make him suffer and the few moments granted to the divers beneath the forbidding element stretched to a longer time for him.

On the second day of diving he went alone. His amateur attempt had been surprisingly fruitful. Standing erect in the immense solitude, he searched the hills and valleys. At length, finding a promising cluster of shellfish, he began to examine them one by one, pulling them loose, feeling in their pulpy interior for the precious jewels. He occupied himself determinedly while the *Katrina* was waiting in Apia, and at the end of the stay he had collected more than sixty pearls of great value and two hundred of moderate worth.

It was, he thought, typical of himself. He had decided to make a fortune of some sort after the first bitter rage over his debacle at Webster had abated in his heart. He realized that without wealth his position in the world would be more difficult and more futile than his fates had decreed. Poverty, at least, he was not forced to bear. He could wrest fortune from nature by his might. That he had begun that task by diving for pearls fitted into his scheme. It was such a method as no other man would have considered and its achievement robbed no one while it enriched him.

===Chinese===
在海洋那奇诡的底部，在海底植物庞大的叶片之间，两个人游着。他们是从水面上那片光亮里来的入侵者，像鱼一样水平地穿过幽暗与阴影疾射而行，而那个世界的居民们憎恨他们的到来。大鱼带着恶意的眼睛从他们身边射过，巨蛤的钳口迅速闭合，试图夹住他们游动的肢体。雨果心醉神迷。他看着另一个人找到牡蛎床，开始发疯似的急急忙忙往篮子里装。等到肺部刺痛、再也无法忍受那种苦楚，那人便转身奋力朝上方的空气游去。然后他们再潜下去。

雨果的血液天生能从空气中汲取更多的氧，他更大的身体密度也使他天然适合这份活计。水压不叫他痛苦，在这片令人生畏的水元素之下，准予潜水员们的短短片刻，于他却延展成了更长的时间。

潜水的第二天他独自前往。他这外行的尝试竟出奇地多产。他直立于那浩大的孤寂之中，搜寻着海底的丘陵与沟谷。终于，找到一丛有望的贝群，他便开始一只一只地检视，把它们拽下来，在它们柔软的体内摸索那些珍贵的珠子。「卡特琳娜」号在阿皮亚（Apia）候航期间，他孜孜不倦地埋头于此，到停留结束时，他已经采到了六十多颗价值不菲的珍珠和两百颗价值中等的。

这，他想，正合他的本色。在韦伯斯特那场溃败带来的最初一阵狂怒在他心中平息之后，他就决定要挣下一份家业。他明白，若没有财富，他在这个世界上的处境会比命运所判定的更加艰难、更加徒劳。贫穷，至少，是他不必被迫忍受的。他可以凭自己的伟力向自然夺取财富。他竟以潜水采珠来开始这项事业，正合他的盘算。这是任何旁人都不会想到的方法，而它的成就只使他富足，不掠任何人分毫。

===Original===
When the *Katrina* turned her prow westward again, Hugo worked with his shipmates in a mood that had undergone considerable change. There was no more despair in him, little of the taciturnity that had marked his earliest days at sea, none of the hatred of mankind. He had buried that slowly and carefully in a dull year of work ashore and a month of toil on the heaving deck of the ship. For six months he had kept himself alive in a manner that he could scarcely remember. Driving a truck. Working on a farm. Digging in a road. His mind a bitter blank, his valiant dreams all dead.

One day he had saved a man’s life. The reaction to that was small, but it was definite. The strength that could slay was also a strength that could succour. He had repeated the act some time later. He felt it was a kind of atonement. After that, he sought deliberately to go where he might be of assistance. In the city, again, in September, when a fire engine clanged and whooped through the streets, he followed and carried a woman from a blazing roof as if by a miracle. Then the seaman. He had counted four rescues by that time. Perhaps his self-condemnation for the boy who had fallen on the field at Webster could be stifled eventually. Human life seemed very precious to Hugo then.

He sold his pearls when the ship touched at large cities—a handful here and a dozen there, bargaining carefully and forwarding the profit to a bank in New York. He might have continued that voyage, which was a voyage commenced half in new recognition of his old wish to see and know the world and half in the quest of forgetfulness; but a slip and shifts in the history of the world put an abrupt end to it. When the *Katrina* rounded the Bec d’Aiglon and steamed into the blue and cocoa harbour of Marseilles, Hugo heard that war had been declared by Germany, Austria, France, Russia, England. …

===Chinese===
当「卡特琳娜」号再次调转船头向西，雨果与船友们一起干活时，心境已经大变。他心里不再有绝望，几乎没有初出海时那些日子标志他的沉默寡言，也没有了对人类的憎恨。那一切，他已在岸上沉闷的一年劳作和船上颠簸甲板上的一个月苦干里，缓慢而小心地埋葬了。有六个月，他以一种他自己几乎记不清的方式维持着生存。开卡车。在农场做工。在公路上挖土。头脑是一片痛苦的空白，英勇的梦想全部死灭。

有一天，他救了一个人的命。那件事的反响很小，却很确实。能杀戮的力量，也是能救人的力量。过了一阵，他又重复了这个举动。他觉得那是一种赎罪。此后，他便有意地寻那些自己能出上力的场合去。九月，又回到城里时，一辆救火车铿锵呼啸着穿过街道，他跟了上去，如奇迹一般从烈焰腾腾的屋顶上抱下来一个女人。然后是这个水手。到那时他已经数出了四次救援。也许，他对那个倒在韦伯斯特球场上的男孩的自责，终归是可以压下去的。那时，人的生命在雨果看来十分珍贵。

船停靠大城市时，他就卖出珍珠——这儿一小把，那儿十来颗，仔细地讨价还价，把利润汇往纽约的一家银行。他本可以继续那次航行——那次航行动身时，一半是重新认识到自己见识世界、认识世界的旧愿，一半是为了寻求遗忘；但世界史上的一次失足与几番变迁使它戛然而止。当「卡特琳娜」号绕过贝克达格隆角（Bec d’Aiglon）、驶入马赛那蓝色与可可色的港湾时，雨果听说德国、奥地利、法国、俄国、英国已经相互宣战。……

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===
In a day the last veil of mist that had shrouded his feelings and thoughts, making them numb and sterile, vanished; in a day Hugo found himself—or believed that he had; in a day his life changed and flung itself on the course which, in a measure, destined its fixation. He never forgot that day.

It began in the early morning when the anchor of the freighter thundered into the harbour water. The crew was not given shore leave until noon. Then the mysterious silence of the captain and the change in the ship’s course was explained. Through the third officer he sent a message to the seamen. War had been declared. The seaways were unsafe. The *Katrina* would remain indefinitely at Marseilles. The men could go ashore. They would report on the following day.

The first announcement of the word sent Hugo’s blood racing. War! What war? With whom? Why? Was America in it, or interested in it? He stepped ashore and hurried into the city. The populace was in feverish excitement. Soldiers were everywhere, as if they had sprung up magically like the seed of the dragon. Hugo walked through street after street in the furious heat. He bought a paper and read the French accounts of mobilizations, of a battle impending. He looked everywhere for someone who could tell him. Twice he approached the American Consulate, but it was jammed with frantic and frightened people who were trying only to get away. Hugo’s ambition, growing in him like a fire, was in the opposite direction. War! And he was Hugo Danner!

===Chinese===
一天之内，那层罩着他的情感与思想、使之麻木而荒芜的雾幕消散了；一天之内，雨果找到了自己——或者自信找到了；一天之内，他的生活变了道，一头扑上那条在很大程度上注定其归宿的轨道。他永远忘不了那一天。

这一天始于清晨，货轮的锚轰然砸进港内的海水。直到正午，船员才获准上岸休假。于是，船长那场神秘的沉默与航向的改变都有了答案。他借三副之口向水手们传话：已经宣战了。海上航道不安全。「卡特琳娜」号将无限期滞留马赛。大家可以上岸。次日回船报到。

「战争」这个词头一回灌进耳朵，雨果的血液便奔腾起来。战争！什么战争？跟谁打？为什么？美国卷进去了吗，还是关心这件事？他踏上岸，急匆匆进了城。民众处在狂热的骚动里。士兵到处都是，仿佛神话里播下的龙种，被施了魔法似的从地里冒出来。雨果在灼人的暑热中穿过一条又一条街。他买了份报纸，读那些关于动员、关于一场迫近之战的法文报道。他到处找一个能给他讲讲的人。他两次走近美国领事馆，馆里却挤满了惊慌失措、只想着逃走的人。雨果的雄心像火一样在胸中越烧越旺，方向恰恰相反。战争！而他，是雨果·丹纳！

===Original===
He sat at a café toward the middle of the afternoon. He was so excited by the contagion in his veins that he scarcely thrilled at the first use of his new and half-mastered tongue. The garçon hurried to his table.

“*De la bière*,” Hugo said.

The waiter asked a question which Hugo could not understand, so he repeated his order in the universal language of measurement of a large glass by his hands. The waiter nodded. Hugo took his beer and stared out at the people. They hurried along the sidewalk, brushing the table at which he sat. They called to each other, laughed, cried sometimes, and shook hands over and over. “*La guerre*” was on every tongue. Old men gestured the directions of battles. Young men, a little more serious perhaps, and often very drunk, were rushing into uniform as order followed order for mobilization. And there were girls, thousands of them, walking with the young men.

===Chinese===
下午三点上下，他在一家咖啡馆坐了下来。血脉里传染来的兴奋太盛，以至于头一回启用他新学了一半的那门语言时，他竟顾不上为之激动。侍者（garçon）快步来到他的桌前。

「*De la bière*（来杯啤酒）。」雨果说。

侍者问了一句雨果听不懂的话，他便改用全世界通用的度量语言——用双手比出一只大杯的大小——重申了自己的要求。侍者点了点头。雨果端起啤酒，望着外面的人群。人们在人行道上匆匆来去，擦着他坐的桌子走过。他们彼此呼喊，大笑，有时哭泣，一遍又一遍握手。「战争」（*la guerre*）挂在了每一张嘴上。老人们打着手势比画战线方向。年轻人——也许严肃一些，又常常烂醉——随着一道接一道的动员令争先恐后地穿上军装。还有姑娘们，成千上万的，同年轻男人们并肩走着。

===Original===
Hugo wanted to be in it. He was startled by the impact of that desire. All the ferocity of him, all the unleashed wish to rend and kill, was blazing in his soul. But it was a subtle conflagration, which urged him in terms of duty, in words that spoke of the war as his one perfect opportunity to put himself to a use worthy of his gift. A war. In a war what would hold him, what would be superior to him, who could resist him? He swallowed glass after glass of the brackish beer, quenching a mighty thirst and firing a mightier ambition. He saw himself charging into battle, fighting till his ammunition was gone, till his bayonet broke; and then turning like a Titan and doing monster deeds with bare hands. And teeth.

Bands played and feet marched. His blood rose to a boiling-point. A Frenchman flung himself at Hugo’s table. “And you—why aren’t you a soldier?”

“I will be,” Hugo replied.

===Chinese===
雨果想要投身其中。这愿望来势之猛，连他自己都吃了一惊。他全部的凶性，全部挣脱了缰绳的撕咬杀戮之欲，在他灵魂里熊熊燃烧。可那是一场微妙的烈火，以责任的名义驱策他，用种种说辞把这场战争讲成他唯一完满的机会，好让这份天赋派上配得上它的用场。一场战争。在战争里，还有什么能拦住他，还有什么能胜过他，还有谁能抵挡他？他一杯接一杯灌下微咸的啤酒，浇着强大的渴，点起更强大的野心。他看见自己冲进战场，打到弹药打光，打到刺刀折断；然后像一尊泰坦转过身来，赤手空拳——还有牙齿——建下骇人的功业。

军乐队奏着乐，脚步齐齐行进。他的血升到了沸点。一个法国人扑到雨果的桌前。「那你呢——你怎么还不是个兵？」

「快是了。」雨果回答。

===Original===
“Bravo! We shall revenge ourselves.” The man gulped a glass of wine, slapped Hugo’s shoulder, and was gone. Then a girl talked to Hugo. Then another man.

Hugo dwelt on the politics of the war and its sociology only in the most perfunctory manner. It was time the imperialistic ambitions of the Central Powers were ended. A war was inevitable for that purpose. France and England had been attacked. They were defending themselves. He would assist them. Even the problem of citizenship and the tangle of red tape his enlistment might involve did not impress him. He could see the field of battle and hear the roar of guns, a picture conjured up by his knowledge of the old wars. What a soldier he would be!

While his mind was still leaping and throbbing and his head was whirling, darkness descended. He would give away his life, do his duty and a hundred times more than his duty. Here was the thing that was intended for him, the weapon forged for his hand, the task designed for his undertaking. War. In war he could bring to a full fruition the majesty of his strength. No need to fear it there, no need to be ashamed of it. He felt himself almost the Messiah of war, the man created at the precise instant he was required. His call to serve was sounding in his ears. And the bands played.

The chaos did not diminish at night, but, rather, it increased. He went with milling crowds to a bulletin board. The Germans had commenced to move. They had entered Belgium in violation of treaties long held sacred. Belgium was resisting and Liége was shaking at the devastation of the great howitzers. A terrible crime. Hugo shook with the rage of the crowd. The first outrages and violations, highly magnified, were reported. The blond beast would have to be broken.

===Chinese===
「好样的！我们要报仇。」那人灌下一杯葡萄酒，拍了拍雨果的肩膀，走了。接着一个姑娘跟雨果搭话。接着又是一个男人。

对这场战争的政治与社会因由，雨果只以最敷衍的方式思量过。同盟国（Central Powers）的帝国主义野心，是到头了。为达此目的，一场战争在所难免。法兰西和英格兰遭到了进攻。它们在自卫。他要去助一臂之力。就连投军可能牵扯的国籍问题和官样文书的纠葛，也没能让他皱一皱眉。他看得见战场，听得见炮声轰鸣——一幅由他所知的旧日战争拼出来的图景。他会成为一名怎样的士兵啊！

他的头脑还在跳跃、悸动，还在打转，夜幕已经落下。他愿献出生命，尽他的义务，并且百倍于义务地尽。这便是为他而生的事物，为他这只手锻造的武器，为他这副肩膀设计的任务。战争。在战争里，他能让自身力量的威严结出完满的果实。在那里无须畏惧它，也无须为它羞愧。他几乎觉得自己是战争的弥赛亚，恰在需要他的一瞬被造出来的那个人。服役的召唤在他耳中鸣响。军乐队奏个不停。

入夜，乱局并未消退，反而愈演愈烈。他随着乱转的人流走到一块公告牌前。德军已经开始行动。他们践踏了久奉为神圣的条约，开进了比利时。比利时在抵抗，列日（Liége）在巨炮的蹂躏下震颤。一桩骇人的罪行。雨果随着人群的怒火发抖。最初的暴行与违犯被添油加醋地登了出来。那头金发的野兽必须被打断脊梁。

===Original===
“God damn,” a voice drawled at Hugo’s side. He turned. A tall, lean man stood there, a man who was unquestionably American. Hugo spoke in instant excitement.

“There sure is hell to pay.”

The man turned his head and saw Hugo. He stared at him rather superciliously, at his slightly seedy clothes and his strong, unusual face. “American?”

“Yeah.”

“Let’s have a drink.”

===Chinese===
「该死。」一个声音在雨果身旁拖长了调子骂道。他回过头。一个瘦高个男人站在那里，一个毫无疑问是美国人的人。雨果顿时兴奋地开了口。

「这下可真要天下大乱了。」

那人转过头，看见了雨果。他颇为高傲地上下打量他，打量他那身略显寒酸的衣服，和他那张坚毅而非凡的脸。「美国人？」

「是。」

「喝一杯去。」

===Original===
They separated themselves from the mob and went to a crowded café. The man sat down and Hugo took a chair at his side. “As you put it,” the man said, “there is hell to pay. Let’s drink on the payment.”

Hugo felt in him a certain aloofness, a detachment that checked his desire to throw himself into flamboyant conversation. “My name’s Danner,” he said.

“Mine’s Shayne, Thomas Mathew Shayne. I’m from New York.”

“So am I, in a way. I was on a ship that was stranded here by the war. At loose ends now.”

Shayne nodded. He was not particularly friendly for a person who had met a countryman in a strange city. Hugo did not realize that Shayne had been besieged all day by distant acquaintances and total strangers for assistance in leaving France, or that he expected a request for money from Hugo momentarily. And Shayne did not seem particularly wrought up by the condition of war. They lifted their glasses and drank. Hugo lost a little of his ardour.

===Chinese===
他们从人群里挤出来，走进一家拥挤的咖啡馆。那人坐下，雨果在他旁边拉过一把椅子。「照你的说法，」那人说，「天下是要大乱一场。那就为这笔账干一杯。」

雨果觉出他身上有种冷淡，一种疏离，压住了他想放开来高谈阔论的兴致。「我姓丹纳。」他说。

「我姓谢恩，托马斯·马修·谢恩（Thomas Mathew Shayne）。纽约人。」

「怎么说我也算半个。我在一条船上，让战争困在这儿了。眼下闲着没事。」

谢恩（Shayne）点了点头。对一个在异乡遇见同胞的人来说，他算不得格外热络。雨果不知道，谢恩一整天都被些八竿子打不着的熟人和素不相识的生人缠着，求他帮着离开法国；也不知道他时时刻刻等着雨果开口向他借钱。而且谢恩似乎并不因战局而格外激动。他们举杯喝了。雨果的热切消退了一点。

===Original===
“Nice mess.”

“Time, though. Time the Germans got their answer.”

Shayne’s haughty eyebrows lifted. His wide, thin mouth smiled. “Perhaps. I just came from Germany. Seemed like a nice, peaceful country three weeks ago.”

“Oh.” Hugo wondered if there were many pro-German Americans. His companion answered the thought.

“Not that I don’t believe the Germans are wrong. But war is such—such a damn fool thing.”

===Chinese===
「好一个烂摊子。」

「不过，也到时候了。该给德国人一个回答了。」

谢恩那两道倨傲的眉毛扬了扬。他那宽阔的薄嘴唇笑了。「也许吧。我刚从德国来。三个礼拜前，那儿瞧着还是个安安静静的好国家。」

「哦。」雨果心想，亲德的美国人是不是很多。他的同伴却把他没说出口的心思答了。

「倒不是我以为德国人没错。只是战争这东西——这么一件天大的蠢事。」

===Original===
“Well, it can’t be helped.”

“No, it can’t. We’re all going to go out and get killed, though.”

“We?”

“Sure. America will get in it. That’s part of the game. America is more dangerous to Germany than France—or England, for that matter.”

“That’s a rather cold-blooded viewpoint.”

Shayne nodded. “I’ve been raised on it. Garçon, *l’addition*, s’il vous plaît.” He reached for his pocketbook simultaneously with Hugo. “I’m sorry you’re stranded,” he said, “and if a hundred francs will help, I’ll be glad to let you have it. I can’t do more.”

===Chinese===
「唉，也是没法子的事。」

「是啊，没法子。不过，到头来咱们全都要出去送命。」

「咱们？」

「当然。美国会卷进去的。这是牌局里注定的一环。美国对德国，比法国更危险——论这一条，比英格兰也危险。」

「这眼光未免太冷血了。」

谢恩点点头。「我就是被这套东西喂大的。侍者，结账，劳驾（*Garçon, l’addition, s’il vous plaît*）。」他和雨果同时伸手去摸皮夹。「很遗憾你被困住了，」他说，「要是一百法郎能顶用，我很乐意借你。多的我无能为力。」

===Original===
Hugo’s jaw dropped. He laughed a little. “Good lord, man, I said my ship was stuck. Not me. And these drinks are mine.” He reached into his pocket and withdrew a huge roll of American bills and a packet of French notes.

Shayne hesitated. His calmness was not severely shaken, however. “I’m sorry, old man. You see, all day I’ve been fighting off starving and startled Americans and I thought you were one. I apologize for my mistake.” He looked at Hugo with more interest. “As a matter of fact, I’m a little skittish about patriotism. And about war. Of course, I’m going to be in it. The first entertaining thing that has happened in a dog’s age. But I’m a conscientious objector on principles. I rather thought I’d enlist in the Foreign Legion tomorrow.”

He was an unfamiliar type to Hugo. He represented the American who had been educated at home and abroad, who had acquired a wide horizon for his views, who was bored with the routine of his existence. His clothes were elegant and impeccable. His face was very nearly inscrutable. Although he was only a few years older than Hugo, he made the latter feel youthful.

They had a brace of drinks, two more and two more. All about them was bedlam, as if the emotions of man had suddenly been let loose to sweep him off his feet. Grief, joy, rage, lust, fear were all obviously there in almost equal proportions.

===Chinese===
雨果的下巴垂了下来。他有点想笑。「老天爷，老兄，我说的是我那条船搁住了。不是我。再说这几杯酒该我请。」他探进口袋，掏出厚厚一大卷美钞，还有一叠法国纸币。

谢恩迟疑了一下。不过他的镇静并未怎么被动摇。「对不起，老伙计。你瞧，我一整天都在应付那些饿着肚子、惊魂未定的美国人，还以为你是其中一个。我为看走眼赔个不是。」他带着多几分兴味地打量雨果。「说实在的，我对爱国主义这玩意儿有点发怵。对战争也一样。当然，我是要入场的。这可是老长时间以来头一桩有趣的事。不过嘛，我在原则上是个良心拒服兵役者。我倒盘算着，明天就去报名参加外籍军团（Foreign Legion）。」

对雨果来说，这是一种陌生的类型。他代表那类在国内外都受过教育、眼界放得很宽、又对自身生活的刻板日子厌倦透了的美国人。他的衣着考究得无可挑剔。他的脸近乎深不可测。他不过比雨果大几岁，却使雨果自觉年少。

他们又对饮了一对，再来两杯，又两杯。四下里一片喧腾鼎沸，仿佛人的种种情感骤然放开，要把人整个卷走。悲、喜、怒、欲、惧，分明都在，分量几乎旗鼓相当。

===Original===
Shayne extended his hand. “They have something to fight for, at least. Something besides money and glory. A grudge. I wonder what it is that makes me want to get in? I do.”

“So do I.”

Shayne shook his head. “I wouldn’t if I were you. Still, you will probably be compelled to in a while.” He looked at his watch. “Do you care to take dinner with me? I had an engagement with an aunt who is on the verge of apoplexy because two of the Boston Shaynes are in Munich. It scarcely seems appropriate at the moment. I detest her, anyway. What do you say?”

“I’d like to have dinner with you.”

===Chinese===
谢恩伸出手。「他们至少有可为之而战的东西。金钱和荣耀之外的东西。一笔旧仇。我不知道是什么让我也想挤进去？可我确实想。」

「我也是。」

谢恩摇了摇头。「换了我就不去。不过，过不了多久，你多半也会身不由己。」他看了看表。「愿意同我共进晚餐吗？我本来约了一位姑妈——因为波士顿谢恩家有两个人困在慕尼黑，她都快气中风了。眼下这个时候怎么也不合适。反正我也厌恶她。你说呢？」

「我很乐意同你吃饭。」

===Original===
They walked down the Cannebière. At a restaurant on the east side near the foot of the thoroughfare they found a table in the corner. A pair of waiters hastened to take their order. The place was riotous with voices and the musical sounds of dining. On a special table was a great demijohn of 1870 cognac, which was fast being drained by the guests. Shayne consulted with his companion and then ordered in fluent French. The meal that was brought approached a perfection of service and a superiority of cooking that Hugo had never experienced. And always the babble, the blare of bands, the swelling and fading persistence of the stringed orchestra, the stream of purple Châteauneuf du Pape and its flinty taste, the glitter of the lights and the bright colors on the mosaics that represented the principal cities of Europe. It was a splendid meal.

“I’m afraid I’ll have to ask your name again,” Shayne said.

“Danner. Hugo Danner.”

“Good God! Not the football player?”

===Chinese===
他们沿着卡纳比埃街（Cannebière）走去。在这条大道近末端的东侧一家餐馆里，他们找到了角落的一张桌子。两名侍者赶上来听点菜。满堂人声鼎沸，杯盘交错作响。一张专设的桌上立着一坛 1870 年干邑的大肚坛，正被客人们飞快地喝空。谢恩同雨果商量了几句，随即用流利的法语点了菜。端上来的那一餐，侍应之完美、烹调之上乘，几乎到了雨果从未见识过的地步。还有那不绝于耳的嘈嘈人语、乐队的高奏、弦乐队绵延起伏挥之不去的乐声；那紫红的教皇新堡（Châteauneuf du Pape）酒流不断，带着燧石般的口味；那灯火的辉映、镶嵌画上明丽的色彩——那些画拼出欧洲的各座名城。真是一顿盛馔。

「恐怕我得再问一遍你的名字。」谢恩说。

「丹纳。雨果·丹纳。」

「天哪！不是那个打橄榄球的吧？」

===Original===
“I did play football—some time ago.”

“I saw you against Cornell—when was it?—two years ago. You were magnificent. How does it happen that—”

“That I’m here?” Hugo looked directly into Shayne’s eyes.

“Well—I have no intention of prying into your affairs.”

“Then I’ll tell you. Why not?” Hugo drank his wine. “I killed a man—in the game—and quit. Beat it.”

===Chinese===
「橄榄球是打过——些年头了。」

「我看过你们对康奈尔（Cornell）那场——哪一年来着？——两年前。你打得太漂亮了。可你怎么会——」

「怎么会在这儿？」雨果直直望着谢恩的眼睛。

「呃——我无意打探你的私事。」

「那我就告诉你。有何不可？」雨果饮了口酒。「我在比赛里打死了一个人——然后就退了。一走了之。」

===Original===
Shayne accepted the statement calmly. “That’s tough. I can understand your desire to get out from under. Things like that are bad when you’re young.”

“What else could I have done?”

“Nothing. What are you going to do? Rather, what were you going to do?”

“I don’t know,” Hugo answered slowly. “What do you do? What do people generally do?” He felt the question was drunken, but Shayne accepted it at its face value.

===Chinese===
谢恩平静地接受了这句话。「那够受的。我明白你想从这个阴影底下钻出去的心情。这种事，人年轻的时候最不好过。」

「不然我还能怎么样？」

「没法子。你打算做什么？或者这么说——你本来打算做什么？」

「不知道。」雨果慢吞吞地答。「你是做什么的？人们一般都做什么？」他自觉这问题问得醉意熏人，谢恩却照字面正经接了下去。

===Original===
“I’m one of those people who have too much money to be able to do anything I really care about, most of the time. The family keeps me in sight and control. But I’m going to cut away tomorrow.”

“In the Foreign Legion? I’ll go with you.”

“Splendid!” They shook hands across the table.

===Chinese===
「我属于那种人——钱多得大多数时候反倒干不成一件自己真心想干的事。家里人盯着我、管着我。可我明天就要脱身走人了。」

「去外籍军团？我跟你一起去。」

「妙极了！」他们隔着桌子握了手。

===Original===
Three hours later found them at another café. They had been walking part of the time in the throngs on the street. For a while they had stood outside a newspaper office watching the bulletins. They were quite drunk.

“Old man,” Shayne said, “I’m mighty glad I found you.”

“Me, too, old egg. Where do we go next?”

“I don’t know. What’s your favorite vice? We can locate it in Marseilles.”

===Chinese===
三个钟头后，他们已坐在另一家咖啡馆里。中间有一段时候，他们在街上的人潮里走；还有一阵，他们站在一家报馆门外看公告。他们都醉得相当可以了。

「老伙计，」谢恩说，「找见你，我真是高兴透了。」

「我也是，老伙计。下一站去哪儿？」

「不知道。你顶喜欢哪宗罪？在马赛，咱们都能寻见。」

===Original===
Hugo frowned. “Well, vice is so limited in its scope.”

His companion chuckled. “Isn’t it? I’ve always said vice was narrow. The next time I see Aunt Emma I’m going to say: ‘Emma, vice is becoming too narrow in its scope.’ She’ll be furious and it will bring her to an early demise and I’ll inherit a lot more money, and that will be the real tragedy. She’s a useless old fool, Aunt Emma. Never did a valuable thing in her life. Goes in for charity—just like we go in for golf and whatnot. Oh, well, to hell with Aunt Emma.”

Hugo banged his glass on the table. “Garçon! *Encore deux whiskey à l’eau* and to hell with Aunt Emma.”

===Chinese===
雨果皱了皱眉。「唉，罪过这东西，门类实在太窄。」

他的同伴呵呵一笑。「可不是么？我一向就说，罪过的路子窄。下回见着艾玛姑妈（Aunt Emma），我要对她说：『艾玛，罪过的门类变得太窄啦。』她准会大发雷霆，就此早早归天，我再继承一大笔钱——那才是真正的悲剧。她是个没用的老糊涂，我们那位艾玛姑妈。一辈子没干成过一件有价值的事。搞慈善——就跟你我打高尔夫之类一个搞法。唉，算了，让艾玛姑妈见鬼去吧。」

雨果把杯子往桌上一顿。「侍者！再来两杯掺水威士忌（*encore deux whiskey à l’eau*）——也叫艾玛姑妈见鬼去。」

===Original===
“Like to play roulette?”

“Like to try.”

They climbed into a taxi. Shayne gave an address and they were driven to another quarter of the town. In a room packed with people in evening clothes they played for an hour. Several people spoke to Shayne and he introduced Hugo to them. Shayne won and Hugo lost. They went out into the night. The streets were quieter in that part of town. Two girls accosted them.

“That gives me an idea,” Shayne said. “Let’s find a phone. Maybe we can get Marcelle and Claudine.”

===Chinese===
「想玩轮盘赌么？」

「想试试。」

他们钻进一辆出租车。谢恩报了个地址，车把他们载到城里另一片街区。在一间挤满晚礼服人群的屋子里，他们玩了一个钟头。有几个人过来同谢恩说话，他把雨果介绍给他们。谢恩赢了，雨果输了。他们出到夜色里。那一带的街道安静些。两个姑娘凑上来搭讪。

「这倒提醒了我，」谢恩说，「找部电话。兴许能把玛塞尔（Marcelle）和克洛蒂娜（Claudine）叫来。」

===Original===
Marcelle and Claudine met them at the door of the old house. Their arms were laden with champagne bottles. The interior of the dwelling belied its cold, grey, ancient stones. Hugo did not remember much of what followed that evening. Short, unrelated fragments stuck in his mind—Shayne chasing the white form of Marcelle up and down the stairs; himself in a huge bathtub washing a back in front of him, his surprise when he saw daylight through the wooden shutters of the house.

Someone was shaking him. “Come on, soldier. The leave’s up.”

He opened his eyes and collected his thoughts. He grinned at Shayne. “All right. But if I had to defend myself right now—I’d fail against a good strong mouse.”

“We’ll fix that. Hey! Marcelle! Got any Fernet-Branca?”

The girl came with two large glasses of the pick-me-up. Hugo swallowed the bitter brown fluid and shuddered. Claudine awoke. “*Chéri*!” she sighed, and kissed him.

===Chinese===
玛塞尔和克洛蒂娜在那座老宅的门口迎他们。两人怀里抱满了香槟酒瓶。宅子的内部与它那灰暗冰冷的老石墙全然两样。那晚随后的事，雨果记不太清了。只余下些互不相连的零碎片段黏在脑中——谢恩追着玛塞尔雪白的身影在楼梯上跑上跑下；他自己泡在一个大浴缸里，给身前的一具脊背擦洗；还有他从宅子的木百叶窗里望见天光时那一惊。

有人在摇他。「起来，大兵。假满啦。」

他睁开眼，把思绪收拢。他冲谢恩咧嘴一笑。「行。可要是这会儿非得自卫不可——来一只健壮的大耗子，我就得败下阵来。」

「这好办。喂！玛塞尔！有菲奈特-布兰卡（Fernet-Branca）吗？」

姑娘端来两大杯那种解酒提神的苦饮。雨果把那褐色的苦汁灌下去，打了个寒噤。克洛蒂娜醒了。「亲爱的（*Chéri*）！」她叹了一声，吻了他。

===Original===
They sat on the edge of the bed. “Boy!” Hugo said. “What a binge!”

“You like eet?” Claudine murmured.

He took her hand. “Loved it, darling. And now we’re going to war.”

“Ah!” she said, and, at the door: “Bonne chance!”

===Chinese===
两人坐在床沿。「好家伙！」雨果说。「这一场痛饮！」

「你喜欢么？」克洛蒂娜喃喃地问。

他握住她的手。「喜欢极了，宝贝。现在，我们要去打仗了。」

「啊！」她说；到了门口，又道：「祝好运（*Bonne chance*）！」

===Original===
Shayne left Hugo, after agreeing on a time and place for their meeting in the afternoon. The hours passed slowly. Hugo took another drink, and then, exerting his judgment and will, he refrained from taking more. At noon he partook of a light meal. He thought, or imagined, that the ecstasy of the day before was showing some signs of decline. It occurred to him that the people might be very sober and quiet before the war was a thing to be written into the history of France.

The sun was shining. He found a place in the shade where he could avoid it. He ordered a glass of beer, tasted it, and forgot to finish it. The elation of his first hours had passed. But the thing within him that had caused it was by no means dead. As he sat there, his muscles tensed with the picturization of what was soon to be. He saw the grim shadows of the enemy. He felt the hot splash of blood. For one suspended second he was ashamed of himself, and then he stamped out that shame as being something very much akin to cowardice.

He wondered why Shayne was joining the Legion and what sort of person he was underneath his rather haughty exterior. A man of character, evidently, and one who was weary of the world to which he had been privileged. Hugo’s reverie veered to his mother and father. He tried to imagine what they would think of his enlistment, of him in the war; and even what they thought of him from the scant and scattered information he had supplied. He was sure that he would justify himself. He felt purged and free and noble. His strength was a thing of wreck and ruin, given to the world at a time when wreck and ruin were needed to set it right. It was odd that such a product should emerge from the dusty brain of a college professor in a Bible-ridden town.

===Chinese===
谢恩与雨果约好下午碰头的时间地点后，便分手了。时间过得很慢。雨果又喝了一杯，随后便动用判断与意志，不再多饮。正午他用了点便饭。他觉得——或者说想象——前一天的狂喜已显出几分退潮的迹象。他忽然想到，等这场战争被写进法兰西的史册之前，人们也许会变得非常清醒、非常安静。

阳光正烈。他寻了处背阴地避开它。他要了一杯啤酒，抿了一口，就忘了喝完。最初几个钟头的亢奋已经过去。但激起那亢奋的他心里的那件东西，绝没有死。他坐在那里，脑海中浮现出不久将临的图景，肌肉便绷紧了。他看见敌人狰狞的暗影。他感到热血的飞溅。有那么悬着的一瞬，他为自己羞愧，随即把那羞愧一脚踩灭，视之为与怯懦相去无几的东西。

他琢磨谢恩为什么要加入军团，琢磨他在那副颇为倨傲的外表底下究竟是怎样的人。显然是个有品格的人，而且是对自己有幸身处的这个世界厌倦了的人。雨果的遐思转向了父亲和母亲。他试着想象他们会怎样看待他的投军、看他走上战场；甚至想象，凭他零星散落寄回去的那点消息，他们又是怎么看他的。他确信自己终将自证其行。他觉得自己被涤净了，自由了，高贵了。他的力量是一件捣毁与倾覆之物，恰恰降生于一个需要捣毁与倾覆来拨乱反正的世间。说来也怪，这样一件产物，竟出自《圣经》当道的小城里一位大学教师那积灰的脑袋。

===Original===
Hugo had not possessed a religion for a long time. Now, wondering on another tangent if the war might not bring about his end, he thought about it. He realized that he would hate himself for murmuring a prayer or asking protection. He was gamer than the Cross-obsessed weaklings who were not wise enough to look life in the face and not brave enough to draw the true conclusions from what they saw. True conclusions? He meditated. What did it matter—agnosticism, atheism, pantheism—anything but the savage and anthropomorphic twaddle that had been doled out since the Israelites singled out Jehovah from among their many gods. He would not commit himself. He would go back with his death to the place where he had been before he was born and feel no more regret than he had in that oblivious past. Meanwhile he would fight! He moved restively and waited for Shayne with growing impatience.

Until that chaotic and gorgeous hour he had lived for nothing, proved nothing, accomplished nothing. Society was no better in any way because he had lived. He excepted the lives he had saved, the few favors he had done. That was nothing in proportion to his powers. He was his own measure, and by his own efforts would he satisfy himself. War! He flexed his arms. War. His black eyes burned with a formidable light.

Then Shayne came. Walking with long strides. A ghostly smile on his lips. A darkness in his usually pale-blue eyes. Hugo liked him. They said a few words and walked toward the recruiting-tent. A poilu in steely blue looked at them and saw that they were good. He proffered papers. They signed. That night they marched for the first time. A week later they were sweating and swearing over the French manual of arms. Hugo had offered his services to the commanding officer at the camp and been summarily denied an audience or a chance to exhibit his abilities. When they reached the lines—that would be time enough. Well, he could wait until those lines were reached.

===Chinese===
雨果已经很久没有宗教了。此刻，他沿着另一条思路想到，这场战争说不定会带来他的末日，于是把宗教又想了一遍。他明白，若是自己低声祷告、祈求庇佑，他会恨自己。他比那些满脑子十字架的懦夫更有胆气——那些人既不够聪明，不能直面人生；又不够勇敢，不敢从所见之中得出真正的结论。真正的结论？他沉思着。有什么要紧——不可知论也罢，无神论也罢，泛神论也罢——什么都行，只要不是自打以色列人从他们那许多神里单单挑出耶和华以来，就一直分发下来的那套野蛮而拟人的呓语。他不把自己托付给任何一宗。他将随死亡回到他出生之前所在的地方，像在那段浑然无觉的往昔里一样，不怀一丝憾恨。而在那之前，他要战斗！他烦躁地挪动身子，越来越不耐烦地等谢恩。

在那个混乱而瑰丽的时辰之前，他活得毫无意义，什么也没证明，什么也没成就。世间不因他活过而在任何一方面变得更好。他救下的那几条命、帮过的那几个忙，姑且除外。比起他的力量，那算不得什么。他自己便是自己的尺度，他要以自己的努力令自己餍足。战争！他绷了绷双臂。战争。他黑色的眼睛里烧着慑人的光。

随后谢恩来了。迈着大步。唇上一点幽灵似的浅笑。他平日淡蓝的眼睛里罩着一层暗色。雨果喜欢他。两人说了几句，便朝征兵棚走去。一个穿钢蓝色军装的法国兵（poilu）打量他们，看出他们是块料。他递上文书。他们签了字。当夜，他们头一回列队行军。一星期后，他们正对着法军的兵器操典挥汗咒骂。雨果曾向营地的指挥官自荐效劳，却被干脆利落地回绝了——既不让面见，也不给机会一显身手。等开到了前线——那时再说不迟。好吧，他等得到开赴前线的日子。

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
Just as the eastern horizon became light with something more steady than the flare of the guns, the command came. Hugo bit his lip till it bled darkly. He would show them﻿—now. They might command him to wait﻿—he could restrain himself no longer. The men had been standing there tense and calm, their needle-like bayonets pointing straight up. “En avant!”

His heart gave a tremendous surge. It made his hands falter as he reached for the ladder rung. “Here we go, Hugo.”

“Luck, Tom.”

===Chinese===
东方的地平线刚刚透出光来——那光比炮火的闪耀更稳定——命令就下来了。雨果咬着嘴唇，一直咬到渗出血来，血色发暗。他要让他们看看——就是现在。他们尽可以命令他等待——可他再也无法克制自己了。士兵们一直紧张而镇静地站在那里，针一样的刺刀笔直朝天。“En avant！”（前进！）

他的心猛地一阵翻涌。伸手去抓梯级的时候，手都颤了一下。“出发了，雨果。”

“祝你好运，汤姆。”

===Original===
He saw Shayne go over. He followed slowly. He looked at no man’s land. They had come up in the night and he had never seen it. The scene of holocaust resembled nothing more than the municipal ash dump at Indian Creek. It startled him. The grey earth in irregular heaps, the litter of metal and equipment. He realized that he was walking forward with the other men. The ground under his feet was mushy, like ashes. Then he saw part of a human body. It changed his thoughts.

The man on Hugo’s right emitted a noise like a squeak and jumped up in the air. He had been hit. Out of the corner of his eye Hugo saw him fall, get up quickly, and fall again very slowly. His foot kicked after he lay down. The rumbling in the sky grew louder and blotted out all other sound.

They walked on and on. It was like some eternal journey through the dun, vacant realms of Hades. Not much light, one single sound, and ghostly companions who faced always forward. The air in front of him was suddenly dyed orange and he felt the concussion of a shell. His ears rang. He was still walking. He walked what he thought was a number of miles.

===Chinese===
他看见谢恩翻了出去。他缓缓跟上。他望着无人地带。他们是夜里上来的，还从没见过它的真容。这片大屠杀后的场面，像极了印第安溪镇上公家的灰渣场。这让他吃了一惊。灰色的土地堆得东一堆西一垛，金属和装备的残骸散落满地。他意识到自己正随着其他人向前走。脚下的地软塌塌的，像灰烬。随后他看见了人体的一部分。这改变了他的念头。

雨果右边那个人发出一声尖细的吱叫，直挺挺跳到半空。他中弹了。雨果用眼角的余光看见他倒下，很快爬起来，又非常缓慢地再倒下去。倒地之后，他的脚还踢了一下。天上的轰隆声越来越响，盖过了一切别的声响。

他们走啊，走啊。像一场永恒的跋涉，穿过冥府那暗褐、空寂的疆域。没有多少光，只有一种声音，还有始终面朝前方的幽灵般的同伴。他面前的空气忽然被染成橙色，他感到了一发炮弹的震荡。耳朵里嗡嗡直响。他还在走。他走了大约有几英里。

===Original===
His great strength seemed to have left him, and in its place was a complete enervation. With a deliberate effort he tested himself, kicking his foot into the earth. It sank out of sight. He squared his shoulders. A man came near him, yelling something. It was Shayne. Hugo shook his head. Then he heard the voice, a feeble shrill note. “Soon be there.”

“Yeah?”

“Over that hill.”

Shayne turned away and became part of the ghost escort of Hugo and his peculiarly lucid thoughts. He believed that he was more conscious of himself and things then than ever before in his life. But he did not notice one-tenth of the expression and action about himself. The top of the rise was near. He saw an officer silhouetted against it for an instant. The officer moved down the other side. He could see over the rise, then.

===Chinese===
他那一身神力仿佛离他而去，取而代之的是彻头彻尾的疲惫。他有意使了一把劲试了试自己，一脚朝地里踹去。脚没进土里，不见了。他挺了挺肩膀。一个人凑近他，喊着什么。是谢恩。雨果摇了摇头。随后他听见了那声音，微弱而尖细：“快到了。”

“是吗？”

“翻过那道坡就是。”

谢恩转过身去，融进那支幽灵的护卫队里，护卫着雨果和他那异样澄明的思绪。他相信，此刻他对自己、对世上万物的知觉，比一生中任何时候都更清醒。可周遭的神情与举动，他连十分之一都没有留意到。坡顶近了。他看见一名军官的剪影在那里现了一瞬。那军官朝另一面坡下去了。于是他也望见了坡那边的景象。

===Original===
Across the grey ashes was a long hole. In front of it a maze of wire. In it﻿—mushrooms. German helmets. Hugo gaped at them. All that training, all that restraint, had been expended for this. They were small and without meaning. He felt a sharp sting above his collar bone. He looked there. A row of little holes had appeared in his shirt.

“Good God,” he whispered, “a machine gun.”

But there was no blood. He sat down. He presumed, as a casualty, he was justified in sitting down. He opened his shirt by ripping it down. On his dark-tanned skin there were four red marks. The bullets had not penetrated him. Too tough! He stared numbly at the walking men. They had passed him. The magnitude of his realization held him fixed for a full minute. He was invulnerable! He should have known it﻿—otherwise he would have torn himself apart by his own strength. Suddenly he roared and leaped to his feet. He snatched his rifle, cracking the stock in his fervour. He vaulted toward the helmets in the trench.

===Chinese===
灰色灰烬的那一边是一道长壕。壕前是迷宫般的铁丝网。壕里——是一朵朵蘑菇。德军的钢盔。雨果目瞪口呆地望着它们。那些训练，那些克制，全都为这个消耗殆尽。它们那么小，毫无意义。他感到锁骨上方一阵刺痛。他朝那里看去。衬衫上出现了一排小孔。

“老天哪，”他低声说，“是机枪。”

可是没有血。他坐了下来。他推想，作为一名伤员，他坐下来是名正言顺的。他一把扯开衬衫。晒得黝黑的皮肤上有四个红点。子弹没有打进他的身体。皮糙肉厚得过了头！他木然瞪视着那些还在前进的人。他们已经越过了他。这一领悟的分量使他呆坐了整整一分钟。他是刀枪不入的！他早该想到这一点——不然他早就被自己的力气撕成两半了。他忽然大吼一声，一跃而起。他抓起步枪，狂热之下把枪托都攥裂了。他朝战壕里那些钢盔纵身扑去。

===Original===
He dropped from the parapet and was confronted by a long knife on a gun. His lips parted, his eyes shut to slits, he drew back his own weapon. There was an instant’s pause as they faced each other﻿—two men, both knowing that in a few seconds one would be dead. Then Hugo, out of his scarlet fury, had one glimpse of his antagonist’s face and person. The glimpse was but a flash. It was finished in quick motions. He was a little man﻿—a foot shorter than Hugo. His eyes looked out from under his helmet with a sort of pathetic earnestness. And he was worried, horribly worried, standing there with his rifle lifted and trying to remember the precise technique of what would follow even while he fought back the realization that it was hopeless. In that split second Hugo felt a human, amazing urge to tell him that it was all right, and that he ought to hold his bayonet a little higher and come forward a bit faster. The image faded back to an enemy. Hugo acted mechanically from the rituals of drill. His own knife flashed. He saw the man’s clothes part smoothly from his bowels, where the point had been inserted, up to the gray-green collar. The seam reddened, gushed blood, and a length of intestine slipped out of it. The man’s eyes looked at Hugo. He shook his head twice. The look became faraway. He fell forward.

Hugo stepped over him. He was trembling and nauseated. A more formidable man approached warily. The bellow of battle returned to Hugo’s ears. He pushed back the threatening rifle easily and caught the neck in one hand, crushing it to a wet, sticky handful. So he walked through the trench, a machine that killed quickly and remorselessly﻿—a black warrior from a distant realm of the universe where the gods had bred another kind of man.

He came upon Shayne and found him engaged. Hugo stuck his opponent in the back. No thought of fair play, no object but to kill﻿—it did not matter how. Dead Legionnaires and dead Germans mingled blood underfoot. The trench was like the floor of an abattoir. Someone gave him a drink. The men who remained went on across the ash dump to a second trench.

===Chinese===
他从胸墙上一跃而下，迎面撞上一把装在枪上的长刀。他双唇分开，眼睛眯成两道细缝，端回了自己的兵器。两人对面峙立，顿了一瞬——两个男人，都明白几秒钟之内必有一个死去。随后，雨果从他那血红的狂怒里，瞥见了对手的脸和整个人。这一瞥不过一闪，在一连串快捷的动作里便告完结。他是个小个子男人——比雨果矮了一英尺。他从钢盔下望出来的那双眼睛里，带着一种可怜的恳切。他还忧心忡忡，忧心得要命，端着上了刺刀的步枪站在那里，一面努力回想接下来动作的准确要领，一面又拼命压下“这毫无希望”的意识。就在那一瞬间，雨果感到一种出于人性的、令人惊异的冲动，想告诉他：没关系的，你的刺刀该端得再高一点，上步该再快一点。那影像又退回去，还原成一个敌人。雨果按照操典的规程机械地动了手。他自己的刀光一闪。他看见那人的衣裳从刀尖插入的小腹处平滑地豁开，一直裂到灰绿色的衣领。裂缝红了，涌出血来，一截肠子从里面滑了出来。那人的一双眼睛望着雨果。他摇了两下头。目光变得遥远了。他向前扑倒。

雨果从他身上跨了过去。他浑身发颤，直想呕吐。一个更强悍的汉子小心翼翼地逼了上来。战斗的吼啸回到雨果耳中。他轻轻推开那杆威逼过来的步枪，一手掐住对方的脖颈，把它攥成湿乎乎、黏糊糊的一把。他就这样穿行在战壕里，一台杀人又快又狠的机器——一名来自宇宙遥远疆域的黑武士，在那里的众神造出了另一种人。

他撞见谢恩，见他正跟人缠斗。雨果一刀捅进那对手的后背。谈不上什么公平较量，除杀人之外别无目的——怎么杀都无所谓。死去的外籍军团士兵和死去的德国人，鲜血在脚下混作一处。战壕活像屠宰场的地面。有人递给他一口酒喝。活着的人继续越过那片灰渣场，向第二道战壕推进。

===Original===
It was night. The men, almost too tired to see or move, were trying to barricade themselves against the ceaseless shell fire of the enemy. They filled bags with gory mud and lifted them on the crumbling walls. At dawn the Germans would return to do what they had done. The darkness reverberated and quivered. Hugo worked like a Trojan. His efforts had made a wide and deep hole in which machine guns were being placed. Shayne fell at his feet. Hugo lifted him up. The captain nodded. “Give him a drink.”

Someone brought liquor, and Hugo poured it between Shayne’s teeth. “Huh!” Shayne said.

“Come on, boy.”

“How did you like it, Danner?”

Hugo did not answer. Shayne went on, “I didn’t either﻿—much. This is no gentleman’s war. Jesus! I saw a thing or two this morning. A guy walking with all his﻿—”

===Chinese===
入夜了。这些人累得几乎看不见、动不了，还在设法筑起工事，抵御敌人不停的炮火。他们往麻袋里装满血污的泥浆，把袋子垒上不断坍塌的胸墙。天一亮德国人就会卷土重来，故技重施。黑暗在轰响中震颤。雨果干起活来不要命。他一双手挖出了一个又宽又深的坑，机枪正在里面架设。谢恩倒在他脚边。雨果把他扶起来。上尉点了点头。“给他喝口酒。”

有人取来酒，雨果把它灌进谢恩的齿缝里。“嚯！”谢恩说。

“撑住，伙计。”

“你觉得这滋味如何，丹纳？”

雨果没有回答。谢恩接着说：“我也不觉得——不怎么样。这可不是绅士们的战争。天哪！今天早上我见了一两样事儿。有个家伙一边走，身上的零碎全都——”

===Original===
“Never mind. Take another drink.”

“Got anything to eat?”

“No.”

“Oh, well, we can fight on empty bellies. The Germans will empty them for us anyhow.”

===Chinese===
“别说了。再喝一口。”

“有吃的吗？”

“没有。”

“哦，也好，咱们空着肚子也能打。反正德国人迟早会替咱们把肚子掏空的。”

===Original===
“The hell they will.”

“I’m pretty nearly all in.”

“So’s everyone.”

===Chinese===
“他们敢。”

“我快累趴下了。”

“谁不是呢。”

===Original===
They put Hugo on watch because he still seemed fresh. Those men who were not compelled to stay awake fell into the dirt and slept immediately. Toward dawn Hugo heard sounds in no man’s land. He leaped over the parapet. In three jumps he found himself among the enemy. They were creeping forward. Hugo leaped back. “*Ils viennent!*”

Men who slept like death were kicked conscious. They rose and fired into the night. The surprise of the attack was destroyed. The enemy came on, engaging in the darkness with the exhausted Legionnaires. Twice Hugo went among them when inundation threatened and, using his rifle barrel as a club, laid waste on every hand. He walked through them striking and shattering. And twice he saved his salient from extermination. Day came sullenly. It began to rain. The men stood silently among their dead.

Hugo lit a cigarette. His eyes moved up and down the shambles. At intervals of two yards a man, his helmet trickling rain, his clothes filthy, his face inscrutable. Shayne was there on sagging knees. Hugo could not understand why he had not been killed.

===Chinese===
他们让雨果站岗，因为他看上去仍然精力充沛。那些不必强撑着清醒的人一头栽进泥土里，立刻睡死过去。将近黎明，雨果听见无人地带里有动静。他跃过胸墙，三个纵跳就落进了敌人中间。他们正匍匐着向前摸。雨果又跳了回来。“*Ils viennent!*”（他们来了！）

睡得如同死人的人们被踢醒。他们爬起来，朝黑夜里开火。偷袭的突然性被粉碎了。敌人继续涌上前来，在黑暗中与精疲力竭的外籍军团士兵接战。两次，当防线眼看要被淹没时，雨果冲进敌群，把枪管当棍子使，横扫四方。他在人群里穿行，又打又砸。两次，他都把自己的突出部从覆灭的边缘救了回来。天色阴沉地亮了。下起雨来。士兵们默默地站在他们的死者中间。

雨果点起一支烟。他的目光在这片屠场里上下扫过。每隔两码就有一个人，钢盔上淌着雨水，军装污秽不堪，脸上深不可测。谢恩也在那儿，靠两条发软的膝盖撑着。雨果弄不明白，他怎么还没有死。

===Original===
Hugo was learning about war. He thought then that the task which he had set for himself was not altogether to his liking. There should be other and more important things for him to do. He did not like to slaughter individuals. The day passed like a cycle in hell. No change in the personnel except that made by an occasional death. No food. No water. They seemed to be exiled by their countrymen in a pool of fire and famine and destruction. At dusk Hugo spoke to the captain.

“We cannot last another night without water, food,” he said.

“We shall die here, then.”

“I should like, sir, to volunteer to go back and bring food.”
