===Chinese===
雨果渐渐领教了战争。他那时觉得，他给自己定下的任务并不完全合他的心意。他该去做别的、更重要的事情。他不喜欢这样屠杀一个一个的人。这一天像地狱里的一整轮轮回。人员没有任何变动，除了偶尔一次死亡带来的更替。没有吃的。没有水。他们仿佛被同胞放逐在一池烈火、饥馑与毁灭之中。黄昏时分，雨果对上尉开了口。

“没有水、没有粮，我们撑不过下一夜了。”他说。

“那我们就死在这儿。”

“长官，我想自告奋勇，回后方去运吃的。”

===Original===
“We need ammunition more.”

“Ammunition, then.”

“One man could not bring enough to assist﻿—much.”

“I can.”

===Chinese===
“我们更需要弹药。”

“那就运弹药。”

“一个人能背来的，帮不上——多大的忙。”

“我背得来。”

===Original===
“You are valuable here. With your club and your charmed life, you have already saved this remnant of good soldiers.”

“I will return in less than an hour.”

“Good luck, then.”

Where there had been a man, there was nothing. The captain blinked his eyes and stared at the place. He swore softly in French and plunged into his dugout at the sound of ripping in the sky.

===Chinese===
“你在这里很宝贵。凭你那根大棒和你那刀枪不入的命，你已经救下了这批好兵的残部。”

“我一小时之内就回来。”

“那就祝你好运。”

方才还站着一个人的地方，转眼空空如也。上尉眨了眨眼，直瞪着那块地方。他用法语轻轻咒骂了一声，听到天空撕裂般的呼啸，便一头扎进了掩蔽部。

===Original===
A half hour passed. The steady, nerve-racking bombardment continued at an unvaried pace. Then there was a heavy thud like that of a shell landing and not exploding. The captain looked. A great bundle, tied together by ropes, had descended into the trench. A man emerged from beneath it. The captain passed his hand over his eyes. Here was ammunition for the rifles and the machine guns in plenty. Here was food. Here were four huge tins of water, one of them leaking where a shell fragment had pierced it. Here was a crate of canned meat and a sack of onions and a stack of bread loaves. Hugo broke the ropes. His chest rose and fell rapidly. He was sweating. The bundle he had carried weighed more than a ton﻿—and he had been running very swiftly.

The captain looked again. A case of cognac. Hugo was carrying things into the dugout. “Where?” the captain asked.

Hugo smiled and named a town thirty kilometres behind the lines. A town where citizens and soldiers together were even then in frenzied discussion over the giant who had fallen upon their stores and supplies and taken them, running off like a locomotive, in a hail of bullets that did no harm to him.

===Chinese===
半小时过去了。那场不紧不慢、折磨神经的炮击仍在以不变的节奏持续。忽然传来一声沉重的闷响，像一发炮弹落地却没有炸。上尉看去。一大捆用绳索捆扎的东西，已经落进了战壕。一个人从它底下钻了出来。上尉抬手抹了抹眼睛。这里是为步枪和机枪备下的充足弹药。这里是食物。这里是四大桶水，其中一桶被弹片穿透的地方正往外渗漏。这里有一箱罐头肉、一袋洋葱和一摞面包。雨果扯断绳索。他的胸膛剧烈起伏。他出了一身汗。他扛来的这捆东西重逾一吨——而他是飞快地一路跑回来的。

上尉又看了一眼。还有一箱干邑。雨果正把东西搬进掩蔽部。“哪儿弄来的？”上尉问。

雨果笑了笑，说了一个后方三十公里外的市镇的名字。在那个镇上，市民和士兵此刻正激动万分地议论着一个巨人：他扑到他们的物资仓库和补给站上，把东西掳了就走，像一列火车头似的扬长而去，枪林弹雨浇了他一身，却伤不了他一根毫毛。

===Original===
“And how?” the captain asked.

“I am strong.”

The captain shrugged and turned his head away. His men were eating the food, and drinking water mixed with brandy, and stuffing their pouches with ammunition. The machine gunners were laughing. They would not be forced to spare the precious belts when the Germans came in the morning. Hugo sat among them, dining his tremendous appetite.

===Chinese===
“可你是怎么弄来的？”上尉问。

“我力气大。”

上尉耸耸肩，把头扭向一边。他的士兵们正在吃东西，喝掺了白兰地的水，往弹药袋里塞子弹。机枪手们在笑。明早德国人来的时候，他们不必再省着用那些宝贵的弹带了。雨果坐在他们中间，美美地饱餐了一顿，满足他那惊人的胃口。

===Original===
Three days went by. Every day, twice, five times, they were attacked. But no offense seemed capable of driving that demoniac cluster of men from their position. A demon, so the enemy whispered, came out and fought for them. On the third day the enemy retreated along four kilometres of front, and the French moved up to reclaim many, many acres of their beloved soil. The Legionnaires were relieved and another episode was added to their valiant history.

Hugo slept for twenty hours in the wooden barracks. After that he was wakened by the captain’s orderly and summoned to his quarters. The captain smiled when he saluted. “My friend,” he said, “I wish to thank you in behalf of my country for your labor. I have recommended you for the Croix de Guerre.”

Hugo took his outstretched hand. “I am pleased that I have helped.”

===Chinese===
三天过去了。每一天，他们都要挨上两次、五次的进攻。但似乎没有任何攻势能把那群魔鬼般的人赶出阵地。据敌人私下相传，有一个魔鬼出来替他们打仗。第三天，敌军沿四公里的战线退却了，法国人向前推进，收复了一亩又一亩他们挚爱的国土。外籍军团士兵得到了换防，他们英勇的战史上又添了一笔。

雨果在木营房里睡了二十个钟头。之后他被上尉的勤务兵叫醒，召到上尉的住处。他敬礼时，上尉微笑了。“我的朋友，”他说，“我谨代表我的国家，为你的辛劳向你致谢。我已为你申报了战争十字勋章（Croix de Guerre）。”

雨果握住他伸出的手。“能帮上忙，我很高兴。”

===Original===
“And now,” the captain continued, “you will tell me how you executed that so unusual coup.”

Hugo hesitated. It was the opportunity he had sought, the chance that might lead to a special commission whereby he could wreak the vengeance of his muscles on the enemy. But he was careful, because he did not feel secure in trusting the captain with too much of his secret. Even in a war it was too terrible. They would mistrust him, or they would attempt to send him to their biologists. And he wanted to accomplish his mission under their permission and with their cooperation. It would be more valuable then and of greater magnitude. So he smiled and said: “Have you ever heard of Colorado?”

“No, I have not heard. It is a place?”

“A place in America. A place that has scarcely been explored. I was born there. And all the men of Colorado are born as I was born and are like me. We are very strong. We are great fighters. We cannot be wounded except by the largest shells. I took that package by force and I carried it to you on my back, running swiftly.”

===Chinese===
“现在嘛，”上尉接着说，“你要告诉我，你是怎么完成那桩如此不寻常的壮举的。”

雨果犹豫了。这正是他一直寻求的机会，一条可能通向特别委任的路，凭那道委任，他就能向敌人施展他这一身肌肉的复仇。但他很谨慎，因为他觉得把太多秘密托付给上尉并不保险。哪怕在战争里，这秘密也太惊人了。他们会猜疑他，或者会设法把他送去交给他们的生物学家。而他希望他的使命是在他们的许可和配合之下去完成。那样它就更有价值，也更具规模。于是他笑着说：“你听说过科罗拉多（Colorado）吗？”

“没听说过。是个地方？”

“美洲的一个地方。一个几乎没人勘探过的地方。我就出生在那里。所有科罗拉多的男人都和我一样降生，都和我一个样。我们非常强壮。我们是了不起的战士。除了最大的炮弹，什么也伤不了我们。那捆东西是我用武力抢来的，背在背上，一路飞跑着送到你们这儿。”

===Original===
The captain appeared politely interested. He thumbed a dispatch. He stared at Hugo. “If that is the truth, you shall show me.”

“It is the truth﻿—and I shall show you.”

Hugo looked around. Finally he walked over to the sentry at the flap of the tent and took his rifle. The man squealed in protest. Hugo lifted him off the floor by the collar, shook him, and set him down.

The man shouted in dismay and then was silent at a word from the captain. Hugo weighed the gun in his hands while they watched and then slowly bent the barrel double. Next he tore it from its stock. Then he grasped the parallel steel ends and broke them apart with a swift wrench. The captain half rose, his eyes bulged, he knocked over his inkwell. His hand tugged at his moustache and waved spasmodically.

===Chinese===
上尉露出一副礼貌的兴致。他用拇指捻着一纸电文。他盯着雨果。“如果这是真的，你得证明给我看。”

“是真的——我会证明给你看。”

雨果环顾四周。最后他走到帐篷门帘边哨兵跟前，拿走了他的步枪。那人抗议着尖叫起来。雨果揪住他的衣领，把他整个人提离地面，抖了抖，又把他放下。

那人惊慌地大叫，上尉一句话，他就安静了。雨果在众人的注视下掂了掂手里的枪，然后缓缓把枪管折成对折。接着把它从枪托上扯了下来。然后他攥住两截并排的钢端，一个利落的拧转，把它们掰断了。上尉半站起来，眼睛都鼓了出来，碰翻了墨水瓶。他一只手扯着小胡子，痉挛似的挥动。

===Original===
“You see?” Hugo said.

The captain went to staff meeting that afternoon very thoughtful. He understood the difficulty of exhibiting his soldier’s prowess under circumstances that would assure the proper commission. He even considered remaining silent about Hugo. With such a man in his company it would soon be illustrious along the whole broad front. But the chance came. When the meeting was finished and the officers relaxed over their wine, a colonel brought up the subject of the merits of various breeds of men as soldiers.

“I think,” he said, “that the Prussians are undoubtedly our most dangerous foe. On our own side we have﻿—”

===Chinese===
“看见了吧？”雨果说。

那天下午，上尉若有所思地去参加参谋会议。他明白，要在一个能确保恰当委任的场合展示他这名士兵的身手，实在困难。他甚至考虑过对雨果的事保持缄默。有这样一个人物在他的连队里，要不了多久，整条宽阔战线都会传遍他的威名。可机会来了。会议结束、军官们端着酒放松下来的时候，一位上校起了话头，谈论各个人种作为军人的优劣。

“我认为，”他说，“普鲁士人无疑是我们最危险的敌人。而在我们这一边，我们有——”

===Original===
“Begging the colonel’s pardon,” the captain said, “there is a species of fighter unknown, or almost unknown, in this part of the world, who excels by far all others.”

“And who may they be?” the colonel asked stiffly.

“Have you ever heard of the Colorados?”

“No,” the colonel said.

===Chinese===
“恕我冒昧，上校，”上尉说，“世上有一种战士，在这一带无人知晓，或者说几乎无人知晓，却远远胜过所有其他人。”

“他们又是何方神圣？”上校生硬地问。

“您听说过科罗拉多人吗？”

“没听说过。”上校说。

===Original===
Another officer meditated. “They are redskins, American Indians, are they not?”

The captain shrugged. “I do not know. I know only that they are superior to all other soldiers.”

“And in what way?”

The captain’s eyes flickered. “I have one Colorado in my troops. I will tell you what he did in five days near the town of Barsine.” The officers listened. When the captain finished, the colonel patted his shoulder. “That is a very amusing fabrication. Very. With a thousand such men, the war would be ended in a week. Captain Crouan, I fear you have been overgenerous in pouring the wine.”

===Chinese===
另一位军官沉吟道：“他们是红种人，美洲的印第安人，对吧？”

上尉耸耸肩。“我不知道。我只知道他们比所有别的士兵都强。”

“强在哪里？”

上尉的目光闪了闪。“我的队伍里就有一个科罗拉多人。我来告诉你们，他在巴尔西纳（Barsine）镇附近五天里干下的事。”军官们听着。上尉讲完，上校拍了拍他的肩膀。“这故事编得非常有趣。非常有趣。要有一千个这样的人，这场战争一个星期就能打完。克鲁昂（Crouan）上尉，恐怕你斟酒的时候手太阔绰了些。”

===Original===
The captain rose, saluted. “With your permission, I shall cause my Colorado to be brought and you shall see.”

The other men laughed. “Bring him, by all means.”

The captain dispatched an orderly. A few minutes later, Hugo was announced at headquarters. The captain introduced him. “Here, messieurs, is a Colorado. What will you have him do?”

===Chinese===
上尉站起身，敬了个礼。“请允许我，我这就叫人把我的科罗拉多人带来，让诸位亲眼看看。”

其他人都笑了。“务必带来。”

上尉派去一名勤务兵。几分钟后，雨果被通报到了指挥部。上尉介绍他。“瞧，诸位先生（messieurs），这就是一个科罗拉多人。你们要他做什么？”

===Original===
The colonel, who had expected the soldier to be both embarrassed and made ridiculous, was impressed by Hugo’s calm demeanour. “You are strong?” he said with a faint irony.

“Exceedingly.”

“He is not humble, at least, gentlemen.” Laughter. The colonel fixed Hugo with his eye. “Then, my good fellow, if you are so strong, if you can run so swiftly and carry such burdens, bring us one of our beautiful seventy-fives from the artillery.”

===Chinese===
上校本指望这士兵会窘态毕露、出尽洋相，此刻却被雨果沉静的气度镇住了。“你很强壮？”他带着一丝讥诮说。

“强得很。”

“至少他不谦虚，诸位先生。”一阵哄笑。上校用眼睛盯住雨果。“那么，我的好伙计，你要真这么强壮，真能跑得这么快、扛得动这么重的东西，就去给我们从炮兵那儿搬一门我们漂亮的七五炮（seventy-five，法军75毫米野战炮）来。”

===Original===
“With your written order, if you please.”

The colonel started, wrote the order laughingly, and gave it to Hugo. He left the room.

“It is a good joke,” the colonel said. “But I fear it is harsh on the private.”

The captain shrugged. Wine was poured. In a few minutes they heard heavy footsteps outside the tent. “He is here!” the captain cried. The officers rushed forward. Hugo stood outside the tent with the cannon they had requested lifted over his head in one hand. With that same hand clasped on the breach, he set it down. The colonel paled and gulped. “Name of the mother of God! He has brought it.”

===Chinese===
“请给我一道手令。”

上校一怔，笑着写了手令，交给雨果。他离开了房间。

“这玩笑不错，”上校说，“只怕对这个列兵太苛刻了。”

上尉耸耸肩。酒斟上了。几分钟过后，他们听见帐篷外响起沉重的脚步声。“他来了！”上尉喊道。军官们一拥而上。雨果站在帐篷外，一只手把他们要的那门炮举过头顶。又用同一只手扣住炮尾，把它放了下来。上校脸色发白，倒吸了一口气。“以圣母之名！他真搬来了。”

===Original===
Hugo nodded. “It was as nothing, my colonel. Now I will show you what we men from Colorado can do. Watch.”

They eyed him. There was a grating sound beneath his feet. Those who were quickest of vision saw his body catapult through the air high over their heads. It landed, bounced prodigiously, vanished.

Captain Crouan coughed and swallowed. He faced his superiors, trying to seem nonchalant. “That, gentlemen, is the sort of thing the Colorados do﻿—for sport.”

===Chinese===
雨果点点头。“这不算什么，我的上校。现在我要让你们看看我们科罗拉多男人的本事。看好。”

他们盯着他。他脚下响起一阵刮擦之声。眼力最快的人看见他的身体弹射到空中，从他们头顶上高高地掠过。落了地，惊人地一弹，便消失了。

克鲁昂上尉咳嗽一声，咽了口唾沫。他面对上级，竭力显得若无其事。“那种事，诸位先生，科罗拉多人——是拿来当消遣的。”

===Original===
The colonel recovered first. “It is not human. Gentlemen, we have been in the presence of the devil himself.”

“Or the Good Lord.”

The captain shook his head. “He is a man, I tell you. In Colorado all the men are like that. He told me so himself. When he first enlisted, he came to me and asked for a special commission to go to Berlin and smash the Reich﻿—to bring back the Kaiser himself. I thought he was mad. I made him peel potatoes. He did not say any more foolish things. He was a good soldier. Then the battle came and I saw him, not believing I saw him, standing on the parapet and wielding his rifle like the lightning, killing I do not know how many men. Hundreds certainly, perhaps thousands. Ah, it is as I said, the Colorados are the finest soldiers on earth. They are more than men.”

===Chinese===
上校最先回过神来。“这不是人类。诸位，我们方才是撞见了魔鬼本尊。”

“要么就是天主他老人家。”

上尉摇了摇头。“他是人，我告诉你们。在科罗拉多，所有的男人都是那副样子。是他亲口对我说的。他刚入伍那会儿，来找我，请求一道特别委任，要去柏林砸烂德意志帝国——把德皇本人捉回来。我以为他疯了。我罚他去削土豆。他就再没说过那样的蠢话。他是个好兵。后来仗打起来，我看见他——不敢相信自己的眼睛——站在胸墙上，把步枪舞得像闪电一样，杀掉的人不知有多少。几百是肯定的，也许上千。啊，正像我说的，科罗拉多人是天下最优秀的士兵。他们不只是凡人。”

===Original===
“He comes!”

Hugo burst from the sky, moving like a hawk. He came from the direction of the lines, many miles away. There was a bundle slung across his shoulder. There were holes in his uniform. He landed heavily among the officers and set down his burden. It was a German. He dropped to the ground.

“Water for him,” Hugo panted. “He has fainted. I snatched him from his outpost in a trench.”

===Chinese===
“他来了！”

雨果从天而降，动作像一只鹰。他从好几英里外的战线方向来。肩上横挎着一个布包。军装上尽是窟窿。他沉甸甸地落在军官们中间，放下了他扛的东西。那是个德国人。他瘫倒在地上。

“给他水，”雨果喘着气说，“他昏过去了。我把他从战壕前哨里一把掳来的。”

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===
At Blaisencourt it was spring again. The war was nearly a year old. Blaisencourt was now a street of houses’ ghosts, of rubble and dirt, populated by soldiers. A little new grass sprouted peevishly here and there; an occasional house retained enough of its original shape to harbour an industry. Captain Crouan, his arm in a sling, was looking over a heap of debris with the aid of field glasses.

“I see him,” he said, pointing to a place on the boiling field where an apparent lump of soil had detached itself.

“He rises! He goes on! He takes one of his mighty leaps! Ah, God, if I only had a company of such men!”

His aide, squatted near by, muttered something under his breath. The captain spoke again. “He is very near their infernal little gun now. He has taken his rope. Ahaaaa! He spins it in the air. It falls. They are astonished. They rise up in the trench. Quick, Phèdre! Give me a rifle.” The rifle barked sharply four, five times. Its bullet found a mark. Then another. “Ahaaa! Two of them! And M. Danner now has his rope on that pig’s breath. It comes up. See! He has taken it under his arm! They are shooting their machine guns. He drops into a shell hole. He has been hit, but he is laughing at them. He leaps. Look out, Phèdre!”

===Chinese===
布莱桑库尔（Blaisencourt）又到了春天。战争打了快一年。布莱桑库尔如今成了一条鬼屋之街，满是瓦砾与尘土，居民换成了士兵。少许新草在各处怄气似的钻出头来；偶尔还有一座房子保留着足够的原形，容得下一桩营生。克鲁昂上尉（Captain Crouan）吊着一条胳膊，正借望远镜察看一堆废墟。

「我看见他了。」他说，手指着那片沸腾的原野上的一处——那里，一团看似土块的东西自己脱开了地面。

「他起来了！他前进了！他来了一个雷霆般的大跳！啊，天主，我要是有整整一连这样的人才好！」

他的副官蹲在近旁，压着嗓子咕哝了一句什么。上尉又开口了。「这会儿他离他们那门可恶的小炮很近了。他拿出了绳子。啊哈！他把绳子在空中抡圆了。绳子落下去了。他们大吃一惊。他们从战壕里站起来了。快，菲德尔（Phèdre）！给我一支步枪。」步枪尖利地连叫四五声。一枪命中了目标。接着又是一枪。「啊哈！两个！丹纳先生这会儿把绳子套上那口猪猡炮了。炮起来了。瞧！他把它夹在腋下了！他们正朝他打机枪。他跳进一个弹坑。他中了弹，可他还在笑话他们呢。他跳起来了。当心，菲德尔！」

===Original===
Hugo landed behind the debris with a small German trench mortar in his arms. He set it on the floor. The captain opened his mouth, and Hugo waved to him to be silent. Deliberately, Hugo looked over the rickety parapet of loose stones. He elevated the muzzle of the gun and drew back the lanyard. The captain, grinning, watched through his glasses. The gun roared.

Its shell exploded presently on the brow of the enemy trench, tossing up a column of smoke and earth. “I should have brought some ammunition with me,” Hugo said.

Captain Crouan stared at the little gun. “Pig,” he said. “Son of a pig! Five of my men are in your little belly! Bah!” He kicked it.

===Chinese===
雨果抱着一门小型德军堑壕臼炮，降落在废墟后面。他把炮搁在地上。上尉张口要嚷，雨果挥手叫他别作声。雨果不慌不忙，从那道乱石垒成、摇摇欲坠的胸墙后探头张望。他抬高了炮口，把拉发绳拉了回来。上尉咧着嘴，从望远镜里观瞧。炮轰然一响。

炮弹不多时在敌壕的壕眉上炸开，掀起一根烟和泥土的柱子。「我该随身捎几发炮弹来的。」雨果说。

克鲁昂上尉瞪着那门小炮。「猪猡，」他说，「猪崽子！我五个人都进了你这小肚皮！呸！」他踹了它一脚。

---
===Original===
Summer in Aix-au-Dixvaches. A tall Englishman addressing Captain Crouan. His voice was irritated by the heat. “Is it true that you French have an Indian scout here who can bash in those Minenwerfers?”

“*Pardon, mon colonel, mais je ne comprends pas l’anglais.*”

He began again in bad French. Captain Crouan smiled. “Ah? You are troubled there on your sector? You wish to borrow our astonishing soldier? It will be a pleasure, I assure you.”

Hot calm night. The sky pinpricked with stars, the air redolent with the mushy flavour of dead meat. So strong it left a taste in the mouth. So strong that food and water tasted like faintly chlorinated putrescence. Hugo, his blue uniform darker with perspiration, tramped through the blackness to a dugout. Fifteen minutes in candlelight with a man who spoke English in an odd manner.

===Chinese===
艾克斯-欧-迪克斯瓦什（Aix-au-Dixvaches）的夏天。一个高个子英国人正对克鲁昂上尉说话。他的嗓音被暑气熏得烦躁。「听说你们法国人这里有个印第安侦察兵，能把那些德军臼炮（Minenwerfer）砸个稀烂，可是真的？」

「*Pardon, mon colonel, mais je ne comprends pas l’anglais.*（对不起，上校，我听不懂英语。）」

他又用蹩脚的法语从头说了一遍。克鲁昂上尉微笑了。「哦？你们自己那段防区吃紧了？你们想借用我们这位惊人的士兵？那将是一桩乐事，我向你保证。」

炎热而平静的夜。天空被繁星扎出细孔，空气里弥漫着腐肉的软烂气味。那气味浓得口中都留下了味道。浓得连食物和水都尝着像掺了淡淡氯气的腐臭。雨果的蓝军装被汗水渍得颜色更深，他一步一步踏过黑暗，走向一处掩蔽部。在烛光下同一个英语说得古里古怪的人待了一刻钟。

===Original===
“They’ve been raisin’ bloody hell with us from a point about there.” The tap of a pencil. “We’ve got little enough confidence in you, God knows﻿—”

“Thank you.”

“Don’t be huffy. We’re obliged to your captain for the loan of you. But we’ve lost too many trying to take the place ourselves not to be fed up with it. I suppose you’ll want a raiding party?”

“No, thanks.”

===Chinese===
「他们差不多从那儿的一个点上，一直他妈的打得我们鸡飞狗跳。」铅笔在图上点了一下。「老天爷知道，我们对你是没多少信心的——」

「多谢。」

「别气鼓鼓的。我们多亏你上尉把你借了来。可我们自己想法子拿下那地方，折损的人手太多，不腻烦才怪。我看你总得要一支突袭队吧？」

「不用，谢谢。」

===Original===
“But, cripes, you can’t make it there alone.”

“I can do it.” Hugo smiled. “And you’ve lost so many of your own men﻿—”

“Very well.”

===Chinese===
「可是，老天在上，你一个人可到不了那儿。」

「我能行。」雨果笑了。「再说，你们自己的人已经搭进去那么多了——」

「那好。」

---
===Original===
Otto Meyer pushed his helmet back on his sandy-haired head and gasped in the feverish air. A noncommissioned officer passing behind him shoved the helmet over his eyes with a muttered word of caution. Otto shrugged. Half a dozen men lounged near by. Beside and above them were the muzzles of four squat guns and the irregular silhouette of a heap of ammunition. Two of the men rolled onto their backs and panted. “I wish,” one said in a soft voice, “that I was back in the Hofbrau at Munich with a tall stein of beer, with that fat Fräulein that kissed me in the Potsdam station last September sitting at my side and the orchestra playing﻿—”

Otto flung a clod of dank earth at the speaker. There were chuckles from the shadows that sucked in and exhaled the rancid air. Outside the pit in which they lay, there was a gentle thud.

Otto scrambled into a sitting posture. “What is that?”

“Nothing. Even these damned English aren’t low enough to fight us in this weather.”

===Chinese===
奥托·迈尔（Otto Meyer）把钢盔往亚麻色头发的脑后一推，在燥热的空气里直喘。一名军士打他身后经过，咕哝着一句告诫，把钢盔扣回他眼睛上。奥托耸耸肩。六个人瘫坐在近旁。他们身边和头顶上，是四门矮墩墩的炮的炮口，和一堆弹药垒出的参差轮廓。有两个人翻过身仰面躺着，直喘气。「我真想，」一个人柔声说，「回到慕尼黑的霍夫布劳啤酒馆（Hofbrau），捧一大杯啤酒，身边坐着去年九月在波茨坦车站亲过我的那个胖乎乎的小姐，乐队奏着乐——」

奥托朝说话的人扔过去一块湿土坷垃。阴影里响起几声闷笑，那些影子正把腐臭的空气吸进又呼出。在他们趴伏的土坑外面，传来一下轻轻的闷响。

奥托慌忙撑坐起来。「那是什么？」

「没什么。就连这帮该死的英国人，也不至于下作到在这种天气跟我们开打。」

===Original===
“You can never tell. At night, in the first battle of﻿—listen!”

The thud was repeated, much closer. It was an ominous sound, like the drop of a sack of earth from a great height. Otto picked up a gun. He was a man who perspired freely, and now, in that single minute, his face trickled. He pointed the gun into the air and pulled the trigger. It kicked back and jarred his arm. In the glaring light that followed, six men peered through the spiderweb of the wire. They saw nothing.

“You see?”

===Chinese===
「这可说不准。夜里，在头一仗的时候——听！」

闷响又来了，近得多。那是一种凶兆般的声音，像一大袋土从极高的地方坠地。奥托抓起一支枪。他是个动辄出汗的人，而此刻，就在那一分钟里，汗水在他脸上淌成了细流。他把枪口指向空中，扣下了扳机。枪托后坐，震得他胳膊发麻。在随之而来的炫光里，六个人眯着眼透过铁丝网的蛛网张望。他们什么也没看见。

「瞧见了吧？」

===Original===
Their eyes smarted with the light and dark, so swiftly exchanged. Came a thud in their midst. A great thud that spattered the dirt in all directions. “Something has fallen.” “A shell!” “It’s a dud!”

The men rose and tried to run. Otto had regained his vision and saw the object that had descended. A package of yellow sticks tied to a great mass of iron﻿—wired to it. Instead of running, he grasped it. His strength was not enough to lift it. Then, for one short eternity, he saw a sizzling spark move toward the sticks. He clutched at it. “Help! The guns must be saved. A bomb!” He knew his arms surrounded death. “I cannot﻿—”

His feeble voice was blown to the four winds at that instant. A terrible explosion burst from him, shattering the escaping men, blasting the howitzers into fragments, enlarging the pit to enormous dimensions. Both fronts clattered with machine-gun fire. Flares lit the terrain. Hugo, running as if with seven-league boots, was thrown on his face by the concussion.

===Chinese===
光与暗瞬息交割，他们的眼睛刺痛。一声闷响落在他们中间。一声巨大的闷响，泥土朝四面八方飞溅。「有东西掉下来了。」「是炮弹！」「是颗哑弹！」

众人爬起来想跑。奥托已经恢复了视力，看清了落下来的物件。一捆黄色药条，缚在一大块铁上——用铁丝缠着。他非但没有跑，反而一把抓住了它。他的力气不够把它提起来。随后，在一个短促的永恒里，他看见一粒咝咝作响的火星向药条窜去。他伸手去抓。「快来人！炮一定得保住。是炸弹！」他知道自己的双臂正环抱着死亡。「我举不起来——」

就在这一瞬，他微弱的喊声被炸散到四面八方。一场骇人的爆炸从他身上迸发，把四散奔逃的人击得粉碎，把榴弹炮炸成齑粉，把土坑扩成一片巨穴。两条战线的机枪都嗒嗒地响成一片。照明弹照亮了地面。雨果正跑得如同脚踩七里靴，也被爆炸的气浪掀翻，摔了个嘴啃泥。

---
===Original===
Winter. Mud. A light fall of snow that was split into festers by the guns before it could anneal the ancient sores. Hugo shivered and stared into no man’s land, whence a groan had issued for twenty hours, audible occasionally over the tumult of the artillery. He saw German eyes turned mutely on the same heap of rags that moved pitifully over the snow, leaving a red wake, dragging a bloody thing behind. It rose and fell, moving parallel to the two trenches. Many machine-gun bullets had either missed it or increased its crimson torment. Hugo went out and killed the heap of rags, with a revolver that cracked until the groans stopped in a low moan. Breaths on both sides were bated. The rags had been gray-green. A shout of low, rumbling praise came from the silent enemy trenches. Hugo looked over there for a moment and smiled. He looked down at the thing and vomited. The guns began again.

===Chinese===
冬天。泥泞。一层薄雪落下，还没来得及把那些陈年的疮口焐合，就被炮火豁成一道道新的溃烂。雨果打着寒噤，凝望无人地带——二十个钟头以来，那里一直传出呻吟，偶尔能穿透炮火的喧嚣，让人听见。他看见德国人的眼睛也默然望着同一堆破布：它在雪地上可怜巴巴地挪动，拖出一线红痕，身后拽着一件血糊淋剌的东西。它一起一伏，与两条战壕平行地移动。许多机枪子弹要么落了空，要么加重了它猩红的苦刑。雨果走出去，用一把左轮打死了那堆破布，枪声一响再响，直到那呻吟止息成一声低泣。两边的呼吸都屏住了。那堆破布原是灰绿色的。沉寂的敌壕里响起一阵低沉隆隆的喝彩。雨果朝那边望了片刻，笑了。他低头看看那东西，呕吐起来。炮声又开始了。

---
===Original===
Another winter. Time had become stagnant. All about it was a pool of mud and suppuration, and shot through it was the sound of guns and the scent of women, the taste of wine and the touch of cold flesh. Somewhere, he could not remember distinctly where, Hugo had a clean uniform, a portfolio of papers, a jewel-case of medals. He was a great man﻿—a man feared. The Colorado in the Foreign Legion. Men would talk about what they had seen him accomplish all through the next fifty years﻿—at watering places in the Sahara, at the crackling fires of country-house parties in Shropshire, on the shores of the South Seas, on the moon, maybe. Old men, at the last, would clear the phlegm from their skinny throats and begin: “When I was a-fightin’ with the Legion in my youngest days, there was a fellow in our company that came from some place in wild America that I disrecollect.” And younger, more sanguine men would listen and shake their heads and wish that there was a war for them to fight.

Hugo was not satisfied with that. Still, he could see no decent exit and contrive no better use for himself. He clung frantically to the ideals he had taken with him and to the splendid purpose with which he had emblazoned his mad lust to enlist. Marseilles and the sentiment it had inspired seemed very far away. He thought about it as he walked toward the front, his head bent into the gale and his helmet pitched to protect his eyes from the sting of the rain.

That night he slept with Shayne, a lieutenant now, twice wounded, thrice decorated, and, like Hugo, thinner than he had been, older, with eyes grown bleak, and seldom vehement. He resembled his lean Yankee ancestors after their exhausting campaigns of the wilderness, alive and sentient only through a sheer stubbornness that brooked neither element nor disaster. Only at rare moments did the slight strain of his French blood lift him from that grim posture. Such a moment was afforded by the arrival of Hugo.

===Chinese===
又一个冬天。时间凝滞了。它四周是一泓泥浆与溃脓的池沼，其中穿行着炮声与女人的气息、酒的滋味与冷肉的触感。在某个地方——他已记不真切是哪里——雨果存着一身干净的军装、一夹文件、一匣勋章。他成了个大人物——一个令人惧怕的人物。外籍军团里的科罗拉多。往后五十年里，人们会一直谈论他们亲眼见他做下的种种——在撒哈拉的疗养绿洲，在什罗普郡乡间宅邸噼啪作响的炉火边，在南太平洋诸岛的海岸上，说不定还在月球上。到头来，老头子们会清一清精瘦喉管里的黏痰，开口说道：「我年纪最轻那阵子跟着军团打仗，我们连里有个家伙，打美利坚哪个荒僻地方来的，我想不起来了。」而更年轻、血气更旺的人会听着，摇着头，巴不得自己也有一场仗好打。

雨果并不以此为满足。然而他仍旧看不到一条体面的退路，也给自己想不出更好的用场。他发疯似的抱住随身带去的那几个理想，抱住他当初给自己那股从军的狂热所镀上的堂皇宗旨。马赛，和马赛在他胸中激起的情怀，似乎已非常遥远。他朝前线走去，一路想着这些，脑袋顶着狂风，钢盔压低，护住眼睛不受冷雨的蜇刺。

那天夜里他和谢恩（Shayne）睡在一处。谢恩如今是中尉了，两度负伤，三度授勋，而且同雨果一样，比从前瘦了、老了，眼神变得冷峻，很少有激昂的时候。他像他那些精瘦的扬基祖先，在旷野中拖垮人的战役打完之后，单凭一股既不服风雨也不服灾祸的执拗，才活着，才有知觉。只有难得的时刻，他那一丝法兰西血统才会把他从那副冷硬姿态里提振起来。雨果的到来，恰好给了这样一个时刻。

===Original===
“Great God, Hugo! We haven’t seen you in a dog’s age.” Other soldiers smiled and brought rusty cigarettes into the dugout where they sat and smoked.

Hugo held out his hand. “Been busy. Glad to see you.”

“Yes. I know how busy you’ve been. Up and down the lines we hear about you. Le Colorado. Damn funny war. You’d think you weren’t human, or anywhere near human, to hear these birds. Wish you’d tell me how you get away with it. Hasn’t one nicked you yet?”

===Chinese===
「老天爷，雨果！我们可有年头没见着你了。」别的士兵微笑起来，把烟锈斑斑的香烟拿进掩蔽部，大家坐下抽烟。

雨果伸出手。「一直忙着。很高兴见到你。」

「是啊，我知道你有多忙。这条战线上上下下，我们都听着你的消息。科罗拉多。这场仗真他妈邪门。听这帮家伙讲起来，你会当他不是人，或者压根儿跟人不沾边。真希望你能告诉我你是怎么应付过来的。就没有一颗子弹蹭破过你？」

===Original===
“Not yet.”

“God damn. Got me here”﻿—he tapped his shoulder﻿—“and here”﻿—his thigh.

“That’s tough. I guess the sort of work I do isn’t calculated to be as risky as yours,” Hugo said.

“Huh! That you can tell to Sweeny.” The Frenchmen were still sitting politely, listening to a dialogue they could not understand. Hugo and Shayne eyed each other in silence. A long, penetrating silence. At length the latter said soberly: “Still as enthusiastic as you were that night in Marseilles?”

===Chinese===
「还没有。」

「他妈的。我这里就挨了一下」——他拍拍肩膀——「还有这里」——拍拍大腿。

「真够呛。我寻思我干的这类活儿，天生就犯不上像你那样玩命。」雨果说。

「哼！这种话你去讲给斯威尼听吧。」法国兵们仍旧客客气气地坐着，听一段他们听不懂的对话。雨果和谢恩默默对视。长久的、洞穿人心的沉默。末了，后者正色道：「还像马赛那晚一样满腔热忱吗？」

===Original===
“Are you?”

“I didn’t have much conception of what war would be then.”

“Neither did I,” Hugo responded. “And I’m not very enthusiastic any more.”

“Oh, well﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你呢？」

「那时候，我对战争会是什么样子还没多少概念。」

「我也一样，」雨果答道，「而且我已经不大热忱了。」

「哦，这个嘛——」

===Original===
“Exactly.”

“Heard from your family?”

“Sure.”

“Well﻿—”

===Chinese===
「正是。」

「家里有信吗？」

「当然。」

「嗯——」

===Original===
They relapsed into silence again. By and by they ate a meal of cold food, supplemented by rank, steaming coffee. Then they slept. Before dawn Hugo woke feeling like a man in the mouth of a volcano that had commenced to erupt. The universe was shaking. The walls of the dugout were molting chunks of earth. The scream and burst of shells were constant. He heard Shayne’s voice above the din, issuing orders in French. Their batteries were to be phoned. A protective counter-fire. A barrage in readiness in case of attack, which seemed imminent. Larger shells drowned the voice. Hugo rose and stood beside Shayne.

“Coming over?”

“Coming over.”

A shapeless face spoke in the gloom. The voice panted. “We must get out of here, my lieutenant. They are smashing in the dugout.” A methodical scramble to the orifice. Hell was rampaging in the trench. The shells fell everywhere. Shayne shook his head. It was neither light nor dark. The incessant blinding fire did not make things visible except for fragments of time and in fantastic perspectives. Things belched and boomed and smashed the earth and whistled and howled. It was impossible to see how life could exist in that cauldron, and yet men stood calmly all along the line. A few of them, here and there, were obliterated.

===Chinese===
两人重又归于沉默。过了一阵，他们吃了一顿冷饭，佐以劣质而滚烫的咖啡。然后睡了。拂晓前雨果醒来，觉得自己像个置身于火山口中的人，而火山已经开始喷发。宇宙在震荡。掩蔽部的四壁簌簌剥落大块的泥土。炮弹的呼啸与爆裂连绵不绝。他听见谢恩的声音压过喧嚣，在用法语下命令。要给他们的炮兵通电话。实施掩护性的反击炮火。备妥一道拦阻射击，以防看来迫在眉睫的进攻。更大的炮弹把人声淹没。雨果起身，站到谢恩身边。

「他们要上来了？」

「要上来了。」

昏暗中一张辨不出形状的脸开了口。那嗓音喘着。「我们得出去，中尉先生。掩蔽部要被砸塌了。」众人有条不紊地朝洞口攀去。战壕里，地狱正在横冲直撞。炮弹四处落下。谢恩摇了摇头。天色不亮也不黑。那不停的炫目炮火照不亮万物，只照出时间的碎片与光怪陆离的透视。万物在喷吐、在轰鸣、在砸碎大地、在呼啸、在嘶吼。看不出生命如何能在这口大锅里存续，然而整条战线上的人都在镇定地站着。其中几个，这里几个，那里几个，被抹去了。

===Original===
The red sky in the southeast became redder with the rising sun. Hugo remained close to the wall. It was no novelty for him to be under shell fire. But at such times he felt the need of a caution with which he could ordinarily dispense. If one of the steel cylinders found him, even his mighty frame might not contain itself. Even he might be rent asunder. Shayne saw him and smiled. Twenty yards away a geyser of fire sprayed the heavens. Ten feet away a fragment of shell lashed down a pile of sandbags. Shayne’s smile widened. Hugo returned it.

Then red fury enveloped the two men. Hugo was crushed ferociously against the wall and liberated in the same second. He fell forward, his ears singing and his head dizzy. He lay there, aching. Dark red stains flowed over his face from his nose and ears. Painfully he stood up. A soldier was watching him from a distance with alarmed eyes. Hugo stepped. He found that locomotion was possible. The bedlam increased. It brought a sort of madness. He remembered Shayne. He searched in the smoking, stinking muck. He found the shoulders and part of Shayne’s head. He picked them up in his hands, disregarding the butchered ends of the raw gobbet. White electricity crackled in his head.

He leaped to the parapet, shaking his fists. “God damn you dirty sons of bitches. I’ll make you pay for this. You got him, got him, you bastards! I’ll shove your filthy hides down the devil’s throat and through his guts. Oh, Jesus!” He did not feel the frantic tugging of his fellows. He ran into that bubbling, doom-ridden chaos, waving his arms and shouting maniacal profanities. A dozen times he was knocked down. He bled slowly where fragments had battered him. He crossed over and paused on the German parapet. He was like a being of steel. Bullets sprayed him. His arms dangled and lifted. Barbed wire trailed behind him.

===Chinese===
东南方赤红的天空，随着太阳升起愈发红得发黑。雨果紧贴着壕壁。置身炮火之下，于他并非新鲜事。可每逢这种时候，他就感到需要一分平日尽可省去的谨慎。倘若那些钢铁圆筒中的一个找上他，即便他强健的躯壳也未必兜得住。连他也可能被撕作两半。谢恩看见他，笑了。二十码外，一股火的喷泉洒向天穹。十英尺外，一块弹片抽塌了一堆沙袋。谢恩的笑容咧得更开了。雨果报以一笑。

随即，赤红的狂怒裹住了两个人。雨果被凶狠地挤压在墙上，又在同一秒钟里被放开。他向前扑倒，耳朵里嗡嗡尖鸣，脑袋发晕。他躺在那里，浑身作痛。深红的血污从他鼻子和耳朵里漫过脸庞。他忍着痛站起来。一个士兵正从远处用惊惶的眼睛望着他。雨果迈了一步。他发现自己还能走动。喧嚣加剧了。喧嚣带来一种疯狂。他记起谢恩。他在冒烟发臭的泥泞里翻寻。他找到了谢恩的双肩和半个头颅。他把它们捧在手里，不顾那块生肉被剁斩的断口。白色的电流在他脑袋里噼啪乱炸。

他跳上胸墙，挥舞双拳。「你们这帮龌龊的狗娘养的，遭天杀的。我要你们血债血偿。你们干掉了他，干掉了他，你们这群畜生！我要把你们的臭皮囊塞进魔鬼的喉咙，再捅穿他的五脏。哦，上帝啊！」他感觉不到同伴们发疯般的拉拽。他跑进那片翻腾着、载满杀机的混沌，挥着双臂，喊出一串串癫狂的咒骂。他被打倒了十几次。弹片砸过的地方，血慢慢渗出来。他冲过无人地带，停在德军的胸墙上。他像一尊钢铁铸成的生灵。子弹朝他泼洒。他的双臂垂下又扬起。带刺铁丝在他身后拖成一串。

===Original===
Down before him, shoulder to shoulder, the attacking regiments waited for the last crescendo of the bombardment. They saw him come out of the fury and smiled grimly. They knew such madness. They shot. He came on. At last they could hear his voice dimly through the tumult. Someone shouted that he was mad﻿—to beware when he fell. Hugo jumped among them. Bayonets rose. Hugo wrenched three knives from their wielders in one wild clutch. His hands went out, snatching and squeezing. That was all. No weapons, no defence. Just﻿—hands. Whatever they caught they crushed flat, and heads fell into those dreadful fingers, sides, legs, arms, bellies. Bayonets slid from his tawny skin, taking his clothes. By and by, except for his shoes, he was naked. His fingers had made a hundred bunches of clotted pulp and then a thousand as he walked swiftly forward in that trench. Ahead of him was a file of green; behind, a clogged row of writhing men. Scarcely did the occupants of each new traverse see him before they were smitten. The wounds he inflicted were monstrous. On he walked, his voice now stilled, his breath sucking and whistling through his teeth, his hands flailing and pinching and spurting red with every contact. No more formidable engine of desolation had been seen by man, no more titanic fury, no swifter and surer death. For thirty minutes he raged through that line. The men thinned. He had crossed the attacking front.

Then the barrage lifted. But no whistles blew. No soldiers rose. A few raised their heads and then lay down again. Hugo stopped and went back into the abattoir. He leaped to the parapet. The French saw him, silhouetted against the sky. The second German wave, coming slowly over a far hill, saw him and hesitated. No ragged line of advancing men. No cacophony of rifle fire. Only that strange, savage figure. A man dipped in scarlet, nude, dripping, panting. Slowly in that hiatus he wheeled. His lungs thundered to the French. “Come on, you black bastards. I’ve killed them all. Come on. We’ll send them down to hell.”

===Chinese===
在他下方，肩并着肩，担任进攻的几个团正等候炮击最后的高潮。他们看见他从那片狂暴中走出，狞笑了。他们认得这种疯狂。他们开枪。他继续进逼。终于，他们能透过喧嚣隐约听见他的声音。有人喊道说他疯了——等他倒下时务须当心。雨果跳进他们中间。刺刀竖起。雨果一把狂抓，从持刀人手里拧下三把刺刀。他的双手伸出去，抓攫，紧捏。仅此而已。没有武器，没有防御。只有——一双手。它们抓住什么就把什么碾扁，脑袋落进那可怕的指间，还有肋腹、大腿、手臂、肚腹。刺刀从他褐黄的皮肤上滑脱，捎带下他的衣裳。渐渐地，除了鞋子，他周身赤裸。他在那条战壕里疾步向前，手指捏出了一百团、继而一千团凝血的烂肉。他身前是一列绿色的人影；身后，是一行壅塞着、翻滚扭动的人。每一段新壕里的守兵几乎还没看见他，就已被击中。他施加的创伤骇人听闻。他继续前行，声音已喑，呼吸从齿缝里抽气作哨，双手挥舞、掐捏，每一下接触都迸出红色。人类还从未见过比这更骇人的蹂躏机器，没有比这更磅礴的泰坦之怒，没有比这更快更确凿的死亡。他在那条战线上肆虐了三十分钟。人渐渐稀薄了。他已经越过了进攻方的整个正面。

随后拦阻炮火延伸了。可是没有哨音吹响。没有士兵跃起。有几个探了探头，又趴了回去。雨果停下，转身回到那座屠宰场里。他跳上胸墙。法军望见他，一个映在天幕上的剪影。德军的第二波，正缓缓翻过一座远丘，也望见了他，迟疑了。没有参差推进的散兵线。没有步枪齐射的嘈杂。只有那个古怪而野蛮的身影。一个浸透猩红的人，赤裸着，滴着血，喘着气。在那段间歇里，他缓缓转过身。他的肺向法军轰鸣。「上啊，你们这帮黑心杂种。我把他们全杀了。上啊。咱们把他们送下地狱。」

===Original===
The officers looked and understood that something phenomenal had happened. No Germans were coming. A man stood above their trench. “Come, quick!” Hugo shouted. He saw that they did not understand. He stood an instant, fell into the trench; and presently a shower of German corpses flung through the air in wide arcs and landed on the very edge of the French position. Then they came, and Hugo, seeing them, went on alone to meet the second line. He might have forged on through that bloody swathe to the heart of the Empire if his vitality had been endless. But, some time in the battle, he fell unconscious on the field, and his forward-leaning comrades, pushing back the startled enemy, found him lying there.

They made a little knot around him, silent, quivering. “It is the Colorado,” someone said. “His friend, Shayne﻿—it is he who was the lieutenant just killed.”

They shook their heads and felt a strange fear of the unconscious man. “He is breathing.” They called for stretcher-bearers. They faced the enemy again, bent over on the stocks of their rifles, surged forward.

===Chinese===
军官们望过去，明白出了异乎寻常的事。没有德国人过来了。一个人站在他们的战壕上方。「快，快来！」雨果喊道。他见他们不明白。他站了一瞬，落进战壕；不多时，一阵德军尸骸的骤雨划出大大的弧线飞过天空，恰恰落在法军阵地的边沿上。于是他们上来了，而雨果一见他们上来，便独自迎向第二道防线。倘若他的生命力无穷无尽，他本可以沿着那条血染的刈痕，直捣帝国的心脏。可是，战斗中的某一刻，他昏倒在了战场上；他那前倾着推进的战友们击退惊惶的敌人，发现他躺在那里。

他们围着他结成一小簇，沉默着，颤动着。「是那个科罗拉多，」有人说，「他的朋友谢恩——方才阵亡的那位中尉正是他。」

他们摇着头，对这昏迷不醒的人怀着一种莫名的恐惧。「他还有气。」他们喊来担架兵。他们再度面向敌人，弓身抵住枪托，涌向前去。

---
===Original===
Hugo was washed and dressed in pyjamas. His wounds had healed without the necessity of a single stitch. He was grateful for that. Otherwise the surgeons might have had a surprise which would have been difficult to allay. He sat in a wheel chair, staring across a lawn. An angular woman in an angular hat and tailored clothes was trying to engage him in conversation.

“Is it very painful, my man?”

Hugo was seeing that trench again﻿—the pulp and blood and hate of it. “Not very.”

Her tongue and saliva made a noise. “Don’t tell me. I know it was. I know how you all bleed and suffer.”

===Chinese===
雨果被洗净了身子，穿上睡衣睡裤。他的伤口都已愈合，一针也不必缝。他为此暗自庆幸。否则，外科医生们恐怕要吃一惊，而且那惊惶不易平息。他坐在轮椅里，凝望一片草坪。一个棱角分明的女人，戴一顶棱角分明的帽子，穿一身裁剪笔挺的衣裳，正试图同他攀谈。

「很疼吧，小伙子？」

雨果又看见了那条战壕——那其中的烂肉、鲜血和仇恨。「不太疼。」

她的舌头和唾沫出了声响。「别跟我抵赖了。我知道一定疼。我知道你们个个怎样流血、怎样受苦。」

===Original===
“Madam, it happens that my wounds were quite superficial.”

“Nonsense, my boy. They wouldn’t have brought you to a base hospital in that case. You can’t fool me.”

“I was suffering only from exhaustion.”

She paused. He saw a gleam in her eye. “I suppose you don’t like to talk﻿—about things. Poor boy! But I imagine your life has been so full of horror that it would be good for you to unburden yourself. Now tell me, just what does it feel like to bayonet a man?”

===Chinese===
「夫人，不巧我的伤都十分表浅。」

「胡说，孩子。要是那样，他们才不会把你送进后方医院呢。你骗不了我。」

「我只是力竭罢了。」

她顿了顿。他看见她眼里闪过一道光。「我猜你不爱谈——谈那些事。可怜的孩子！不过我想，你的生活一定充满了恐怖，把心事倾吐出来对你有好处。现在告诉我，用刺刀捅一个人，究竟是什么滋味？」

===Original===
Hugo trembled. He controlled his voice. “Madam,” he replied, “it feels exactly like sticking your finger into a warm, steaming pile of cow-dung.”

“Oh!” she gasped. And he heard her repeat it again in the corridor.

===Chinese===
雨果战栗了一下。他克制住了嗓音。「夫人，」他答道，「那滋味，恰恰就像把你的手指插进一堆热气腾腾的牛粪里。」

「哦！」她倒抽一口气。随后他听见她在走廊里又把这句话转述了一遍。

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
“**Mr. and Mrs. Ralph Jordan Shayne,**” Hugo wrote. Then he paused in thought. He began again. “I met your son in Marseilles and was with him most of the time until his death.” He hesitated. “In fact, he died in my arms from the effect of the same shell which sent me to this hospital. He is buried in Carcy cemetery, on the south side. It is for that reason I take the liberty to address you.

“I thought that you would like to know some of the things that he did not write to you. Your son enlisted because he felt the war involved certain ideals that were worthy of preservation. That he gave his life for those ideals must be a source of pride to you. In training he was always controlled, kindly, unquarrelsome, comprehending. In battle he was aggressive, brilliant, and more courageous than any other man I have ever known.

“In October, a year ago, he was decorated for bringing in Captain Crouan, who was severely wounded during an attack that was repulsed. Under heavy shell fire Tom went boldly into no man’s land and carried the officer from a shell pit on his back. At the time Tom himself sustained three wounds. He was mentioned a number of times in the dispatches for his leadership of attacks and patrols. He was decorated a second time for the capture of a German field officer and three of his staff, a coup which your son executed almost single-handed.

“Following his death his company made an attack to avenge him, which wiped out the entire enemy position along a sector nearly a kilometre in width and which brought a permanent advantage to the Allied lines. That is mute testimony of his popularity among the officers and men. I know of no man more worthy of the name ‘American,’ no American more worthy of the words ‘gentleman’ and ‘hero.’

“I realize the slight comfort of these things, and yet I feel bound to tell you of them, because Tom was my friend, and his death is grievous to me as well as to you.

===Chinese===
「**拉尔夫·乔丹·谢恩先生暨夫人：**」雨果写道。随即他停下来，凝神思索，又重新落笔。「我在马赛结识令郎，此后与他相处良久，直到他阵亡。」他踌躇了一下。「事实上，他就死在我的怀里，致他于死的是那发同样把我送进这家医院的炮弹。他安葬在卡尔西公墓的南侧。正是为此，我才冒昧修书致意二位。

「有些事他没有写信告诉过你们，我想二位会愿意知道。令郎投军从戎，是因为他感到这场战争关乎某些值得捍卫的理想。他为这些理想献出了生命，这想必足以令二位引以为豪。受训时，他始终克制、和善、不与人争、通达事理；作战时，他勇猛锐进、才干出众，比我平生所知的任何人都要英勇。

「一年前的十月，他因冒死救回克鲁昂上尉而受勋——上尉在一次被击退的进攻中身负重伤。汤姆顶着密集的炮火，只身闯入无人地带，把那位军官从弹坑里背了出来，而当时他自己也已身负三处创伤。他因指挥进攻与巡逻，屡屡在战报中受到表彰。他还第二次荣获奖章，是因为俘获了一名德军野战军官及其三名幕僚——这一壮举，几乎是令郎只身完成的。

「他阵亡之后，他所在的连队为替他复仇而发起进攻，一举荡平了正面宽近一公里的整段敌军阵地，为协约国战线赢得了一处持久的优势。这便是官兵上下爱戴他的无声明证。据我所知，没有谁比他更配得上『美国人』之名，也没有哪个美国人比他更配得上『绅士』与『英雄』这两个字眼。

「我明白，这些话慰藉有限，但我仍觉得非向二位奉告不可，因为汤姆是我的朋友，他的死，令我哀恸，也令你们哀恸。

===Original===
“Yours sincerely,

“(Lieutenant) Hugo Danner”

Hugo posted the letter. When the answer came, he was once again in action, the guns chugging and rumbling, the earth shaking. The reply read:

===Chinese===
「谨启，

「（中尉）雨果·丹纳」

雨果把信寄了出去。回信寄到时，他已再度置身战火之中，炮声突突轰鸣，大地颤抖。回信写道：

===Original===
“Dear Lieutenant Danner:

“Thank you for your letter in reference to our son. We knew that he had enlisted in some foreign service. We did not know of his death. I am having your statements checked, because, if they are true, I shall be one of the happiest persons alive, and his mother will be both happy and sad. The side of young Tom which you claim to have seen is one quite unfamiliar to us. At home he was always a waster, much of a snob, and impossible to control. It may be harsh to say such things of him now that he is dead, but I cannot recall one noble deed, one unselfish act, in his life here with us.

“That I have a dead son would not sadden me. Tom had been disinherited by us, his mother and father. But that my dead son was a hero makes me feel that at last, coming into the Shayne blood and heritage, he has atoned. And so I honour him. If the records show that all you said of him is true, I shall not only honour him in this country, but I shall come to France to pay my tribute with a full heart and a knowledge that neither he nor I lived in vain.

“Gratefully yours,

“R. J. Shayne”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的丹纳中尉：

「承蒙来函告知小儿之事，谢谢。我们只知道他投了某支外国军队，并不知道他已经死了。你信中所陈各节，我正在派人核实，因为倘若属实，我将成为世上最幸福的人之一，而他的母亲则会又喜又悲。你说你在小汤姆身上看到的那一面，于我们实在陌生得很。在家时，他向来是个败家子，十足的势利眼，且无从管教。人都死了还说这些，未免刻薄，可他在家中与我们共同度过的岁月里，我实在想不起一桩高尚的举动、一件无私的事。

「儿子死了，这件事本身并不使我悲伤。汤姆早已被我们——他的父母——剥夺了继承权。可我这位死去的儿子是个英雄，这使我感到，他终于融入了谢恩家的血统与门楣，赎清了前愆。所以我敬重他。倘若档案证明你所言句句属实，我不但要在这个国家敬重他，还要亲赴法兰西，向他致敬，且是满怀赤诚，并且心知：他没有虚度此生，我也没有。

「谨致谢忱，

「R·J·谢恩」

===Original===
Hugo reread the letter and stood awhile with wistful eyes. He remembered Shayne’s Aunt Emma, Shayne’s bitter calumniation of his family. Well, they had not understood him and he had not wanted them to understand him. Perhaps Shayne had been more content than he admitted in the mud of the trenches. The war had been a real thing to him. Hugo thought of its insufficiencies for himself. The world was not enough for Shayne, but the war had been. Both were insufficient for Hugo Danner. He listened to the thunder in the sky tiredly.

Two months later Hugo was ordered from rest billets to the major’s quarters. A middle-aged man and woman accompanied by a sleek Frenchman awaited him. The man stepped forward with dignified courtesy. “I am Tom Shayne’s father. This is Mrs. Shayne.”

Hugo felt a great lack of interest in them. They had come too late. It was their son who had been his friend. He almost regretted the letter. He shook hands with them. Mrs. Shayne went to an automobile. Her husband invited Hugo to a café. Over the wine he became suddenly less dignified, more human, and almost pathetic. “Tell me about him, Danner. I loved that kid once, you know.”

Hugo found himself unexpectedly moved. The man was so eager, so strangely happy. He stroked his white moustache and turned away moist eyes. So Hugo told him. He talked endlessly of the trenches and the dark wet nights and the fire that stabbed through them. He invented brave sorties for his friend, tripled his accomplishments, and put gaiety and wit in his mouth. The father drank every syllable as if he was committing the whole story to memory as the text of a life’s solace. At last he was crying.

===Chinese===
雨果把信重读了一遍，怅然伫立良久。他想起谢恩的艾玛姑妈，想起谢恩对自己家人那番怨毒的诋毁。唉，他们不曾理解他，他也从没想让他们理解。也许，在战壕的泥泞里，谢恩比自己肯承认的要知足。这场战争于他是件实实在在的事。雨果想到战争于自己的不足。这个世界满足不了谢恩，战争却满足了他；而对雨果·丹纳，两者都嫌太小。他疲惫地听着天上的雷鸣。

两个月后，雨果奉命从休整营房前往少校的住所。一位中年男子和一位妇人正在那里等他，身旁陪着个油头粉面的法国人。男子仪态庄重、客气周到地上前一步。「我是汤姆·谢恩的父亲。这位是谢恩太太。」

雨果对他们兴味索然。他们来得太迟了。他的朋友，是他们的儿子。他几乎后悔写了那封信。他与他们握了手。谢恩太太走向一辆汽车，她丈夫请雨果去了一家咖啡馆。几杯酒下肚，他忽然放下了架子，更有人情味了，还近乎可怜。「跟我讲讲他吧，丹纳。你要知道，我从前是疼那孩子的。」

雨果发觉自己竟出乎意料地被打动了。这人是那样急切，那样出奇地快乐。他抚着白胡子，转过湿润的眼睛。于是雨果讲了起来。他滔滔不绝地讲战壕，讲黑暗潮湿的夜晚，讲刺破夜色的炮火。他为朋友虚构了一场场英勇的出击，把他的功绩翻了三倍，还替他说出种种风趣与妙语。那位父亲一字一音都贪婪地喝了下去，仿佛要把整个故事背进记忆，当作余生慰藉的经文。最后，他哭了起来。

===Original===
“That was the Tom I knew,” Hugo said softly.

“And that was the Tom I dreamed and hoped and thought he would become when he was a little shaver. Well, he did, Danner.”

“A thousand times he did.”

Ralph Jordan Shayne blew his nose unashamedly. He thought of his patiently waiting wife. “I’ve got to go, I suppose. This has been more than kind of you, Mr. Danner﻿—Lieutenant Danner. I’m glad﻿—more glad than I can say﻿—that you were there. I understand from the major that you’re no small shakes in this army yourself.” He smiled deferentially. “I wish there was something we could do for you.”

===Chinese===
「我认识的汤姆，就是那样的。」雨果轻声说。

「那也是我当年梦想、盼望并且认定他会长成的那种人——在他还是个小娃娃的时候。唉，他做到了，丹纳。」

「何止做到，他做到了千倍万倍。」

拉尔夫·乔丹·谢恩毫不忌讳地擤了擤鼻子。他想起还在耐心等候的妻子。「我想，我该走了。你实在太过客气了，丹纳先生——丹纳中尉。我很庆幸——庆幸得难以言表——当时有你在他身边。听少校说，你本人在这支军队里也不是等闲角色。」他恭谨地笑了笑。「真希望我们能为你做点什么。」

===Original===
“Nothing. Thank you, Mr. Shayne.”

“I’m going to give you my card. In New York﻿—my name is not without meaning.”

“It is very familiar to me. Was before I met your son.”

“If you ever come to the city﻿—I mean, when you come﻿—you must look us up. Anything we can do﻿—in the way of jobs, positions﻿—” He was confused.

Hugo shook his head. “That’s very kind of you, sir. But I have some means of my own and, right now, I’m not even thinking of going back to New York.”

===Chinese===
「没有什么。谢谢您，谢恩先生。」

「我要给你留一张名片。在纽约——我的名字并非无足轻重。」

「这名字我熟得很。还没认识令郎之前就熟了。」

「你若哪天到纽约来——我是说，等你来的时候——一定要来找我们。但凡我们能效劳的——差事啦、职位啦——」他有些语无伦次了。

雨果摇了摇头。「您真是太好了，先生。不过我自己还有些资财，而且眼下，我连回纽约的事都还没有想过。」

===Original===
Mr. Shayne stepped into the car. “I would like to do something.” Hugo realized the sincerity of that desire. He reflected.

“Nothing I can think of﻿—”

“I’m a banker. Perhaps﻿—if I might take the liberty﻿—I could handle your affairs?”

Hugo smiled. “My affairs consist of one bank account in the City Loan that would seem very small to you, Mr. Shayne.”

“Why, that’s one of my banks. I’ll arrange it. You know and I know how small the matter of money is. But I’d appreciate your turning over some of your capital to me. I would consider it a blessed opportunity to return a service, a great service with a small one, I’m afraid.”

===Chinese===
谢恩先生跨进汽车。「我总想为你做点什么。」雨果觉察出这份心愿的诚挚。他沉吟了一下。

「我想不出有什么——」

「我是个银行家。也许——倘若容我冒昧——你的事务可以交给我代管？」

雨果微微一笑。「我的全部事务，不过是城市贷款银行（City Loan）里的一个户头——在您看来想必微不足道，谢恩先生。」

「哎呀，那正是我开的银行之一。这事我来安排。你我心里都明白，金钱是何等小事。不过，你若肯把一部分资本移到我名下打理，我将感激不尽。我会把它看作一个天赐良机，得以回报一份恩情——只怕是以小惠报大德了。」

===Original===
“Thanks,” Hugo said.

The banker scribbled a statement, asked a question, and raised his eyebrows over the amount Hugo gave him. Then he was the father again. “We’ve been to the cemetery, Danner. We owe that privilege to you. It says there, in French: ‘The remains of a great hero who gave his life for France.’ Not America, my boy; but I think that France was a worthy cause.”

When they had gone, Hugo spent a disturbed afternoon. He had not been so moved in many, many months.

===Chinese===
「多谢。」雨果说。

银行家匆匆写下一纸文书，问了一个问题，听到雨果报出的数目，不禁扬起了眉毛。随后，他又是那位父亲了。「我们去墓地了，丹纳。这份荣幸是拜你所赐。那里用法文写着：『一位为法兰西捐躯的伟大英雄之遗骸。』不是为美国，我的孩子；不过我想，法兰西是一项值得的事业。」

他们走后，雨果心乱如麻地度过了那个下午。他已经许多许多个月不曾如此动情了。

## XV / 第十五章

===Original===
Now the streets of Paris were assailed by the color of olive drab, the twang of Yankee accents, the music of Broadway songs. Hugo watched the first parade with eyes somewhat proud and not a little sombre. Each shuffling step seemed to ask a rhythmic question. Who would not return to Paris? Who would return once and not again? Who would be blind? Who would be hideous? Who would be armless, legless, who would wear silver plates and leather props for his declining years? Hugo wondered, and, looking into those sometimes stern and sometimes ribald faces, he saw that they had not yet commenced to wonder.

They did not know the hammer and shock of falling shells and the jelly and putty which men became. They chafed and bantered and stormed every café and cocotte impartially, recklessly. Even the Legion had been more grim and better prepared for the iron feet of war. They fell upon Hugo with their atrocious French﻿—two young men who wanted a drink and could not make the bartender understand.

“Hey, fransay,” they called to him, “*comment dire que nous voulez des choses boire?*”

===Chinese===
如今，巴黎的街头被橄榄绿的颜色、扬基腔的鼻音、百老汇歌曲的调子所侵袭。雨果看着第一批入城的阅兵队伍，眼神里带着几分自豪，也有不小的阴郁。每一个拖沓的脚步都仿佛在应着一个节奏打出问句：谁将回不了巴黎？谁将回来一次便不再回？谁会瞎？谁会面目狰狞？谁会没了胳膊、没了腿，下半辈子靠银板和皮撑子撑着过活？雨果想着这些，望进那些时而冷峻、时而浪荡的面孔，看出他们自己还没开始想。

他们还不知道炮弹坠下时那铁锤般的冲击，不知道人会变成怎样的肉冻和烂泥。他们躁动、打趣，不分彼此、毫无顾忌地闹遍每一家咖啡馆、缠上每一个风尘女子。就连外籍军团也比他们更阴沉、更经得起战争那双铁蹄的践踏。他们操着蹩脚的法语向雨果袭来——是两个想喝点什么却没法让酒保听懂的年轻人。

“嘿，fransay（法国佬），”他们朝他喊，“*comment dire que nous voulez des choses boire?*（‘我们想喝点东西’怎么说？）”

===Original===
Hugo smiled. “What do you birds want to drink?”

“God Almighty! Here’s a Frog that speaks United States. Get the gang. What’s your name, bo?”

“Danner.”

“Come on an’ have a flock of drinks on us. You’re probably dying on French pay. You order for the gang and we’ll treat.” Eager, grinning American faces. “Can you get whisky in this Godforsaken dump?”

“Straight or highball?”

“That’s the talk. Straight, Dan. We’re in the army now.”

===Chinese===
雨果笑了。“你们这帮小子想喝什么？”

“全能的上帝！这儿有个会说美国话的法国佬。把大伙儿都叫来。你叫什么，老兄？”

“丹纳。”

“来，跟我们喝上一大轮，我们请。拿法国人的军饷，你八成快饿死了。你替大伙儿点单，我们掏钱。”一张张热切嬉笑的美国面孔。“这鬼不拉屎的破地方能弄到威士忌吗？”

“纯饮还是兑苏打？”

“这才像话。纯的，丹。咱们如今是当兵的人了。”

===Original===
Hugo drank with them. Only for one moment did they remember they were in the army to fight: “Say, Dan, the war really isn’t as tough as they claim, is it?”

“I don’t know how tough they claim it is.”

“Well, you seen much fightin’?”

“Three years.”

“Is it true that the Heinies﻿—?” His hands indicated his question.

“Sometimes. Accidentally, more or less. You can’t help it.”

===Chinese===
雨果跟他们喝了起来。只有那么一小会儿，他们才想起自己参军是来打仗的：“哎，丹，这仗其实不像他们讲的那么苦，对吧？”

“我不知道他们讲得有多苦。”

“那你见过不少仗啰？”

“三年。”

“德国佬真的会——？”他的双手替他比出了问题。

“有时候会。多少算是失手，免不了的。”

===Original===
“And do them machine guns really mow ’em down?”

Hugo shrugged. “There are only four men in service now who started with my company.”

“Ouch! Garçon! Encore! An’ tell him to make it double﻿—no, triple﻿—Dan, old man. It may be my last.” To Hugo: “Well, it’s about time we got here an’ took the war off your shoulders. You guys sure have had a bellyful. An’ I’m goin’ to get me one right here and now. Bottoms up, you guys.”

===Chinese===
“那些机枪真能把人成片撂倒？”

雨果耸了耸肩。“当初跟我那个连一起入伍的，如今还留着的只剩四个人。”

“哎哟！Garçon（伙计）！Encore（再来）！叫他给我加倍——不，来三倍的——丹，老伙计，这没准儿是我的最后一杯了。”又对雨果说：“行了，也该咱们上这儿来，把这场仗从你们肩上接过去了。你们这帮家伙可真是受够了。我这就要在这儿、就现在，痛痛快快来上一顿。干杯，伙计们。”

===Original===
Hugo was transferred to an American unit. The officers belittled the recommendations that came with him. They put him in the ranks. He served behind the lines for a week. Then his regiment moved up. As soon as the guns began to rumble, a nervous second lieutenant edged toward the demoted private. “Say, Danner, you’ve been in this before. Do you think it’s all right to keep on along this road the way we are?”

“I’m sure I couldn’t say. You’re taking a chance. Plane strafing and shells.”

“Well, what else are we to do? These are our orders.”

“Nothing,” Hugo said.

===Chinese===
雨果被转调到一支美军部队。军官们并不把他随身带来的举荐当回事。他们把他编进列兵的行伍。他在后方待了一星期。随后他的团开拔上前线。炮声一响，一名紧张兮兮的少尉便朝这个被降了职的列兵凑过来。“哎，丹纳，你从前打过仗。你看咱们照现在这样沿这条路走下去，行不行？”

“我说不好。你们这是在碰运气。飞机扫射，还有炮弹。”

“可我们还能怎么办？命令就是这样。”

“没办法，”雨果说。

===Original===
When the first shells fell among them, however, Danner forgot that his transference had cost his commission and sadly bereft Captain Crouan and his command. He forgot his repressed anger at the stupidity of American headquarters and their bland assumption of knowledge superior to that gained by three years of actual fighting. He virtually took charge of his company, ignoring the bickering of a lieutenant who swore and shouted and accomplished nothing and who was presently beheaded for his lack of caution. A month later, with troops that had some feeling of respect for the enemy﻿—a feeling gained through close and gory association﻿—Hugo was returned his commission.

Slowly at first, and with increasing momentum, the war was pushed up out of the trenches and the Germans retreated. The summer that filled the windows of American homes with gold stars passed. Hugo worked like a slave out beyond the front trenches, scouting, spying, destroying, salvaging, bending his heart and shoulders to a task that had long since become an acid routine. September. October. November. The end of that holocaust was very near.

Then there came a day warmer than the rest and less rainy. Hugo was riding toward the lines on a camion. He rode as much as possible now. He had not slept for two days. His eyes were red and twitching. He felt tired﻿—tired as if his fatigue were the beginning of death﻿—tired so that nothing counted or mattered﻿—tired of killing, of hating, of suffering﻿—tired even of an ideal that had tarnished through long weathering. The camion was steel and it rattled and bumped as it moved over the road. Hugo lay flat in it, trying to close his eyes.

===Chinese===
然而，等第一批炮弹落进他们中间，丹纳便忘了这次转调让他丢了军衔、让克鲁昂上尉和他的部下痛失一员；也忘了他对美国司令部那股压着的怒火——他们一副安然自若的做派，只当天生比三年实战换来的知识更高明。他实际上接管了自己的连，全不理会一个少尉的聒噪——那少尉骂骂咧咧、大喊大叫、一事无成，不久就因不知谨慎而丢了脑袋。一个月后，带着一支部下——他们对敌人已生出几分敬意，那是从贴身血肉的交锋里换来的敬意——雨果官复原职。

起初缓慢，随后势头越来越猛，战争被一步步顶出战壕，德国人节节后退。那个在美国人家的窗棂上缀满金星（家有阵亡者的标志）的夏天过去了。雨果在前沿战壕之外像奴隶一样劳作：侦察、刺探、破坏、抢修，把心和肩膀一齐折进一桩早已沤成辛酸例行公事的苦役。九月。十月。十一月。那场大屠杀的终结已近在眼前。

后来来了一个比别的日子暖和些、雨也少些的天。雨果坐着一辆军用卡车朝前线去。如今他能坐车就尽量坐车。他两天没合眼了。眼睛红着，直抽动。他觉得累——累得仿佛这份疲惫就是死亡的开端——累得什么都不算数、什么都无所谓——累得厌倦了杀戮、厌倦了仇恨、厌倦了受苦——连那个理想也厌倦了，它在长年的风吹雨打里早已失了光泽。卡车是钢的，一路哐啷哐啷、磕磕绊绊。雨果平躺在车斗里，努力想合上眼。

===Original===
After a time, moving between the stumps of a row of poplars, they came abreast of a regiment returning from the battle. They walked slowly and dazedly. Each individual was still amazed at being alive after the things he had witnessed. Hugo raised himself and looked at them. The same expression had often been on the faces of the French. The long line of the regiment ended. Then there was an empty place on the road, and the speed of the truck increased.

Finally it stopped with a sharp jar, and the driver shouted that he could go no farther. Hugo clambered to the ground. He estimated that the battery toward which he was travelling was a mile farther. He began to walk. There was none of the former lunge and stride in his steps. He trudged, rather, his head bent forward. A little file of men approached him, and, even at a distance, he did not need a second glance to identify them. Walking wounded.

By ones and twos they began to pass him. He paid scant attention. Their field dressings were stained with the blood that their progress cost. They cursed and muttered. Someone had given them cigarettes, and a dozen wisps of smoke rose from each group. It was not until he reached the end of the straggling line that he looked up. Then he saw one man whose arms were both under bandage walking with another whose eyes were covered and whose hand, resting on his companion’s shoulder, guided his stumbling feet.

===Chinese===
过了一阵，车子在一排白杨树的残桩间穿行，同一个刚从战场上撤下来的团并行。他们走得又慢又恍惚。每个人都还在诧异，自己目睹了那些场面之后居然还活着。雨果支起身子望他们。同样的神情，常在法国人的脸上见过。团的长队走完了。路上空出一段，卡车加快了速度。

最后，车身猛地一震，停住了，司机喊着说他没法再往前开。雨果爬下车。他估摸着，要去的那座炮位还在一英里开外。他开始步行。脚步里全没了从前那种一冲一跨的劲头。他只是跋涉，头朝前倾着。一小队人迎面走来，隔着老远，他不必看第二眼就能认出：还能行走的伤员。

他们三三两两地开始从他身边经过。他没怎么留意。他们的战地绷带上洇着行路耗出的血。他们咒骂着、咕哝着。有人给了他们香烟，每一小簇人头上都升起十几缕烟。直到走到这支拖沓长队的末尾，他才抬起头来。于是他看见一个人，两条胳膊都缠着绷带，同另一个人并肩走着；那另一个双眼蒙着，一只手搭在同伴肩上，引着自己踉跄的脚步。

===Original===
Hugo viewed them as they came on and presently heard their conversation. “Christ, it hurts,” one of them said.

“The devil with hurting, boy,” the blinded man answered. “So do I, for that matter. I feel like there was a hot poker in my brains.”

“Want another butt?”

“No, thanks. Makes me kind of sick to drag on them. Wish I had a drink, though.”

“Who doesn’t?”

Hugo heard his voice. “Hey, you guys,” it said. “Here’s some water. And a shot of cognac, too.”

===Chinese===
雨果望着他们走近，随即听见了他们的谈话。“老天，疼啊，”其中一个说。

“疼个屁，小子，”瞎了眼的那人答道。“要这么说，我也一样。我觉得脑仁里像插了根烧红的火钳。”

“再来支烟？”

“不了，谢了。抽多了有点恶心。倒是真想喝一口。”

“谁不想？”

雨果听见自己开口了。“嘿，你们俩，”那声音说。“这儿有点水。还有一口干邑。”

===Original===
The first man stopped and the blind man ran into him, bumping his head. He gasped with pain, but his lips smiled. “Damn nice of you, whoever you are.”

They took the canteen and swallowed. “Go on,” Hugo said, and permitted himself a small lie. “I can get more in a couple of hours.” He produced his flask. “And finish off on a shot of this.”

He held the containers for the armless man and handed them to the other. Their clothes were ragged and stained. Their shoes were in pieces. Sweat had soaked under the blind man’s armpits and stained his tunic. As Hugo watched him swallow thirstily, he started. The chin and the hair were familiar. His mind spun. He knew the voice, although its tenor was sadly changed.

“Good God,” he said involuntarily, “it’s Lefty!”

Lefty stiffened. “Who are you?”

“Hugo Danner.”

===Chinese===
头一个人停住脚，瞎眼的收不住撞了上去，磕了脑袋。他疼得倒抽冷气，嘴唇却笑了。“真够意思，不管你是谁。”

他们接过水壶，吞了几口。“接着走，”雨果说，还容许自己撒了个小谎。“过几个钟头我还能再弄到。”他掏出扁酒壶。“再把这个也干了，收个尾。”

他替那个双臂不能动的人举着容器，再递给另一个。两人的衣衫又破又脏。鞋子都成了碎片。瞎眼那人腋下被汗浸透，军装上洇出汗渍。雨果看着他干渴地吞咽，猛然一怔。那下巴、那头发，好生眼熟。他的脑子飞转起来。那声音他认得，尽管它的音调已变得令人心酸。

“老天爷，”他不由自主地说，“是左撇子！”

左撇子僵住了。“你是谁？”

“雨果·丹纳。”

===Original===
“Hugo Danner?” The tortured brain reflected.

“Hugo! Good old Hugo! What, in the name of Jesus, are you doing here?”

“Same thing you are.”

An odd silence fell. The man with the shattered arms broke it. “Know this fellow?”

“Do I know him! Gee! He was at college with me. One of my buddies. Gosh!” His hand reached out. “Put it there, Hugo.”

They shook hands. “Got it bad, Lefty?”

===Chinese===
“雨果·丹纳？”那颗受尽折磨的脑子回想着。

“雨果！好一个老雨果！看在耶稣份上，你怎么会在这儿？”

“跟你干的一样。”

一阵古怪的沉默。那双臂尽毁的人打破了它。“认识这家伙？”

“何止认识！嗬！他跟我上的同一个大学。我的老哥们儿之一。天哪！”他伸出手来。“把手放这儿，雨果。”

他们握了手。“伤得重吗，左撇子？”

===Original===
The bound head shook. “Not so bad. I guess﻿—I kind of feel that I won’t be able to see much any more. Eyes all washed out. Got mustard gas in ’em. But I’ll be all right, you know. A little thing like that’s nothing. Glad to be alive. Still have my sex appeal, anyhow. Still got the old appetite. But﻿—listen﻿—what happened to you? Why in hell did you quit? Woodman nearly went crazy looking for you.”

“Oh﻿—” Hugo’s thoughts went back a distance that seemed infinite, into another epoch and another world﻿—“oh, I just couldn’t stick it. Say, you guys, wait a minute.” He turned. His camion-driver was lingering in the distance. “Wait here.” He rushed back. The armless man whistled.

“God in heaven! Your friend there can sure cover the ground.”

“Yeah,” Lefty said absently. “He always could.”

In a moment Hugo returned. “I got it all fixed up for you two to ride in. No limousine, but it’ll carry you.”

Lefty’s lip trembled. “Gee﻿—Jesus Christ﻿—” he amended stubbornly; “that’s decent. I don’t feel so dusty today. Damn it, if I had any eyes, I guess I’d cry. Must be the cognac.”

===Chinese===
那颗缠着绷带的头摇了摇。“不算重。我猜——我总有点觉得，往后再看不清什么东西了。眼睛全泡坏了。进了芥子气。不过我会没事的，你放心。这点小事不算什么。活着就高兴。不管怎么说，我那点男人魅力还在。老胃口也还在。可是——听着——你是怎么回事？你到底为什么撂挑子不干了？伍德曼找你差点找疯了。”

“哦——”雨果的思绪退回一段仿佛无限遥远的距离，退进另一个纪元、另一个世界——“哦，我就是熬不下去了。哎，你们俩，等一分钟。”他转过身。他的卡车司机还在远处磨蹭。“在这儿等着。”他飞奔回去。断臂的人吹了声口哨。

“老天爷！你那位朋友跑得可真快。”

“可不是，”左撇子心不在焉地说。“他一直就那么能跑。”

不大工夫雨果就回来了。“我全给你们俩安排好车了。算不上豪华轿车，但能拉你们。”

左撇子的嘴唇抖了。“哎呀——老天爷——”他又倔强地改了口；“真够意思。我今天浑身不得劲。妈的，要是我这双眼睛还在，我想我会哭的。准是那干邑闹的。”

===Original===
“Nothing at all, Lefty old kid. Here, I’ll give you a hand.” He took Lefty’s arm over his shoulder, encircled him with his own, and carried him rapidly over the broken road.

“Still got the old fight,” Lefty murmured as he felt himself rushed forward.

“Still.”

“Been in this mess long?”

“Since the beginning.”

===Chinese===
“小事一桩，左撇子老伙计。来，我搭把手。”他把左撇子的手臂架到自己肩上，又用自己的胳膊环住他，带着他飞快走过那段残破的路。

“当年那股子劲头一点没减，”左撇子觉得自己被推着疾行，喃喃道。

“没减。”

“这场烂仗打了很久啦？”

“从开打那天起。”

===Original===
“I should have thought of that. I often wondered what became of you. Iris used to wonder, too.”

“How is she?”

“All right.”

They reached the truck. Lefty sat down on the metal bottom with a sigh. “Thanks, old bean. I was just about kaputt. Tough going, this war. I saw my first shell fall yesterday. Never saw a single German at all. One of those squdgy things came across, and before I knew it, there was onion in my eye for a goal.” The truck motor roared. The armless man came alongside and was lifted beside Lefty. “Well, Hugo, so long. You sure were a friend in need. Never forget it. And look me up when the Krauts are all dead, will you?” The gears clashed. “Thanks again﻿—and for the cognac, too.” He waved airily. “See you later.”

Hugo stalked back on the road. Once he looked over his shoulder. The truck was a blur of dust. “See you later. See you later. See you later.” Lefty would never see him later﻿—never see anyone ever.

===Chinese===
“我早该想到这一层。我常纳闷你后来去了哪儿。艾瑞丝从前也常念叨。”

“她好吗？”

“挺好的。”

他们到了卡车跟前。左撇子叹了口气，在铁车斗上坐下来。“谢了，老伙计。我刚才都快趴窝了。这仗打得真够呛。昨天我才头一回见着炮弹落地。一个德国人都没见着过。一个那种软塌塌的玩意儿飞过来，还没等我明白过来，一颗洋葱就进了我的眼睛当球门。”卡车马达轰响起来。断臂的人被搀到车旁，举到左撇子身边坐下。“好了，雨果，再见。你可真是个雪中送炭的朋友。一辈子忘不了。等德国佬都死绝了，来看看我，行不？”排挡咔啦一响。“再谢一回——还有那口干邑。”他满不在乎地挥挥手。“回头见。”

雨果大步走回路上。他回过一次头。卡车已经成了一团模糊的尘影。“回头见。回头见。回头见。”左撇子永远不会有那个“回头见”了——永远再也见不着任何人。

===Original===
That night he sat in a quiet stupor, all thought of great ideal, of fine abandon, of the fury of justice, and all flagrant phrases brought to an abrupt end by the immediate claims of his own sorrow. Tom Shayne was blasted to death. The stinging horror of mustard had fallen into Lefty’s eyes. All the young men were dying. The friendships he had made, the human things that gave in memory root to the earth were ripped up and shrivelled. That seemed grossly wrong and patently ignoble. He discarded his personal travail. It was nothing. His life had been comprised of attempt and failure, of disappointment and misunderstanding; he was accustomed to witness the blunting of the edge of his hopes and the dulling of his desires when they were enacted.

Even his great sacrifice had been vain. It was always thus. His deeds frightened men or made them jealous. When he conceived a fine thing, the masses, individually or collectively, transformed it into something cheap. His fort in the forest had been branded a hoax. His effort to send himself through college and to rescue Charlotte from an unpleasant life had ended in vulgar comedy. Even that had been her triumph, her hour, and an incongruous strain of greatness had filtered through her personality rather than his. Now his years in the war were reduced to no grandeur, to a mere outlet for his savage instinct to destroy. After such a life, he reflected, he could no longer visualize himself engaged in any search for a comprehension of real values.

===Chinese===
那天夜里，他坐在一片安静的木然里，一切关于伟大理想、关于壮烈的投身、关于正义之怒的念头，一切浮夸的辞藻，都被眼下的哀伤戛然截断。汤姆·谢恩被炸得粉身碎骨。芥子气那灼人的恐怖落进了左撇子的眼睛。年轻人都在死去。他结下的那些情谊，那些在记忆里把人扎进泥土的尘世之物，被连根拔起、晒得枯萎。这显得粗暴地错谬，昭然地卑劣。他把自己个人的苦难撇开。那不算什么。他这一生本就由尝试与失败、失望与误解拼成；他早已惯于目睹希望的锋刃变钝、愿望在兑现的那一刻黯然失色。

连他那场伟大的牺牲也是徒然。向来如此。他的事迹吓坏了人们，或是招来嫉妒。每当他构想出一件美好的事，众人便或单个、或成群地把它变成廉价货。他在林中的堡垒被扣上骗局的骂名。他供自己读完大学、把夏洛特从一种不愉快的生活里搭救出来的努力，落得一场庸俗的喜剧收场。就连那也成了她的胜利、她的时辰，一缕不甚相称的伟大透过她而非他的人格渗漏出来。如今他这几年的战火岁月也缩水得毫无壮丽可言，只剩他毁灭野性的一个出气口。经过这样的一生，他想，他再无法设想自己去从事任何一场对真正价值的求索了。

===Original===
His mind was thorny with doubts. Seeing himself as a man made hypocritical by his gifts and the narrowness of the world, discarding his own problem as tragically solved, Hugo then looked upon the war as the same sort of colossal error. A waste. Useless, hopeless, gaining nothing but the temporal power which it so blatantly disavowed, it had exacted the price of its tawdry excitement in lives, and, now that it was almost finished, mankind was ready to emerge blank-faced and panting, no better off than before.

His heart ached as he thought of the toil, the effort, the energy and hope and courage that had been spilled over those mucky fields to satisfy the lusts and foolish hates of the demagogues. He was no longer angry. The memory of Lefty sitting smilingly on the van and calling that he would see him later was too sharp an emotion to permit brain storms and pyrotechnics.

===Chinese===
他的头脑里长满怀疑的荆棘。他把自己看作一个被自身的天赋和世界的狭隘逼成了伪君子的人，把自己的难题当作一场悲剧性的了结而弃置一旁；于是雨果把这场战争也看成同一种庞然的谬误。一场浪费。无用，无望，除了它公然撇清的那种一时的权势之外一无所获；它拿性命偿付了那俗艳刺激的价码，而如今它行将收场，人类正准备一脸空白、气喘吁吁地走出来，境况不比从前好上分毫。

一想到那倾泼在这些泥泞田野上的辛劳、气力、精力与希望和勇气，只为了喂饱煽动家们的私欲和愚蠢的仇恨，他的心便发疼。他不再愤怒了。左撇子笑吟吟坐在卡车上、喊着回头再见的那段记忆，是太锋利的一种情感，容不得脑海里的风暴与烟火。

===Original===
If he could but have ended the war single-handed, it might have been different. But he was not great enough for that. He had been a thousand men, perhaps ten thousand, but he could not be millions. He could not wrap his arms around a continent and squeeze it into submission. There were too many people and they were too stupid to do more than fear him and hate him. Sitting there, he realized that his naive faith in himself and the universe had foundered. The war was only another war that future generations would find romantic to contemplate and dull to study. He was only a species of genius who had missed his mark by a cosmic margin.

When he considered his failure, he believed that he was not thinking about himself. There he was, entrusted with special missions which he accomplished no one knew how, and no one questioned in those hectic days. Those who had seen him escape machine-gun fire, carry tons, leap a hundred yards, kill scores, still clung to their original concepts of mankind and discredited the miracle their own eyes had witnessed. Too many strange things happened in that blasting carnival of destruction for one strange sight or one strange man to leave a great mark. Personal security was at too great a premium to leave much room for interest and speculation. Even Captain Crouan believed he was only a man of freak strength and Major Ingalls in his present situation was too busy to do more than note that Hugo was capable and nod his head when Hugo reported another signal victory, ascribing it to his long experience in the war rather than to his peculiar abilities.

===Chinese===
倘若他能单枪匹马终结这场战争，也许会是另一番光景。可他还没有伟大到那个地步。他抵得上千人，或许万人，却抵不成几百万人。他无法张开双臂环住一片大陆，把它勒到服帖。人太多了，而且他们蠢得除了怕他、恨他之外一无所能。坐在那里，他意识到他对自己和宇宙的那份天真的信念已经沉没。这场战争不过是又一场战争，后代子孙玄想时觉得浪漫、研读时觉得乏味的那种。他不过是一种天才，一种在天文尺度的偏差上脱了靶的天才。

他思量自己的失败时，相信自己所想的并非自己。瞧瞧他的处境：受命执行种种特殊任务，完成得神不知鬼不觉，在那些狂乱的日子里也没有人过问。那些亲眼见他躲过机枪火力、扛起数吨重物、一跃百码、杀敌数十的人，仍旧抱守他们对人类的原有观念，不肯相信自己双目见证的奇迹。在那场炸裂的毁灭狂欢里，怪事太多，一件怪景或一个怪人留不下多大的印记。人身安全的价钱高到离谱，没给好奇与推究留多少余地。连克鲁昂上尉也只当他是个天生怪力的汉子；而英戈尔斯少校眼下的处境太忙，至多记下雨果能干，在雨果报告又一次辉煌胜利时点一点头，把它归功于他在战争中资历深厚，而非归功于他那特异的本领。

===Original===
As he sat empty-eyed in the darkness, smoking cigarettes and breathing in his own and the world’s tragic futility, his own and the world’s abysmal sorrow, that stubborn ancestral courage and determination that was in him still continued to lash his reason. “Even if the war is not worth while,” it whispered, “you have committed yourself to it. You are bound and pledged to see it to the bitter end. You cannot finish it on a declining note. Tonight, tomorrow, you must begin again.” At the same time his lust for carnage stirred within him like a long-subdued demon. Now he recognized it and knew that it must be mastered. But it combined with his conscience to quicken his sinews anew.

It was a cold night, but Hugo perspired. Was he to go again into the holocaust to avenge a friend? Was he to live over those crimson seconds that followed the death of Shayne, all because he had helped a blind friend into a camion? He knew that he was not. Never again could his instinct so triumph over his reason. That was the greatest danger in being Hugo Danner. That, he commenced to see, was the explanation of all his suffering in the past. The idea warmed and encouraged him. Henceforth his emotions and sentiments would be buried even deeper than his first inbred caution had buried them. He would be a creature of intelligence, master of his caprice as well as of the power he possessed to carry out that caprice.

===Chinese===
他两眼空空地坐在黑暗里，抽着烟，吸进自己与世界的悲剧性徒劳，吸进自己与世界的深渊般的哀伤；而他与生俱来的那份倔强的勇气与决断，仍在不住鞭挞他的理智。“纵然这场战争不值得，”它低语道，“你已把自己许给了它。你受了约束、立了誓，要眼看它到最后一刻。你不能在颓音上收场。今夜，明日，你必须重新开始。”与此同时，他对杀戮的渴望像一头久被制服的恶魔，在他体内蠢动。此刻他认出了它，知道必须将它降服。但它与他的良心合流，重新绷紧了他的筋腱。

夜很冷，雨果却出汗了。他还要再赴那场大屠杀，去为一个朋友复仇吗？他还要把谢恩死后那猩红的几秒钟重活一遍——只因为他扶了一个瞎眼的朋友上卡车？他知道自己不会了。他的本能再也不能这样压倒他的理智。这就是身为雨果·丹纳最大的危险。这一点，他开始明白了，正是他过去一切苦难的解释。这个念头让他温暖、给他鼓舞。从今往后，他的情感与心绪将埋得比最初那天生的谨慎埋下的还要深。他要做一只理智的造物，既做自身奇念的主人，也做那股实现奇念的力量的主人。

===Original===
He lit a fresh cigarette and planned what he would do. On the next night he would prepare himself very carefully. He would eat enormously, provide himself with food and water, rest as much as he could, and then start south and east in a plane. He would drive it far into Germany. When its petrol failed, he would crash it. Stepping from the ruins, he would hasten on in the darkness, on, on, like Pheidippides, till he reached the center of the enemy government. There, crashing through the petty human barriers, he would perform his last feat, strangling the Emperor, slaying the generals, pulling the buildings apart with his Samsonian arms, and disrupting the control of the war.

He had dreamed of such an enterprise even before he had enlisted. But he had known that he lacked sufficient stamina without a great internal cause, and no rage, no blood-madness, was great enough to drive him to that effort. With amazement he realized that a clenched determination depending on the brain rather than the emotions was a greater catalyst than any passion. He knew that he could do such a thing. In the warmth of that knowledge he completed his plan tranquilly and retired. For twelve hours, by order undisturbed, Hugo slept.

In the bright morning, he girded himself. He requisitioned the plane he needed through Major Ingalls. He explained that requirement by saying that he was going to bomb a battery of big guns. The plane offered was an old one. Hugo had seen enough of flying in his French service to understand its navigation. He ate the huge meal he had planned. And then, a cool and grim man, he made his way to the hangar. In fifteen minutes his last adventure would have commenced. But a dispatch rider, charging on to the field in a roaring motor cycle, announced the signing of the Armistice and the end of the war.

Hugo stood near his plane when he heard the news. Two men at his side began to cry, one repeating over and over: “And I’m still alive, so help me God. I wish I was dead, like Joey.” Hugo was rigid. His first gesture was to lift his clenched fist and search for an object to smash with it. The fist lingered in the air. His rage passed﻿—rage that would have required a giant vent had it occurred two days sooner. He relaxed. His arm fell. He ruffled his black hair; his blacker eyes stared and then twinkled. His lips smiled for the first time in many months. His great shoulders sagged. “I should have guessed it,” he said to himself, and entered the rejoicing with a fervour that was unexpected.

===Chinese===
他点上一支新烟，盘算起自己要做的事。第二天夜里，他要周密地准备妥当。他会饱餐一顿，备好食物和水，尽量休息，然后乘一架飞机朝南偏东飞去。他要驾着它深入德国腹地。汽油耗尽时，他就让它坠毁。从残骸里跨出来，他将在黑暗中疾行，走啊，走啊，像菲迪皮德斯一样，直捣敌方政府的中心。在那里，他将撞碎那些渺小的人间藩篱，演出他最后的绝技：扼死德皇，诛杀将军们，用他参孙般的双臂把楼宇拆得四分五裂，搅碎这场战争的操控中枢。

早在参军之前，他就梦想过这样一桩壮举。但他知道，缺了一个伟大的内心动因，他便拿不出足够的毅力，而任何狂怒、任何嗜血的疯魔，都不足以驱使他付出那份气力。此刻他惊异地认识到，一种倚仗头脑而非情感的咬紧牙关的决心，是比任何激情都更强的催化剂。他知道自己做得到这件事。怀着那份认知的暖意，他从容拟完了计划，就寝了。遵着命令，无人打扰，雨果睡了十二个钟头。

晴朗的早晨，他束装停当。他通过英戈尔斯少校调来了自己需要的飞机。他解释这项需求时说，他要去轰炸一座重炮阵地。拨给他的是一架旧飞机。雨果在法军服役时见过的飞行够多，懂得怎么驾驶它。他吃下了计划中的那顿大餐。然后，作为一个冷静而冷峻的人，他朝机棚走去。再过十五分钟，他最后的冒险就要开始了。可是一个传令兵驾着轰鸣的摩托车冲进机场，宣布了停战协定的签署和战争的结束。

听到消息时，雨果正站在自己的飞机旁。身旁两个人哭了起来，一个翻来覆去地念叨：“老天作证，我居然还活着。我倒希望死了，像乔伊那样。”雨果僵立着。他的第一个动作是举起紧攥的拳头，想找个什么东西来砸个粉碎。拳头悬在半空。他的怒气过去了——这怒火若在两天前发作，本需要一场巨大的宣泄。他松了下来。手臂垂落。他揉乱自己的黑发；那双更黑的眼睛瞪着，继而闪出光来。多少个月来，他的嘴唇头一次笑了。他伟岸的双肩松垮下来。“我早该猜到的，”他自言自语，随即以一种自己也未曾料到的热忱，汇入了欢庆的洪流。

## XVI / 第十六章

===Original===
There must be in heaven a certain god﻿—a paunchy, cynical god whose task it is to arrange for each of the birthward-marching souls a set of circumstances so nicely adjusted to its character that the result of its life, in triumph or defeat, will be hinged on the finest of threads. So Hugo must have felt coming home from war. He had celebrated the Armistice hugely, not because it had spared his life﻿—most of the pomp, parade, bawdiness, and glory had originated in such a deliverance﻿—but because it had rescued him from the hot blast of destructiveness. An instantaneous realization of that prevented despair. He had failed in the hour of becoming death itself; such failure was fortunate because life to him, even at the end of the war, seemed more the effort of creation than the business of annihilation.

To know that had cost a struggle﻿—a struggle that took place at the hangar as the dispatch-bearer rode up and that remained crucial only between the instant when he lifted his fist and when he lowered it. Brevity made it no less intense; a second of time had resolved his soul afresh, had redistilled it and recombined it.

Not long after that he started back to America. The mass of soldiers surrounding him were undergoing a transition that Hugo felt vividly. These men would wake up sweating at night and cry out until someone whispered roughly that there were no more submarines. A door would slam and one of them would begin to weep. There were whisperings and bickerings about life at home, about what each person, disintegrated again to individuality, would do and say and think. Little fears about lost jobs and lost girls cropped out, were thrust back, came finally to remain. And no one wanted life to be what it had been; no one considered that it could be the same.

===Chinese===
天上想必住着某位神明——一位大腹便便、愤世冷嘲的神明，他的职司是为每一个朝出生行进的灵魂安排一套与其性情调校得丝丝入扣的境遇，使其一生的结局，无论凯旋还是败亡，都悬系于最纤细的丝线之上。从战争中归来的雨果，想必正是这般滋味。他曾为停战纵情欢庆，倒不是因为它保全了他的性命——那场庆典里的排场、游行、粗鄙的狂欢与荣光，多半正源自这种死里逃生——而是因为它把他从毁灭欲的灼热狂飙里拯救了出来。瞬息间领悟及此，才使他不致坠入绝望。在化身为死亡本身的那一刻，他失败了；这失败是万幸，因为在他看来，纵使到了战争的尽头，生命终究更像创造的劳作，而非毁灭的营生。

悟到这一点并非没有代价——那是一场挣扎，就发生在机棚旁、传令兵疾驰而至的当口，而它之所以性命攸关，也只在举起拳头到放下拳头的那一瞬之间。短暂并未使其剧烈减损分毫；一秒钟的工夫，他的灵魂被重新解析，重新蒸馏，又重新化合。

此后不久，他启程返回美国。身边成群的士兵正经历一场转变，雨果对此感受深切。这些人夜里会一身冷汗地惊醒，喊叫不休，直到有人粗声粗气地低语说，再也没有潜艇了。一扇门砰然关上，其中便会有一个哭出声来。人们交头接耳、争争吵吵，谈的是回家后的生活，谈的是重新散作孤立个人的每一个人，日后要做什么、说什么、想什么。关于丢掉差事、失去姑娘的种种小小恐惧时而冒头，被摁了回去，最终留驻不去。谁也不愿生活照旧；谁也不信它还能照旧。

===Original===
Hugo wrote to his family that the war was ended, that he was well, that he expected to see them some time in the near future. The ship that carried him reached the end of the blue sea; he was disembarked and demobilized in New York. He realized even before he was accustomed to the novelty of civilian clothes that a familiar, friendly city had changed. The retrospective spell of the eighties and nineties had vanished. New York was brand-new, blatant, rushing, prosperous. The inheritance from Europe had been assimilated; a social reality, entirely foreign and American, had been wrought and New York was ready to spread it across the parent world. Those things were pressed quickly into Hugo’s mind by his hotel, the magazines, a chance novel of the precise date, the cinema, and the more general, more indefinite human pulses.

After a few days of random inspection, of casual imbibing, he called upon Tom Shayne’s father. He would have preferred to escape all painful reminiscing, but he went partly as a duty and partly from necessity: he had no money whatever.

===Chinese===
雨果写信告诉家人：战争结束了，他安然无恙，不日即可与他们相见。载他渡海的轮船驶到了蔚蓝大海的尽头；他在纽约登岸，复员除役。还没等他习惯便装带来的新鲜感，他便察觉这座熟稔而亲切的城市已经变了样。十九世纪八九十年代那股怀旧余韵已然消散。纽约焕然一新，喧嚣、匆促、繁荣。得自欧洲的遗产已被消化吸收；一种纯然异质又纯然美国的社会现实已然铸成，而纽约正准备把它播撒到生养它的旧大陆上去。这一切经由他的旅馆、几本杂志、一本恰是当下年月新出的小说、电影院，以及更宽泛、也更朦胧的人心脉动，迅速涌进了雨果的头脑。

随意各处看看、随处浅酌了几日之后，他去拜访了汤姆·谢恩的父亲。他本想躲开一切令人伤怀的追忆，但此行一半出于情分，一半出于必要：他已经身无分文。

===Original===
A butler opened the door of a large stone mansion and ushered Hugo to the library, where Mr. Shayne rose eagerly. “I’m so glad you came. Knew you’d be here soon. How are you?”

Hugo was slightly surprised. In his host’s manner was the hardness and intensity that he had observed everywhere. “I’m very well, thanks.”

“Splendid! Cocktails, Smith.”

There was a pause. Mr. Shayne smiled. “Well, it’s over, eh?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
一位男管家打开石砌大宅的门，引雨果进了书房，谢恩先生急忙起身相迎。「你来了，我真高兴。就知道你快回来了。你好吗？」

雨果略感意外。主人的举止间带着他随处都能察觉的那种生硬与狂热。「很好，谢谢。」

「好极了！上鸡尾酒，史密斯。」

谈话停了片刻。谢恩先生微微一笑。「呃，结束了，嗯？」

「是的。」

===Original===
“All over. And now we’ve got to beat the spears into plowshares, eh?”

“We have.”

Mr. Shayne chuckled. “Some of my spears were already made into plows, and it was a great season for the harvest, young man﻿—a great season.”

===Chinese===
「彻底结束了。如今咱们得把长矛打成犁头了，嗯？」

「是啊。」

谢恩先生呵呵一笑。「我的有些长矛早就打成犁了，而且正赶上收成的大好年景，年轻人——大好年景啊。」

===Original===
Hugo was still uncertain of Mr. Shayne’s deepest viewpoint. His uncertainty nettled him. “The grim reaper has done some harvesting on his own account﻿—” He spoke almost rudely.

Mr. Shayne frowned disapprovingly. “I made up my mind to forget, Danner. To forget and to buckle down. And I’ve done both. You’ll want to know what happened to the funds I handled for you﻿—”

“I wasn’t particularly﻿—”

The older man shook his head with grotesque coyness. “Not so fast, not so fast. You were particularly eager to hear. We’re getting honest about our emotions in this day and place. You’re eaten with impatience. Well﻿—I won’t hold out. Danner, I’ve made you a million. A clean, cold million.”

===Chinese===
雨果仍摸不透谢恩先生最深处的想法。这种捉摸不透让他恼火。「那位冷酷的收割者可也替自己收了一茬——」他说这话时近乎无礼。

谢恩先生不以为然地皱起眉。「我拿定主意要忘掉，丹纳。忘掉，再埋头苦干。这两样我都做到了。你想知道我替你经手的那些款项的下落——」

「我倒不是特别——」

老人摇了摇头，那副忸怩作态近乎怪诞。「别急，别急。你分明急着要听呢。如今这年头、这地方，咱们对自个儿的感情都坦坦荡荡了。你急得心痒难耐。好吧——我不兜圈子了。丹纳，我替你赚了一百万。干干净净、冷冰冰的一百万。」

===Original===
Hugo had been struggling in a rising tide of incomprehension; that statement engulfed him. “Me? A million?”

“In the bank in your name waiting for a blonde girl.”

“I’m afraid I don’t exactly understand, Mr. Shayne.”

===Chinese===
雨果本就在一阵高过一阵的茫然里挣扎；这句话把他整个吞没了。「我？一百万？」

「存在银行里，用的是你的名字，就等哪位金发姑娘来取了。」

「恐怕我还不太明白，谢恩先生。」

===Original===
The banker readjusted his glasses and swallowed a cocktail by tipping back his head. Then he rose, paced across the broad carpet, and faced Hugo. “Of course you don’t understand. Well, I’ll tell you about it. Once you did a favor for me which has no place in this conversation.” He hesitated; his face seemed to flinch and then to be jerked back to its former expression. “In return I’ve done a little for you. And I want to add a word to the gift of your bank book. You have, if you’re careful, leisure to enjoy life, freedom, the world at your feet. No more strife for you, no worry, and no care. Take it. Be a hedonist. There is nothing else. I’ve lain in bed nights enjoying the life that lies ahead of you, my boy. Vicariously voluptuous. Catchy phrase, isn’t it? My own. I want to see you do it up brown.”

===Chinese===
这位银行家扶了扶眼镜，仰头把一杯鸡尾酒一饮而尽。随后他站起身，踱过宽大的地毯，面对雨果站定。「你当然不明白。好吧，我讲给你听。你曾帮过我一个忙——那件事不宜在此谈起。」他迟疑了一下；他的脸似乎瑟缩了一下，随即又被拽回原先的神情。「作为回报，我也为你做了一点事。在奉上你的银行存折之外，我还想再添上一句。只要你处处小心，你便有闲暇去享受人生、享受自由，整个世界都在你脚下。你不必再奋斗，没有忧虑，也无所挂牵。收下吧。做个享乐主义者。除此之外，别无他物。多少个夜晚，我躺在床上，替你品味着铺展在你前方的人生，我的孩子。代人纵欲。这说法够俏皮吧？我的独创。我要看你把日子过得淋漓尽致。」

===Original===
Hugo rubbed his hand across his forehead. It was not long ago that this same man had sat at an estaminet and wept over snatches of a childhood which death had made sacred. Here he stood now, asking that a life be done up brown, and meaning cheap, obvious things. He wished that he had never called on Tom’s father.

“That wasn’t my idea of living﻿—” he said slowly.

“It will be. Forget the war. It was a dream. I realized it suddenly. If I had not, I would still be﻿—just a banker. Not a great banker. The great banker. I saw, suddenly, that it was a dream. The world was mad. So I took my profit from it, beginning on the day I saw.”

===Chinese===
雨果抬手抹了抹前额。就在不久之前，这个人还坐在一家咖啡馆里，为死亡镀上神圣之色的童年断片垂泪。而此刻他站在这里，要求把一段人生也这样「过得淋漓尽致」，指的却是些廉价、浅露的东西。雨果但愿自己从未来拜访汤姆的父亲。

「那不是我理想中的生活——」他缓缓地说。

「往后就会是了。忘掉那场战争。那是一场梦。我是突然明白过来的。若不是悟到了这一点，我至今仍只是——区区一个银行家。当不成大银行家，更当不成那独一无二的大银行家。我突然看清了，那是一场梦。整个世界都疯了。于是我从中取利，从我看清的那一天便开始了。」

===Original===
“How, exactly?”

“Eh?”

“I mean﻿—how did you profit by the war?”

Mr. Shayne smiled expansively. “What was in demand then, my boy? What were the stupid, traduced, misguided people raising billions to get? What? Why, shells, guns, foodstuffs.
