She gathered her legs under herself and ran. Hugo made no attempt to follow her. He merely watched. Twice she tripped and once she fell. At the stone wall she looked back at him. It was not necessary to be able to see her expression. She went on across the fields—a skinny, flapping thing—at last a mere spot of moving color.

===Chinese===
他咽了口唾沫；那几个字、那种憋在喉咙里的语调，他熟悉极了。「我知道。我力气非常大。」

罗珊妮低头望着草地。「把手擦一擦，行吗？」

他在土里搓了搓手。「你千万别害怕。」

「别害怕？」她轻笑了一声。「那我该是什么滋味呢？我还活着，我浑身上下爬满了恐惧。别碰我！」她尖叫着直往后缩。

「我能解释。」

「你什么都能解释！可这个不行。」

「偏偏赶在这个时候，出这种荒唐、狂乱、不讲道理的事。」他说。「我这一生就是这样。」他望向她身后。「我一开头就想做惊天动地的大事。越是尝试，越是灰心。你知道，我力气大。也曾有过别的在意义上如同这头公牛的事。可那些事情本身越来越小、越来越荒诞，因为我的雄心和胆气越缩越小。」他垂下头。「总有一天——我会什么都不想再干了。接二连三、异乎寻常的挫败，也许会激得有些人发狠做出一番大事业。它只是把我拖得疲惫。」他悲伤地抬眼望向她的眼睛。「罗珊妮——！」

她把双腿收拢到身下，爬起来跑了。雨果没有丝毫要去追的意思。他只是望着。她绊了两跤，摔了一跤。到了石墙那儿，她回头看了他一眼。用不着看清她的神情也知道那是什么。她继续穿过田野跑去——一个瘦削的、衣衫翻飞的东西——最后只剩一小点移动的颜色。

===Original===
Hugo turned and stared at the brown mound of the bull. After a moment he walked over and stood above it. Its tongue hung out and its mouth grinned. It lay there dead, and yet to Hugo it still had life: the indestructibility of a ghost and the immortality of a symbol. He sat beside it until sundown.

At twilight he entered the barn and tended the cows. The doors of the house were closed. He went without supper. Cane returned jubilantly later in the evening. He called Hugo from the back porch.

“Telegram for you.”

Hugo read the wire. His father was sick and failing rapidly. “I want my wages,” he said. Then he went back to the barn. His trifling belongings were already wrapped in a bundle. Cane reluctantly counted out the money. Hugo felt nauseated and feverish. He put the money in his pocket, the bundle under his arm; he opened the gate, and his feet found the soft earth of the road in the darkness.

===Chinese===
雨果转过身，凝视着那堆褐色的牛尸。片刻之后，他走过去，站在它上方。它吐着舌头，咧着嘴。它躺在那里死了，然而在雨果眼里它仍然有生命：幽灵般的不灭，象征般的不朽。他在它旁边一直坐到日落。

暮色四合时，他走进谷仓照料奶牛。宅子的门窗都关着。他没有吃晚饭。晚间，凯恩兴高采烈地回来了。他从后门廊把雨果喊了过去。

「有你一封电报。」

雨果读了电文。父亲病了，正迅速衰弱下去。「我要我的工钱。」他说。说完便回谷仓去了。他那点微不足道的行装早已打成一个小包。凯恩不情愿地数出了钱。雨果只觉得恶心，浑身发烫。他把钱放进衣袋，把小包夹在腋下；他推开院门，双足在黑暗中踏上了路面松软的泥土。

## XX / 第二十章

===Original===
Hugo had three hours to wait for a Chicago train. His wages purchased his ticket and left him in possession of twenty dollars. His clothing was nondescript; he had no baggage. He did not go outside the Grand Central Terminal, but sat patiently in the smoking-room, waiting for the time to pass. A guard came up to him and asked to see his ticket. Hugo did not remonstrate and produced it mechanically; he would undoubtedly be mistaken for a tramp amid the sleek travellers and commuters.

When the train started, his fit of perplexed lethargy had not abated. His hands and feet were cold and his heart beat slowly. Life had accustomed him to frustration and to disappointment, yet it was agonizing to assimilate this new cudgeling at the hands of fate. The old green house in the Connecticut hills had been a refuge; Roseanne had been a refuge. They were, both of them, peaceful and whimsical and they had seemed innocent of the capacity for great anguish. Every man dreams of the season-changed countryside as an escape; every man dreams of a woman on whose broad breast he may rest, beneath whose tumbling hair and moth-like hands he may discover forgetfulness and freedom. Some men are successful in a quest for those anodynes. Hugo could understand the sharp contours of one fact: because he was himself, such a quest would always end in failure. No woman lived who could assuage him; his fires would not yield to any temporal powers.

He was barren of desire to investigate deeper into the philosophy of himself. All people turned aside by fate fall into the same morass. Except in his strength, Hugo was pitifully like all people: wounds could easily be opened in his sensitiveness; his moral courage could be taxed to the fringe of dilemma; he looked upon his fellow men sometimes with awe at the variety of high places they attained in spite of the heavy handicap of being human—he looked upon them again with repugnance—and very rarely, as he grew older, did such inspections of his kind include a study of the difference between them and him made by his singular gift. When that thought entered his mind, it gave rise to peculiar speculations.

He approached thirty, he thought, and still the world had not reechoed with his name; the trumps, banners, and cavalcade of his glory had been only shadows in the sky, dust at sunset that made evanescent and intangible colors. Again, he thought, the very perfection of his prowess was responsible for its inapplicability; if he but had an Achilles’ heel so that his might could taste the occasional tonic of inadequacy, then he could meet the challenge of possible failure with successful effort. More frequently he condemned his mind and spirit for not being great enough to conceive a mission for his thews. Then he would fall into a reverie, trying to invent a creation that would be as magnificent as the destructions he could so easily envision.

In such a painful and painstaking mood he was carried over the Alleghenies and out on the Western plains. He changed trains at Chicago without having slept, and all he could remember of the journey was a protracted sorrow, a stabbing consciousness of Roseanne, dulled by his last picture of her, and a hopeless guessing of what she thought about him now.

===Chinese===
雨果要等三个小时才有去芝加哥的火车。工钱买了车票之后，他手里还剩二十美元。他那一身穿戴说不上名目，又没有行李。他没有走出大中央车站，只耐着性子坐在吸烟室里，等时间过去。一个警卫走上前来，要看他的车票。雨果没有争辩，机械地掏了出来；在那些衣着光鲜的旅客和通勤客中间，他无疑会被当成一个流浪汉。

火车开动时，他那一阵迷惘的委顿仍未消退。他手脚冰凉，心跳缓慢。生活已让他惯于挫折与失望，然而命运这一番新添的棒击，吞咽下去仍旧苦不堪言。康涅狄格山中的绿老宅曾是一处避难所；罗珊妮曾是一处避难所。它们俩都是安宁的、随性任情的，而且似乎全然不知大悲大痛为何物。每个男人都梦想着随季换装的乡野，拿它当作逃遁之地；每个男人都梦想着一个女人，可以在她宽阔的胸脯上安歇，在她纷披的长发和飞蛾般轻柔的手下寻得遗忘与自由。有些男人寻到了这类止痛的良药。雨果却能看清一个轮廓分明的事实：因为他就是他自己，这样的寻求注定以失败告终。世上没有一个活着的女人能宽慰他；他那一身烈火，不会向任何尘世的力量低头。

他毫无兴致再深究自己的哲学。凡被命运拨到一旁的人，都掉进同一片泥沼。除了那一身力气，雨果可悲地与常人无异：他的敏感处很容易被撕开伤口；他的道德勇气被一再勒索，也会被逼到两难的边缘；他有时怀着敬畏看他的同类，惊叹他们背着「生而为人」这副沉重枷锁，仍能登上形形色色的高处——有时又满怀憎恶地看他们——而随着年岁渐长，他这样审视同类时，已很少再去琢磨自己的异禀在他们与他之间划出的那道鸿沟了。每当这个念头进入脑海，总会激起种种奇特的玄想。

他年近三十了，他想，世上却还没有哪个角落回荡过他的名字；属于他荣耀的号角、旌旗与仪仗，始终只是天上的幻影，日落时分的浮尘，映出些转瞬即逝、无从把捉的颜色。他又想，正是他神力的尽善尽美，造成了它的无处可用；倘若他也有一个阿喀琉斯之踵，让他的伟力偶尔尝一尝力有不逮的强身之苦，那他便能以成功的努力，去迎击可能到来的失败。而更多的时候，他责怪自己的心智与灵魂不够伟大，想不出一桩配得上这一身筋骨的使命。于是他坠入遐想，试图构想出一件造物，能与他不费吹灰之力便能想见的种种毁灭同样壮丽。

就在这样痛苦而费神的心境里，他被人载着越过阿勒格尼山脉，驶上西部的平原。他在芝加哥换了车，一路不曾合眼；这趟旅程留在他记忆里的，只有一场绵长的悲哀，一阵刺心的对罗珊妮的知觉——已被她最后的身影磨钝了——还有一场无望的猜测：她如今怎样想他。

===Original===
Hugo’s mother met him at the station. She was unaltered, everything was unaltered. The last few instants in the vestibule of the train had been a series of quick remembrances; the whole countryside was like a long-deserted house to which he had returned. The mountains took on a familiar aspect, then the houses, then the dingy red station. Lastly his mother, upright and uncompromisingly grim, dressed in her perpetual mourning of black silk. Her recognition of Hugo produced only the slightest flurry and immediately she became mundane.

“Whatever made you come in those clothes?”

“I was working outdoors, mother. I got right on a train. How is father?”

“Sinking slowly.”

“I’m glad I’m in time.”

===Chinese===
雨果的母亲到车站来接他。她没有变，一切都没有变。在车厢过道里的最后几分钟，往事一连串地闪电般掠过；整个乡野像一座他重新踏进的、荒废已久的屋子。群山先显出熟悉的模样，然后是座座房舍，然后是那座暗红色的旧车站。最后是他的母亲，腰板笔直，冷峻得不留余地，穿着她那身永远不变的黑绸丧服。她认出雨果，只掀起最轻微的一阵悸动，随即便谈起家长里短来。

「你怎么穿着这么一身衣裳回来的？」

「我在外头干力气活来着，妈。我一下就上了火车。爸怎么样？」

「在慢慢沉下去。」

「还好我赶得上，我心里就踏实了。」

===Original===
“It’s God’s will.” She gazed at him. “You’ve changed a little, son.”

“I’m older.” He felt diffident. A vast gulf had risen between this vigorous, religious woman and himself.

She opened a new topic. “Whatever in the world made you send us all that money?”

Hugo smiled. “Why—I didn’t need it, mother. And I thought it would make you and father happy.”

“Perhaps. Perhaps. It has done some good. I’ve sent four missionaries out in the field and I am thinking of sending two more. I had a new addition put on the church, for the drunkards and the fallen. And we put a bathroom in the house. Your father wanted two, but I wouldn’t hear of it.”

===Chinese===
「这是上帝的旨意。」她端详着他。「你变了一点，孩子。」

「我上了年纪了。」他感到局促。这个精力充沛、笃信宗教的女人与他之间，已隔起一道巨大的鸿沟。

她换了个话头。「你究竟为什么给我们寄来那么多钱？」

雨果笑了。「这个——我用不着那些钱，妈。我想着它能让您和爸高兴。」

「也许吧。也许吧。它也确实做了些好事。我派出去了四个传教士，还在想着再派两个。我给教堂加盖了一间新偏屋，专为酒鬼和堕落之人用的。家里也装了一间浴室。你爸要装两间，我可没答应。」

===Original===
“Have you got a car?”

“Car? I couldn’t use one of those inventions of Satan. Your father made me hire this one to meet you. There’s Anna Blake’s house. She married that fellow she was flirting with when you went away. And there’s our house. It was painted last month.”

Now all the years had dropped away and Hugo was a child again, an adolescent again. The car stopped.

“You can go right up. He’s in the front room. I’ll get lunch.”

Hugo’s father was lying on the bed watching the door. A little wizened old man with a big head and thin yellow hands. Illness had made his eyes rheumy, but they lighted up when his son entered, and he half raised himself.

===Chinese===
「家里买车了吗？」

「车？撒旦的那些个发明，我可用不来。是你爸叫我雇了这一辆来接你的。那是安娜·布莱克家的房子。她嫁给了你离家那阵子她一直眉来眼去的那个男人。咱们家到了。上个月刚刷的漆。」

此刻，经年的岁月纷纷剥落，雨果又成了孩子，又成了少年。车停下了。

「你直接上楼去吧。他在前屋。我去弄午饭。」

雨果的父亲躺在床上，望着门口。一个干瘪的小老头，大大的脑袋，一双枯黄瘦削的手。病痛让他的眼睛水汪汪的，但儿子一进门，那双眼便亮了起来，他半支起了身子。

===Original===
“Hello, father.”

“Hugo! You’ve come back.”

“Yes, father.”

“I’ve waited for you. Sit down here on the bed. Move me over a little. Now close the door. Is it cold out? I was afraid you might not get here. I was afraid you might get sick on the train. Old people are like that, Hugo.” He shaded his eyes. “You aren’t a very big man, son. Somehow I always remembered you as big. But—I suppose”—his voice thinned—“I suppose you don’t want to talk about yourself.”

“Anything you want to hear, father.”

“I can’t believe you came back.” He ruminated. “There were a thousand things I wanted to ask you, son—but they’ve all gone from my mind. I’m not so easy in your presence as I was when you were a little shaver.”

===Chinese===
「你好，爸。」

「雨果！你回来了。」

「是的，爸。」

「我一直等着你。坐到床边上来。把我往里挪一挪。把门关上。外头冷吗？我怕你赶不到。我怕你在火车上病倒。人老了就是这个样子，雨果。」他用手遮着眼睛。「你个子并不怎么高大啊，孩子。不知怎么，我一直记着你高高大大的。可是——我想」——他的声音细了下去——「我想你是不愿意谈你自己的。」

「您想听什么，我就讲什么，爸。」

「我真不敢相信你回来了。」他咀嚼着往事。「我本来有一千件事要问你，孩子——可它们全都从脑子里溜走了。你在我跟前，我不像你还是个小毛头那时候那么自在了。」

===Original===
Hugo knew what those questions would be. Here, on his deathbed, his father was still a scientist. His soul flinched from giving its account. He saw suddenly that he could never tell his father the truth; pity, kindredship, kindness, moved him. “I know what you wanted to ask, father. Am I still strong?” It took courage to suggest that. But he was rewarded. The old man sighed ecstatically. “That’s it, Hugo, my son.”

“Then—father, I am. I grew constantly stronger when I left you. In college I was strong. At sea I was strong. In the war. First I wanted to be mighty in games and I was. Then I wanted to do services. And I did, because I could.”

The head nodded on its feeble neck. “You found things to do? I—I hoped you would. But I always worried about you. Every day, son, every day for all these years, I picked up the papers and looked at them with misgivings. ‘Suppose,’ I said to myself, ‘suppose my boy lost his temper last night. Suppose someone wronged him and he undertook to avenge himself.’ I trusted you, Hugo. I could not quite trust—the other thing. I’ve even blamed myself and hated myself.” He smiled. “But it’s all right—all right. So I am glad. Then, tell me—what—what—”

“What have I done?”

“Do you mind? It’s been so long and you were so far away.”

“Well—” Hugo swept his memory back over his career—“so many things, father. It’s hard to recite one’s own—”

===Chinese===
雨果知道那些问题会是什么。在这里，在死亡的床边，他的父亲仍然是一个科学家。他的灵魂瑟缩着，不敢交出自己的账目。他蓦然明白，他永远无法对父亲说出真相；怜悯、骨肉之情、慈爱，一齐打动了他。「我知道您想问什么，爸。我是不是还强壮？」主动提起这一句，是需要勇气的。但他得到了报偿。老人喜极而叹。「正是，雨果，我的儿子。」

「那么——爸，我还强壮。离开您以后，我一刻不停地越长越壮。上大学时我强壮。在海上我强壮。在战场上也一样。起初我想在竞技里称雄，我做到了。后来我想做些有益的事。我也做了，因为我有这个能力。」

那颗头颅在孱弱的脖颈上点动着。「你找到了可做的事？我——我一直盼着你能。可我总为你提心吊胆。每一天，孩子，这些年来的每一天，我拿起报纸，都是满腹疑惧地翻看。『万一，』我对自己说，『万一我的孩子昨夜动了怒。万一有人亏待了他，他要自己去讨还。』我信得过你，雨果。我却不能全然信得过——那样东西。我甚至责备过自己，恨过自己。」他笑了。「可现在都好了——都好了。我太高兴了。那么，告诉我——你做了——做了什么？」

「我做了什么？」

「你愿意讲讲吗？毕竟过了这么久，你又去了那么远的地方。」

「好吧——」雨果的记忆扫回了他这一生的经历——「太多了，爸。一个人很难细数自己的——」

===Original===
“I know. But I’m your father, and my ears ache to hear.”

“I saved a man pinned under a wagon. I saved a man from a shark. I pulled open a safe in which a man was smothering. Many things like that. Then—there was the war.”

“I know. I know. When you wrote that you had gone to war, I was frightened—and happy. Try as I might, I could not think of a great constructive cause for you to enter. I had to satisfy myself by thinking that you could find such a cause. Then the war came. And you wrote that you were in it. I was happy. I am old, Hugo, and perhaps my nationalism and my patriotism are dead. Sides in a war did not seem to matter. But peace mattered to me, and I thought—I hoped that you could hasten peace. Four years, Hugo. Your letters said nothing. Four years. And then it stopped. And I understood. War is property fighting property, not David fighting Goliath. The greatest David would be unavailing now. Even you could do little enough.”

“Perhaps not so little, father.”

“There were things, then?”

===Chinese===
「我知道。可我是你的父亲，我的耳朵都盼疼了。」

「我救过一个被货车压住的人。我从鲨鱼口中救过一个人。我掰开过一间把人闷得半死的保险库。诸如此类，还有很多。再后来——就是那场战争。」

「我知道。我知道。你写信来说你从了军，我又害怕——又高兴。我千方百计地想，也想不出一桩你可以投身的大有建树的事业。我只好这样宽慰自己：你自会找到那样的事业。后来战争来了。你写信说你在其中。我高兴。我老了，雨果，也许我的民族主义和爱国主义都已死灭。战争里站在哪一边，在我看来已无关紧要。要紧的是和平，我想着——我盼着你能催得和平早一天到来。四年啊，雨果。你的信里只字不提。四年。后来就断了。我明白了。战争是财产权在和财产权厮杀，不是大卫对阵歌利亚。如今即便是最伟大的大卫，也无济于事。连你能做的，也实在有限。」

「也许并没有那么有限，爸。」

「这么说，你真做了些事？」

===Original===
Hugo could not disappoint his father with the whole formidable truth. “Yes.” He lied with a steady gaze. “I stopped the war.”

“You!”

“After four years I perceived the truth of what you have just said. War is a mistake. It is not sides that matter. The object of war is to make peace. On a dark night, father, I went alone into the enemy lines. For one hundred miles that night I upset every gun, I wrecked every ammunition train, I blew up every dump—every arsenal, that is. Alone I did it. The next day they asked for peace. Remember the false armistice? Somehow it leaked out that there would be victory and surrender the next night—because of me. Only the truth about me was never known. And a day later—it came.”

The weak old man was transported. He raised himself up on his elbows. “You did that! Then all my work was not in vain. My dream and my prayer were justified! Oh, Hugo, you can never know how glad I am you came and told me this. How glad.”

He repeated his expression of joy until his tongue was weary; then he fell back. Hugo sat with shining eyes during the silence that followed. His father at length groped for a glass of water. Strength returned to him. “I could ask for no more, son. And yet we are petulant, insatiable creatures. What is doing now? The world is wicked. Yet it tries half-heartedly to rebuild itself. One great deed is not enough—or are you tired?”

===Chinese===
雨果不忍心用全部骇人的真相，让父亲失望。「是的。」他目光镇定地说了谎。「我结束了那场战争。」

「你！」

「四年之后，我悟出了您方才说的道理。战争是个错误。要紧的不是站在哪一边。战争的目的，本是缔造和平。在一个漆黑的夜里，爸，我独自进了敌军的防线。那一夜，在一百英里的地段上，我掀翻了每一门大炮，捣毁了每一列军火火车，炸飞了每一处弹药堆积场——确切地说，是每一座军械库。是我一个人干的。第二天他们便求和了。还记得那场假停战吗？不知怎么走漏了风声，说第二天夜里就会胜利、就会投降——因为我。只是关于我的真相，从没有人知道。又过了一天——真正的停战来了。」

衰弱的老人欣喜若狂。他用双肘撑起了身子。「那是你干的！那么我毕生的心血没有白费。我的梦和我的祈祷都得了报偿！啊，雨果，你永远不会知道，你回来把这件事讲给我听，我是多么高兴。多么高兴。」

他把那份欢喜翻来覆去地说，直到舌敝唇焦，才重新躺下。随后的沉默里，雨果坐在一旁，双眼发亮。父亲终于伸手去够一杯水。气力又回到了他身上。「我本可以别无所求了，孩子。可我们终究是些任性而不知餍足的造物。如今你在做什么？这个世界邪恶横行。它却也三心二意地想重建自身。一件伟业是不够的——或者说，你累了吗？」

===Original===
Hugo smiled. “Am I ever tired, father? Am I vulnerable?”

“I had forgotten. It is so hard for the finite mind to think beyond itself. Not tired. Not vulnerable. No. There was Samson—the cat.” He was embarrassed. “I hurt you?”

“No, father.” He repeated it. Every gentle fall of the word “father” from his lips and every mention of “son” by his father was rare privilege, unfamiliar elixir to the old man. His new lie took its cue from Abednego Danner’s expressions. “My work goes on. Now it is with America. I expect to go to Washington soon to right the wrongs of politics and government. Vicious and selfish men I shall force from their high places. I shall secure the idealistic and the courageous.” It was a theory he had never considered, a possible practice born of necessity. “The pressure I shall bring against them will be physical and mental. Here a man will be driven from his house mysteriously. There a man will slip into the limbo. Yonder an inconspicuous person will suddenly be braced by a new courage; his enemies will be gone and his work will progress unhampered. I shall be an invisible agent of right—right as best I can see it. You understand, father?”

Abednego smiled like a happy child. “I do, son. To be you must be splendid.”

“The most splendid thing on earth! And I have you to thank, you and your genius to tender gratitude to. I am merely the agent. It is you that created and the whole world that benefits.”

===Chinese===
雨果笑了。「我几时累过，爸？我会受伤害吗？」

「我倒忘了。有限的心灵，总难想到自身以外去。不会累。不会受伤害。是啊。不过还有参孙——那只猫。」他有些窘迫。「叫你难过了？」

「没有，爸。」他又说了一遍。他口中每一声轻唤的「爸」，父亲口中的每一声「孩子」，对这个老人都是难得的恩宠，是一剂他不曾品尝过的灵药。他新的谎言，正是顺着亚伯尼歌·丹纳的话头编下去的。「我的事业还在继续。如今轮到美国了。我不日就要去华盛顿，匡正政治与政府中的种种不义。狠毒而自私的人，我要把他们从高位上赶下去。心怀理想、勇气充沛的人，我要保全他们。」这是一套他从未想过的理论，一场因需要而生的、可能的实践。「我要施于他们身上的压力，将是肉体的和精神的双重压力。这儿，会有一个人莫名其妙地被逐出家门。那儿，会有一个人无声无息地湮没无踪。再远一点，某个不起眼的小人物会忽然被一股新的勇气撑起来；他的仇人消失了，他的事业再无阻拦地向前推进。我将成为正义的隐形使者——尽我所能看见的正义。您明白吗，爸？」

亚伯尼歌笑得像个幸福的孩子。「我明白，孩子。做你这样的人，必定妙极了。」

「天底下最妙的事！而这正该感激你，要向你、向你的天才致谢。我不过是个执行者。创造的是你，受益的是整个世界。」

===Original===
Abednego’s face was serene—not smug, but transfigured. “I yearned as you now perform. It is strange that one cloistered mortal can become inspired with the toil and lament of the universe. Yet there is a danger of false pride in that, too. I am apt to fall into the pit because my cup is so full here at the last. And the greatest problem of all is not settled.”

“What problem?” Hugo asked in surprise.

“Why, the problem that up until now has been with me day and night. Shall there be made more men like you—and women like you?”

===Chinese===
亚伯尼歌的面容安详——不是沾沾自喜，而是焕然改容。「我曾那样渴求，正如你如今那般施行。一个离群索居的凡人，竟能为这宇宙的劳苦与哀恸所感召，真是奇事。可这里面也潜着虚妄自尊的危险。到了这最后的关头，我的杯盏盛得太满，我自己反倒容易跌进那个坑里去。何况，最重大的那个问题，还没有解决。」

「什么问题？」雨果诧异地问。

「就是那个至今还日夜萦绕在我心头的问题啊。世上该不该再造出更多像你一样的人——还有像你一样的女人？」

===Original===
The idea staggered Hugo. It paralyzed him and he heard his father’s voice come from a great distance. “Up in the attic in the black trunk are six notebooks wrapped in oilpaper. They were written in pencil, but I went over them carefully in ink. That is my lifework, Hugo. It is the secret—of you. Given those books, a good laboratory worker could go through all my experiments and repeat each with the same success. I tried a little myself. I found out things—for example, the effect of the process is not inherited by the future generations. It must be done over each time. It has seemed to me that those six little books—you could slip them all into your coat pocket—are a terrible explosive. They can rip the world apart and wipe humanity from it. In malicious hands they would end life. Sometimes, when I became nervous waiting for the newspapers, waiting for a letter from you, I have been sorely tempted to destroy them. But now—”

“Now?” Hugo echoed huskily.

“Now I understand. There is no better keeping for them than your own. I give them to you.”

“Me!”

“You, son. You must take them, and the burden must be yours. You have grown to manhood now and I am proud of you. More than proud. If I were not, I myself would destroy the books here on this bed. Matilda would bring them and I would watch them burn so that the danger would go with—” he cleared his throat—“my dream.”

===Chinese===
这个念头把雨果击懵了。他浑身瘫软，听着父亲的声音从极远的地方传来。「阁楼上那只黑箱子里，有六本用油纸包着的笔记。原先是用铅笔写的，后来我又用钢笔仔细誊了一遍。那是我的毕生事业，雨果。那是——你的秘密。有了那几本书，一个合格的实验室人员，就能重做我全部的实验，而且每一个都能同样成功。我自己也稍作过尝试。我弄明白了一些事——比如，那套流程的效果不会遗传给后代。每一次都必须从头再做。我常常觉得，那六本小书——把它们全塞进你的外套口袋都绰绰有余——是一件可怕的炸药。它们能把世界炸得四分五裂，能把人类从世界上抹去。落在歹毒的人手里，它们会终结生命。有些时候，我等报纸等得心神不宁、等你的信等得心焦，曾一次次狠了心，想毁掉它们。可是如今——」

「如今怎样？」雨果嗓音沙哑地应了一声。

「如今我明白了。天下再没有比你更稳妥的保管者了。我把它们交给你。」

「给我！」

「给你，孩子。你必须收下它们，这副担子必须由你来挑。你已长大成人，我为你骄傲。何止骄傲。倘若我不是这样，我就要在这张床上亲手毁掉那几本书。让玛蒂尔达拿来，我亲眼看它们烧成灰，好让那危险随同——」他清了清嗓子「——随同我的梦一起去了。」

===Original===
“But—”

“You cannot deny me. It is my wish. You can see what it means. A world grown suddenly—as you are.”

“I, father—”

“You have not avoided responsibility. You will not avoid this, the greatest of your responsibilities. Since the days when I made those notes—what days!—biology has made great strides. For a time I was anxious. For a time I thought that my research might be rediscovered. But it cannot be. Theory has swung in a different direction.” He smiled with inner amusement. “The opticians have decided that the microscope I made is impossible. The biochemists, moving through the secretions of such things as hippuric acid in the epithelial cells, to enzymes, to hormones, to chromosomes, have put a false construction on everything. It will take hundreds, thousands of years to see the light. The darkness is so intense and the error so plausible that they may never see again exactly as I saw. The fact of you, at best, may remain always no more than a theory. This is not vanity. My findings were a combination of accidents almost outside the bounds of mathematical probability. It is you who must bear the light.”

===Chinese===
「可是——」

「你不能拒绝我。这是我的心愿。你明白这意味着什么。一个人人突然间变得——和你一样的世界。」

「我，爸——」

「你从不曾逃避责任。你也不会逃避这一件——你所有责任中最重大的一件。自从我写下那些笔记——那是些什么样的日子啊！——生物学已长足迈进。有一阵我很忧虑。有一阵我以为，我的研究说不定会被别人重新发现。但不会的。理论的潮流，已拐向了另一个方向。」他心里暗觉有趣地笑了。「光学师傅们已经断定，我造的那台显微镜是不可能存在的。生物化学家们，从上皮细胞里马尿酸一类的分泌物，研究到酶，研究到激素，研究到染色体，把一切都解释错了。要重见天日，还得上百年、上千年。这黑暗如此浓重，这谬误又如此貌似有理，他们或许永远再见不到我当年所见的确切真相了。你的存在，往好处说，也许永远不过是一个理论。这并非自负。我的种种发现，是一连串偶然的凑合，几乎越出了数学概率的边界。这盏灯，必须由你来擎。」

===Original===
Hugo felt that now, indeed, circumstance had closed around him and left him without succour or recourse. He bowed his head. “I will do it, father.”

“Now I can die in peace—in joy.”

With an almost visible wrench Hugo brought himself back to his surroundings. “Nonsense, father. You’ll probably get well.”

“No, son. I’ve studied the progress of this disease in the lower orders—when I saw it imminent. I shall die—not in pain, but in sleep. But I shall not be dead—because of you.” He held out his hand for Hugo.

===Chinese===
雨果感到，此刻，境遇当真已四面合围，把他困在其中，无援无助，无路可退。他低下了头。「我会做到的，爸。」

「这下我能安宁地死去了——欢欢喜喜地死去。」

雨果几乎是费了一股看得见的力气，才把自己拽回到眼前的环境中来。「胡说，爸。您多半会好起来的。」

「不会的，孩子。这种病在低等动物身上的进程，我研究过——在我看出它将至的时候。我会死去——没有痛苦，就在睡梦里。但我并不算真正死去——因为你还在。」他向雨果伸出了手。

===Original===
Some time later the old professor fell asleep and Hugo tiptoed from the room. Food was sizzling downstairs in the kitchen, but he ignored it, going out into the sharp air by the front door. He hastened along the streets and soon came to the road that led up the mountain. He climbed rapidly, and when he dared, he discarded the tedious little steps of all mankind. He reached the side of the quarry where he had built the stone fort, and seated himself on a ledge that hung over it. Trees, creepers, and underbrush had grown over the place, but through the October-stripped barricade of their branches he could see a heap of stones that was his dolmen, on which the hieroglyph of him was inscribed.

Two tears scalded his cheeks; he trembled with the welter of his emotions. He had failed his father, failed his trust, failed the world; and in the abyss of that grief he could catch no sight of promise or hope. Having done his best, he had still done nothing, and it was necessary for him to lie to put the thoughts of a dying man to rest. The pity of that lie! The folly of the picture he had painted of himself—Hugo Danner the scourge of God, Hugo Danner the destroying angel, Hugo Danner the hero of a quick love-affair that turned brown and dead like a plucked flower, the sentimental soldier, the involuntary misanthrope.

“I must do it!” he whispered fiercely. The ruined stones echoed the sound of his voice with a remote demoniac jeer. Do what? What, strong man? What?

===Chinese===
过了一阵，老教授睡着了，雨果蹑手蹑脚地退出房间。楼下厨房里饭菜正滋滋作响，他却不理会，从前门走进凛冽的空气里。他沿着街道疾行，很快踏上了通往山顶的那条路。他飞快地攀登，一到无人看见的地方，便抛开全人类那种磨磨蹭蹭的碎步。他到了当年筑石堡的那座采石场旁，在一个悬在坑口的岩檐上坐下。树木、藤蔓和杂树丛早已爬满了那地方，但透过十月里枝叶落尽的屏障，他能望见一小堆石头——他的石棚，上面镌刻着属于他的那道象形符号。

两行热泪烫着他的双颊；他在翻腾的情感中浑身发抖。他辜负了父亲，辜负了父亲的重托，辜负了这个世界；在那悲伤的深渊里，他望不见一丝许诺与希望。他已竭尽全力，却依然一事无成，还得靠撒谎来安顿一个垂死之人的心事。那谎言多么可哀！他为自己描画的那幅画像又是多么荒唐——雨果·丹纳，上帝之鞭；雨果·丹纳，毁灭天使；雨果·丹纳，一段速起速灭的爱情的主角，那爱情像一朵摘下的花，枯黄而死；一个多愁善感的士兵，一个身不由己的厌世者。

「我必须做到！」他狠狠地低声说。残破的石头用一声遥远的、魔鬼般的嘲弄，回响着他的声音。做什么？做什么，大力士？做什么？

## XXI / 第二十一章

===Original===
Now the winds keened from the mountains, and snow fell. Abednego Danner, the magnificent Abednego Danner, was carried to his last resting-place, the laboratory of nature herself. His wife and his son followed the bier; the dirge was intoned, the meaningless cadence of ritual was spoken to the cold ground; a ghostly obelisk was lifted up over his meagre remains. Hugo had a wish to go to the hills and roll down some gigantic chunk of living rock to mark that place until the coming of a glacier, but he forbore and followed all the dark conventions of disintegration.
The will was read and the bulk of Hugo’s sorry gains was thrust back into his keeping. He went into the attic and opened the black trunk where the six small notebooks lay in oilpaper. He took them out and unwrapped them. The first two books were a maze of numbered experiments. In the third a more vigorous calligraphy, a quivering tracery of excitement, marked the repressed beginning of a new earth.
He bought a bag and some clothes and packed; the false contralto of his mother’s hymns as she went about the house filled him with such despair that he left after the minimum interval allowed by filial decency. She was a grim old woman still, one to whom the coming of the kingdom to Africa was a passion, the polishing of the coal stove a duty, and the presence of her unfamiliar son a burden.

===Chinese===
如今，风从山间呼啸悲鸣，雪落了下来。亚伯尼歌·丹纳，气宇不凡的亚伯尼歌·丹纳，被抬到了他最后的安息之地——大自然她本人的实验室。妻子和儿子随柩送行；挽歌低诵，那套毫无意义的仪文腔调对着冰冷的土地念了一遍；一座惨白的方尖碑，竖立在他那点微薄的遗骸之上。雨果一度想去山里，推下一大块仍有生命的巨岩来标记那个地方，直到冰川纪再度降临，但他忍住了，照旧奉行了肉身归于朽坏的一切阴沉惯例。
遗嘱宣读了，雨果那笔寒酸所得的大头又塞回他自己手里。他上到阁楼，打开那口黑色的大衣箱，六本小笔记簿正躺在油纸里。他把它们取出来，一一解开。头两本是一堆编了号的实验，密如迷宫。第三本上，一种更为遒劲的笔迹、一阵激动得发颤的线条，标记出一个新天地被压抑的开端。
他买了一只提包和几件衣裳，收拾好行李；母亲在屋里来来去去唱着圣歌，那假嗓的女低音灌得他满心绝望，于是挨到孝道体面所允许的最短期限一满，他便离家了。她依旧是个冷峻的老妇人：对她来说，让天国降临非洲是一腔热望，把煤炉擦得锃亮是一桩本分，而这个陌生的儿子待在家里是一副担子。

===Original===
When he said goodbye, he kissed her, which left her standing on the station platform looking at the train with a flat, uncomprehending expression. Hugo knew where he was going and why: he was on his way to Washington. The great crusade was to begin. He had no plans, only ideals, which are plans of a sort. He had told his father he was making the world a better place, and the idea had taken hold of him. He would grapple the world, his world, at its source; he would no longer attempt to rise from a lowly place; he would exert his power in the highest places; government, politics, law, were malleable to the force of one man.
Most of his illusion was gone. As he had said so glibly to his father, there were good men and corrupt in the important situations in the world; to the good he would lend his strength, to the corrupt he would exhibit his embattled antipathy. He would be not one impotent person seeking to dominate, but the agent of uplift. He would be what he perceived life had meant him to be: an instrument. He could not be a leader, but he could create a leader.
Such was his intention; he had seen a new way to reform the world, and if his inspiration was clouded occasionally with doubt, he disavowed the doubts as a Christian disavows temptation. This was to be his magnificent gesture; he closed his eyes to the inferences made by his past.

===Chinese===
道别的时候，他吻了她；这一吻过后，她站在站台上，用一种平平的、全然不解的神情望着火车。雨果知道自己要去哪里、为什么去：他正在去华盛顿的路上。伟大的远征就要开始了。他没有计划，只有理想——理想也算是一种计划。他对父亲说过，他要让世界变得更美好，这个念头已经攥住了他。他要在他这个世界的源头处扼住它；他不再企图从卑微处往上攀；他要在最高的地方施展力量；政府、政治、法律，在一个人的力量面前都是可揉可塑之物。
他的幻梦大半已经褪去。正如他对父亲信口说过的那样，世界上各处要津有好人也有腐化的人；对好人，他愿出借自己的力量；对腐化的人，他要摆出严阵以待的敌意。他不做一个渴望支配他人的无能之辈，而要做推动世道向上的代理人。他要做他认定生命本意要他做的人：一件工具。他做不成领袖，但他可以造就一个领袖。
这便是他的打算；他看到了一条改造世界的新路，即便灵感偶尔被疑虑蒙上阴影，他也像基督徒否认试探那样否认那些疑虑。这将是他豪迈的一搏；对他的过去所引出的种种推论，他闭上了眼睛。

===Original===
He never thought of himself as pathetic or quixotic; his ability to measure up to external requirements was infinite; his disappointment lay always (he thought) in his spirit and his intelligence. He went to Washington: the world was pivoting there.
His first few weeks were dull. He installed himself in a pleasant house and hired two servants. The use to which he was putting his funds compensated for their origin. It was men like Shayne who would suffer from his mission. And such a man came into view before very long.
Hugo interested himself in politics and the appearance of politics. He read the *Congressional Record*, he talked with everyone he met, he went daily to the Capitol and listened to the amazing pattern of harangue from the lips of innumerable statesmen. In looking for a cause his eye fell naturally on the problem of disarmament. Hugo saw at once that it was a great cause and that it was bogged in the greed of individuals. It is not difficult to become politically partisan in the Capitol of any nation. It was patent to Hugo that disarmament meant a removal of the chance for war; Hugo hated war. He moved hither and thither, making friends, learning, entertaining, never exposing his plan—which his new friends thought to be lobbying for some impending legislation.

===Chinese===
他从不觉得自己可悲，也不觉得自己堂吉诃德；他满足外在要求的能力是无穷无尽的；他的失意（他以为）始终在心气与才智上。他去了华盛顿：世界正以那里为枢轴转动。
最初几个星期颇为沉闷。他住进一栋舒适的宅子，雇了两个仆人。他把这笔钱派上的用场，抵消了这笔钱的来路。因他这场使命而破财的，正是谢恩那样的人。这样一个人，不久便进入了他的视野。
雨果对政治、也对政治的表象发生了兴趣。他读《国会议事录》，逢人便攀谈，每天去国会大厦，听无数政治家唇间吐出的那令人惊叹的宏论套路。在寻找一项事业时，他的目光自然而然落在了裁军问题上。雨果立刻看出，这是一项伟大的事业，而且它正陷在个人的贪欲里拔不出脚。在任何国家的国会里，都很容易沾上党派之见。对雨果来说不言自明的是：裁军意味着铲除战争的契机；雨果恨战争。他四处走动，交友、见识、宴饮，从不暴露自己的计划——他的新朋友们以为，那不过是为某项即将提出的法案四处游说。

===Original===
He picked out an individual readily enough. Some of the men he had come to know were in the Senate, others in the House of Representatives, others were diplomats, newspaper reporters, attachés. Each alliance had been cemented with care and purpose. His knowledge of an enemy came by whisperings, by hints, by plain statements.
Congressman Hatten, who argued so eloquently for laying down arms and picking up the cause of humanity, was a guest of Hugo’s.
“Danner,” he said, after a third highball, “you’re a sensible chap. But you don’t quite get us. I’m fighting for disarmament—”
“And making a grand fight—”

===Chinese===
他不费什么力气就挑中了一个对象。他结识的人里，有的在参议院，有的在众议院，有的是外交官、报纸记者、随员。每一段结交都经过细心而有的放矢的巩固。他对某一个敌人的了解，得自耳语，得自暗示，得自直截了当的陈述。
哈滕众议员为放下武器、担起人道的事业而慷慨陈词，他是雨果座上的客人。
「丹纳，」第三杯高球酒下肚后，他说，「你是个明白人。可你不太看得懂我们。我为裁军而战——」
「而且打了一场漂亮仗——」

===Original===
The Congressman waved his hand. “Sure. That’s what I mean. You really want this thing for itself. But, between you and me, I don’t give a rap about ships and guns. My district is a farm district. We aren’t interested in paying millions in taxes to the bosses and owners in a coal and iron community. So I’m against it. Dead against it—with my constituency behind me. Nobody really wants to spend the money except the shipbuilders and steel men. Maybe they don’t, theoretically. But the money in it is too big. That’s why I fight.”
“And your speeches?”
“Pap, Danner, pure pap. Even the yokels in my home towns realize that.”
“It doesn’t seem like pap to me.”

===Chinese===
众议员摆了摆手。「那当然。我就是这个意思。你是真心要办成这件事本身。可是，咱们私下说说，船啊炮啊，我半个子儿也不在乎。我那个选区是农业选区。我们犯不着交上几百万的税，去贴补煤炭钢铁那伙人的老总和业主。所以我反对。坚决反对——有我的选民给我撑腰呢。除了造船商和钢铁商，没有谁真愿意掏这笔钱。兴许连他们也不是真愿意，从理论上说。可这里头的钱太大啦。所以我才斗。」
「那你的那些演说呢？」
「哄小孩的奶话，丹纳，纯粹的奶话。连我家乡镇子上的乡巴佬都听得出来。」
「我听着倒不像是奶话。」

===Original===
“That’s politics. In a way it isn’t. Two boys I was fond of are lying over there in France. I don’t want to make any more shells. But I have to think of something else first. If I came from some other district, the case would be reversed. I’d like to change the tariff. But the industrials oppose me in that. So we compromise. Or we don’t. I think I could put across a decent arms-limitation bill right now, for example, if I could get Willard Melcher out of town for a month.”
“Melcher?”
“You know him, of course—at least, who he is. He spends the steel money here in Washington—to keep the building program going on. Simple thing to do. The Navy helps him. Tell the public about the Japanese menace, the English menace, all the other menaces, and the public coughs up for bigger guns and better ships. Run ’em till they rust and nobody ever really knows what good they could do.”

===Chinese===
「这就是政治。可也不全是。我有两个疼爱的孩子，躺在法国那边的土里。我不想再铸炮弹了。可我得先顾别的。我要是出身别的选区，情况就全倒过来了。我还想改关税来着。可实业界在那上头跟我作对。所以我们就妥协。要么就僵着。比方说，眼下我大概就能通过一项像样的军备限制法案——只要我能让威拉德·梅尔彻出城一个月。」
「梅尔彻？」
「你总该认识他——至少知道他是谁。他在华盛顿替钢铁业花钱——好让造舰计划一直办下去。这事儿简单。海军给他帮腔。跟公众念叨念叨日本威胁、英国威胁，还有什么什么威胁，公众就掏钱买更大的炮、更好的船。把船开到生锈报废，也没人真正弄明白过它们究竟顶什么用。」

===Original===
“And Melcher does that?”
The Congressman chuckled. “His payroll would make your eyes bulge. But you can’t touch him.”
Hugo nodded thoughtfully. “Don’t you think anyone around here works purely for an idea?”
“How’s that? Oh—I understand. Sure. The cranks!” And his laughter ended the discussion.

===Chinese===
「干这些的就是梅尔彻？」
众议员咯咯一笑。「他那份收买人心的开支单子，能让你眼珠子鼓出来。可你动不了他。」
雨果若有所思地点点头。「这儿就没有一个人，是纯粹为一个理念干事的？」
「怎么说？哦——我明白了。有啊。那些怪人！」他的笑声给这场谈话画上了句号。

===Original===
Hugo began. He walked up the brick steps of Melcher’s residence and pulled the glittering brass knob. A servant came to the door.
“Mr. Danner to see Mr. Melcher. Just a moment.”
A wait in the hall. The servant returned. “Sorry, but he’s not in.”
Hugo’s mouth was firm. “Please tell him that I saw him come in.”

===Chinese===
雨果动手了。他走上梅尔彻宅邸的砖阶，拉了拉那颗亮闪闪的黄铜门钮。一个仆人应声来到门前。
「丹纳先生求见梅尔彻先生。请稍候。」
在门厅里等了一阵。仆人回来说：「对不起，他不在。」
雨果的嘴角绷紧了。「请告诉他，我亲眼看见他进去了。」

===Original===
“I’m sorry, sir, but he is going right out.”
“Tell him—that he will see me.”
The servant raised his voice. “Harry!” A heavy person with a flattened nose and cauliflower ears stepped into the hall. “This gentleman wishes to see Mr. Melcher, and Mr. Melcher is not in—to him. Take care of him, Harry.” The servant withdrew.
“Run along, fellow.”

===Chinese===
「很抱歉，先生，他马上就要出门。」
「告诉他——说他会见我。」
仆人提高了嗓门。「哈里！」一个塌鼻梁、菜花耳的大块头跨进门厅。「这位先生要见梅尔彻先生，可梅尔彻先生不在——不接待他。你来打发他，哈里。」仆人退了下去。
「走吧，小子。」

===Original===
Hugo smiled. “Mr. Melcher keeps a bouncer?”
An evil light flickered in the other’s eyes. “Yeah, fellow. And I came up from the Pennsy mines. I’m a tough guy, so beat it.”
“Not so tough your ears and nose aren’t a sight,” Hugo said lightly.
The man advanced. His voice was throaty. “Git!”

===Chinese===
雨果微微一笑。「梅尔彻先生还养着个看门的打手？」
对方眼里闪过一道凶光。「是吗，小子。老子还是从宾州矿井里爬上来的呢。老子是条硬汉，滚吧。」
「硬汉硬得连耳朵鼻子都改了模样。」雨果轻飘飘地说。
那人逼上前来，嗓音闷在喉咙里。「滚！」

===Original===
“You go to the devil. I came here to see Melcher and I’m going to see him.”
“Yeah?”
The tough one drew back his fist, but he never understood afterwards what had taken place. He came to in the kitchen an hour later. Mr. Melcher heard him rumble to the floor and emerged from the library. He was a huge man, bigger than his bouncer; his face was hard and sinister and it lighted with an unpleasant smile when he saw the unconscious thug and measured the size of Hugo. “Pulled a fast one on Harry, eh?”

===Chinese===
「你见鬼去吧。我来见梅尔彻，就非见到不可。」
「是吗？」
那条硬汉缩回了拳头，可他事后始终没弄明白出了什么事。一个钟头之后，他才在厨房里醒过来。梅尔彻先生听见他轰隆一声栽倒在地，从书房里走了出来。他是个彪形大汉，比他的打手还大一号；那张脸又硬又阴鸷，看见不省人事的恶棍，又打量了一番雨果的块头，脸上便亮起一个不怀好意的笑。「在哈里身上耍了个花招，嗯？」

===Original===
“I came to see you, Melcher.”
“Well, might as well come in now. I worked up from the mines myself, and I’m a hard egg. If you got funny with me, you’d get killed. Wha’ daya want?”
Hugo sat down in a leather chair and lit a cigarette. He was comparatively without emotion. This was his appointed task and he would make short shrift of it. “I came here, Melcher,” he began, “to talk about your part in the arms conferences. It happens that I disagree with you and your propaganda. It happens that I have a method of enforcing my opinion. Disarmament is a great thing for the world, and putting the idea across is the first step toward even bigger things. I know the relative truths of what you say about America’s peril and what you get from saying it. Am I clear?”

===Chinese===
「我是来找你的，梅尔彻。」
「好吧，事到如今，进来也无妨。我也是从矿井里熬出头的，我是个硬壳蛋。你要是跟我来邪的，你就得死。你想要啥？」
雨果在一张皮椅里坐下，点起一支烟。他大体上不动声色。这是他既定的任务，他要速战速决。「我到这儿来，梅尔彻，」他开口道，「是要谈谈你在军备会议里扮演的角色。碰巧，我不赞成你和你那套宣传。碰巧，我有办法强制推行我的主张。裁军是造福世界的大事，把这件事办成，是迈向更大事业的第一步。你那套美国危殆论里几分是真、你又从那套说辞里捞到什么，我都掂得清清楚楚。我说得够明白了吗？」

===Original===
Melcher had reddened. He nodded. “Perfectly.”
“I have nothing to add. Get out of town.”
Melcher’s eyes narrowed. “Do you really believe that sending me out of town would do any good? Do you have the conceit to think that one nutty shrimp like you can buck the will and ideas of millions of people?”
Hugo did not permit his convictions to be shaken. “There happen to be extenuating circumstances, Melcher.”

===Chinese===
梅尔彻的脸涨红了。他点点头。「一清二楚。」
「我无话可补充。滚出城去。」
梅尔彻眯起了眼睛。「你当真以为，把我弄出城去能顶什么用？你竟自负到这般地步，以为凭你这么个疯疯癫癫的小虾米，能拧得过千百万人的意志和想法？」
雨果不容自己的信念动摇。「碰巧还有几分从轻的情节，梅尔彻。」

===Original===
“Really? You surprise me.” The broad sarcasm was shaken like a weapon. “And do you honestly think you could chase me—me—out of here?”
“I am sure of it.”
“How?”
Hugo extinguished his cigarette. “I happen to be more than a man. I am—” he hesitated, seeking words—“let us say, a devil, or an angel, or a scourge. I detest you and what you stand for. If you do not leave—I can ruin your house and destroy you. And I will.” He finished his words almost gently.

===Chinese===
「是吗？你倒叫我吃了一惊。」那毫不掩饰的讥讽，像武器一样抡了出来。「你还当真以为，你能把我——把我——从这儿撵出去？」
「我确信无疑。」
「怎么个撵法？」
雨果掐灭了香烟。「碰巧，我不只是一个人。我是——」他顿了顿，找着字眼——「不妨说，是个恶魔，或者天使，或者一场天罚。我看你不顺眼，也看不顺你所代表的一切。你要是不走——我能毁掉你的房子，毁掉你。而且我说到做到。」他把话说完的时候，语气近乎温柔。

===Original===
Melcher appeared to hesitate. “All right. I’ll go. Immediately. This afternoon.”
Hugo was astonished. “You will go?”
“I promise. Good afternoon, Mr. Danner.”
Hugo rose and walked toward the door. He was seething with surprise and suspicion. Had he actually intimidated Melcher so easily? His hand touched the knob. At that instant Melcher hit him on the head with a chair. It broke in pieces. Hugo turned around slowly.

===Chinese===
梅尔彻像是迟疑了。「行。我走。马上。今天下午就走。」
雨果吃了一惊。「你会走？」
「我保证。午安，丹纳先生。」
雨果起身朝门口走去。他心里翻腾着惊讶与疑窦。他真的这么轻而易举就镇住了梅尔彻？他的手碰到了门钮。就在这一瞬，梅尔彻抡起一把椅子砸在他头上。椅子四分五裂。雨果缓缓转过身来。

===Original===
“I understand. You mistook me for a dangerous lunatic. I was puzzled for a moment. Now—”
Melcher’s jaw sagged in amazement when Hugo did not fall. An instant later he threw himself forward, arms out, head drawn between his shoulders. With one hand Hugo imprisoned his wrists. He lifted Melcher from the floor and shook him. “I meant it, Melcher. And I will give you a sign. Rotten politics, graft, bad government, are doomed.” Melcher watched with staring eyes while Hugo, with his free hand, rapidly demolished the room. He picked up the great desk and smashed it, he tore the stone mantelpiece from its roots; he kicked the fireplace apart; he burst a hole in the brick wall—dragging the bulk of a man behind him as he moved. “Remember that, Melcher. No one else on earth is like me—and I will get you if you fail to stop. I’ll come for you if you squeal about this—and I leave it to you to imagine what will happen.”
Hugo walked into the hall. “You’re all done for—you cheap swindlers. And I am doom.” The door banged.

===Chinese===
「我明白了。你把我错当成危险的疯子了。我方才也懵了一下。现在——」
见雨果没有倒下，梅尔彻惊得下巴都耷拉了下来。转瞬之间，他整个人猛扑上前，双臂张开，脑袋缩进肩膀当中。雨果一只手钳住了他的双腕。他把梅尔彻从地板上拎起来，摇晃着。「我说的是真格的，梅尔彻。我再给你个凭据。腐烂的政治、贪贿、恶政，都在劫难逃。」梅尔彻瞪圆了眼睛，看着雨果用另一只手飞快地拆了那间屋子。他抡起那张大书桌，砸了个粉碎；他把石头壁炉台连根掰了下来；他把壁炉踢散了架；他在砖墙上撞出一个窟窿——一路走还一路拖着那一大块头的身躯。「记住这个，梅尔彻。普天之下，像我这样的再无第二人——你要是不洗手不干，我就来收拾你。你要是把今天的事嚷嚷出去，我也会来找你——会落得个什么下场，留给你自己去想。」
雨果走进门厅。「你们全完蛋了——你们这些廉价的骗子。而我，就是劫数。」门砰的一声关上了。

===Original===
Melcher swayed on his feet, swallowed hard, and ran upstairs. “Pack,” he said to his valet.
He had gone; Hugo had removed the first of the public enemies. Yet Hugo was not satisfied. His approach to Melcher had been dramatic, terrifying, effective. There were rumors of that violent morning. The rumors said that Melcher had been attacked, that he had been bought out for bigger money, that something peculiar was occurring in Washington. If ten, twenty men left and those rumors multiplied by geometrical progression, sheer intimidation would work a vast good.
But other facts disconcerted Hugo. In the first place, his mind kept reverting to Melcher’s words: “Do you have the conceit to think that one person can buck the will of millions?” No matter how powerful that person, his logic added. Millions of dollars or people? the same logic questioned. After all, did it matter? People could be perjured by subtler influences than gold. Secondly, the parley over arms continued to be an impasse despite the absence of Melcher. Perhaps, he argued, he had not removed Melcher soon enough. A more carefully focused consideration showed that, in spite of what Hatten had said. It was not individuals against whom the struggle was made, but mass stupidity, gigantic bulwarks of human incertitude. And a new man came in Melcher’s place—a man who employed different tactics. Hugo could not exorcise the world.

===Chinese===
梅尔彻脚下一个趔趄，艰难地咽了口唾沫，跑上楼去。「收拾行李。」他对贴身男仆说。
他走了；雨果除掉了第一个人民公敌。然而雨果并不满足。他对梅尔彻的这一手，富于戏剧性，骇人听闻，也立竿见影。关于那个狂暴的上午，流言四起。流言说梅尔彻遭了袭击，说他被一笔更大的钱收买了出去，说华盛顿正在出什么蹊跷事。倘若走掉十个、二十个人，这些流言再按几何级数翻涨，单凭威吓，就能成就一桩大大的善举。
但另一些事实让雨果乱了方寸。首先，他的头脑总在翻回梅尔彻那句话：「你以为凭一个人就能拧得过千万人的意志，你自负到这个地步了吗？」不论那个人多么强大，他的逻辑补上一句。是千百万的美元，还是千百万的人民？同一个逻辑又问。说到底，这有什么要紧？人心可以被比黄金更精巧的手段收买过去。其次，梅尔彻虽然已经不在，军备谈判依旧是一场僵局。也许，他辩解道，是他除掉梅尔彻除得还不够早。更冷静地对准了看的思考表明：尽管哈滕说过那些话，这场斗争要对付的并不是一个个个人，而是群体的愚蠢，是人类犹疑不定垒成的巨大壁垒。梅尔彻的位子上又来了个新人——一个换了手法的角色。雨果没法给整个世界驱邪。

===Original===
A few days later Hugo learned that two radicals had been thrown into jail on a charge of murder. The event had taken place in Newark, New Jersey. A federal officer had attempted to break up a meeting. He had been shot. The men arrested were blamed, although it was evident that they were chance seizures, that their proved guilt could be at most only a social resentfulness. At first no one gave the story much attention. The slow wheels of Jersey justice—printed always in quotation marks by the dailies—began to turn. The men were summarily tried and convicted of murder in the first degree. A mob assaulted the jail where they were confined—without success. Two of the mob were wounded by riot guns.
A meeting was held in Berlin, one in London, another in Paris. Moscow was silent, but Moscow was reported to be in an uproar. The trial assumed international proportions overnight. Embassies were stormed; legations from America were forced to board cruisers. Strikes were ordered; long queues of sullen men and women formed at camp kitchens. The President delivered a message to Congress on the subject. Prominent personages debated it in public halls, only to be acclaimed and booed concomitantly. The sentence imposed on two Russian immigrants rocked the world. In some cities it was not safe for American tourists to go abroad in the streets. And all the time the two men drew nearer to the electric chair.
It was then that Hugo met Skorvsky. Many people knew him; he was a radical, a writer; he lived in Washington, he styled himself an unofficial ambassador of the world. A small, dark man with a black moustache who attended one of Hugo’s informal afternoon discussions on a vicarious invitation. “Come over and see Hugo Danner. He’s something new in Washington.”

===Chinese===
几天以后，雨果听说两个激进分子被以谋杀罪名投进了大牢。事情出在新泽西州的纽瓦克。一名联邦人员试图驱散一场集会，中弹身亡。被捕的两个人被定了罪，尽管明眼人一望便知，他们不过是顺手抓来的顶数，即便坐实罪名，至多也只算一种对社会的怨愤。起初，谁也没把这件事太放在心上。「新泽西司法」——日报上提到这几个字时永远打着引号——那缓慢的轮子开始转动。两人被草草审理，定成一级谋杀。一伙暴徒袭击了关押他们的监狱——没有得手。暴徒里有两个挨了防暴枪的枪子儿。
柏林开了一场大会，伦敦一场，巴黎又一场。莫斯科一言不发，但据报，莫斯科已经群情鼎沸。这场审判一夜之间闹成了国际事件。各地的美国使馆遭到围攻；美国的使团人员被迫登上巡洋舰避难。罢工令下达了；阴沉着脸的男男女女在施粥处前排起长龙。总统就此事向国会递交了咨文。头面人物们在大庭广众之下辩论此事，换来的却是喝彩与倒彩齐飞。落在两个俄国移民头上的判决，震撼了整个世界。在一些城市里，美国游客连上街都不安全。而那两个人，始终在一步一步走近电椅。
就在此时，雨果认识了斯科尔夫斯基。认识他的人很多；他是个激进分子，一个作家；他住在华盛顿，自称世界的非官方大使。他是个矮小黝黑、蓄着黑髭的男人，凭一张辗转相托的请帖，出席了雨果的一场非正式午后恳谈。「来看看雨果·丹纳吧。他在华盛顿可是件新鲜事物。」

===Original===
“Something new in Washington? I shall omit the obvious sarcasm. I shall go.” Skorvsky went.
Hugo listened to him talk about the two prisoners. He was lucid; he made allowances for the American democracy, which in themselves were burning criticism. Hugo asked him to dinner. They dined at Hugo’s house.
“You have the French taste in wines,” Skorvsky said, “but, as it is to my mind the finest taste in the world, I can say only that.”

===Chinese===
「华盛顿的新鲜事物？显而易见的挖苦话，我就不说了。我会去的。」斯科尔夫斯基去了。
雨果听他谈那两个囚犯。他条理分明；他对美国式民主作了种种体谅——而这些体谅本身，就是火辣辣的批评。雨果请他吃饭。他们在雨果家里用的晚餐。
「您品酒有法国人的口味，」斯科尔夫斯基说，「不过，既然这口味在我看来是天下第一的口味，我也就只能说到这儿了。」

===Original===
Hugo tried to lead him back to the topic that interested both of them so acutely. Skorvsky shrugged. “You are polite—or else you are curious. I know you—an American business man in Washington with a purpose. Not an apparent purpose—just now. No, no. Just now you are a host, cultivated and genial, and retiring. But at the proper time—ah! A dam somewhere in Arizona. A forest that you covet in Alaska. Is it not so?”
“What if it is not?”
Skorvsky stared at the ceiling. “What then? A secret? Yes, I thought that about you while we were talking to the others today. There is something deep about you, my new friend. You are a power. Possibly you are not even really an American.”

===Chinese===
雨果想把他引回两人都急切关注的话题。斯科尔夫斯基耸了耸肩。「您真客气——要不然，就是好奇。我把您看得透——一个身在华盛顿、怀有目的的美国生意人。眼下还看不出来的目的。不，不。眼下您只是一位主人，有教养，和气，深藏不露。可一旦时机到了——啊！亚利桑那的哪一座水坝。阿拉斯加您眼馋的哪一片森林。是不是这么回事？」
「要是不是这么回事呢？」
斯科尔夫斯基两眼望着天花板。「不是又怎样？一个秘密？是的，今天跟大家说话的时候，我就对您起过这个疑心。您这个人有深不可测的地方，我的新朋友。您是一种力量。说不定，您根本就不是一个真正的美国人。」

===Original===
“That is wrong.”
“You assure me that I am right. But I will agree with you. You are, let us say, the very epitome of the man Mr. Mencken and Mr. Lewis tell us about so charmingly. I am Russian and I cannot know all of America. You might divulge your errand, perhaps?”
“Suppose I said it was to set the world aright?”

===Chinese===
「这您可说岔了。」
「您这一保证，倒叫我确信自己没说岔。不过，我愿意依着您。您就算是这样一个人吧——门肯先生和刘易斯先生用那支妙笔给我们描绘过的那种美国人的极致化身。我是俄国人，不可能把整个美国都摸透。您那桩差事，或许可以透个底？」
「要是我说，是要把这个世界扶正呢？」

===Original===
Skorvsky laughed lightly. “Then I should throw myself at your feet.”
Both men were in deadly earnest, Hugo not quite willing to adopt the Russian’s almost effeminate delicacy, yet eager to talk to him, or to someone like him—someone who was more than a great self-centerd wheel in the progress of the nation. Hugo yielded a little further. “Yet that is my purpose. And I am not altogether impotent. There are things I can do—” He got up from the table and stretched himself with a feline grace.
“Such as?”

===Chinese===
斯科尔夫斯基轻轻笑了。「那我就该一头拜倒在您脚下了。」
两个人都是十二万分的认真。雨果不太情愿迁就俄国人那股近乎脂粉气的细腻劲儿，却又渴望同他、或同他这一类人交谈——一个不单是国家进程里一只硕大而自我为轴的轮子的人。雨果又退让了一步。「然而这正是我的目的。而且，我也并非全然无能为力。有些事，我能做——」他从桌旁站起身，带着猫一般的优雅舒展了一下肢体。
「比如？」

===Original===
“I was thinking of your two compatriots who were recently given such wretched justice. Suppose they were liberated by force. What then?”
“Ah! You are an independent communist?”
“Not even that. Just a friend of progress.”

===Chinese===
「我在想您那两位同胞，他们新近领受了那样悲惨的司法。假如他们被人用武力解救出去。那会怎样？」
「啊！您是个独来独往的共产主义者？」
「连那个也算不上。只是进步的朋友。」

===Original===
“So. A dreamer. One of the few who have wealth. And you have a plan to free these men?”
Hugo shrugged. “I merely speculated on the possible outcome of such a thing; assume that they were snatched from prison and hidden beyond the law.”
Skorvsky meditated. “It would be a great victory for the cause, of course. A splendid lift to its morale.”

===Chinese===
「原来如此。一个梦想家。有财力的人里头寥寥可数的那种。这么说，您有一套营救这两个人的计划？」
雨果耸耸肩。「我不过推想了一下这件事可能的结局；假定他们被人从牢里劫走，藏到法律够不着的地方。」
斯科尔夫斯基沉吟起来。「那自然是一场大胜，对这事业而言。对士气，是一次辉煌的提振。」

===Original===
“The cause of Bolshevism?”
“A higher and a different cause. I cannot explain it briefly. Perhaps I cannot explain it at all. But the old world of empires is crumbled. Democracy is at its farcical height. The new world is not yet manifest. I shall be direct. What is your plan, Mr. Danner?”
“I couldn’t tell you. Anyway, you would not believe it. But I could guarantee to deliver those two men anywhere in the country within a few days without leaving a trace of how it was done. What do you think of that, Skorvsky?”

===Chinese===
「布尔什维主义的事业？」
「一个更高的、也不一样的事业。我没法三言两语说清。也许我根本说不清。但帝国的旧世界已经土崩瓦解。民主正走到它滑稽透顶的高处。新世界还没有显出形来。我就直说了吧。您的计划是什么，丹纳先生？」
「我没法告诉您。反正您也不会信。但我可以担保，不出几天，把那两个人送到国内随便哪个地方，而且不留一丝痕迹。您觉得这话如何，斯科尔夫斯基？」

===Original===
“I think you are a dangerous and a valuable man.”
“Not many people do.” Hugo’s eyes were moody. “I have been thinking about it for a long time. Nothing that I can remember has happened during my life that gives me a greater feeling of understanding than the imprisonment and sentencing of those men. I know poignantly the glances that are given them, the stupidity of the police and the courts, the horror-stricken attitude of those who condemn them without knowledge of the truth or a desire for such knowledge.” He buried his face in his hands and then looked up quickly. “I know all that passionately and intensely. I know the blind fury to which it all gives birth. I hate it. I detest it. Selfishness, stupidity, malice. I know the fear it engenders—a dreadful and a justified fear. I’ve felt it. Very little in this world avails against it. You’ll forgive so much sentiment, Skorvsky?”
“It makes us brothers.” The Russian spoke with force and simplicity. “You, too—”

===Chinese===
「我觉得，您是个危险人物，也是个可贵的人物。」
「这么看的人不多。」雨果的眼里带着忧郁。「这件事我已经想了很久。在我的记忆里，一生中还没有哪一件事，比这两个人的被囚和被判，更让我生出透彻于心的了解。那些投向他们的目光，警察和法庭的愚蠢，那些不问真相、也不想问真相便定他们罪的人脸上那种惊骇之色——这一切，我都钻心刻骨地知道。」他双手掩面，随即又猛地抬起来。「这一切，我都知道得热烈而透彻。我知道这一切生出的那种盲目的狂怒。我恨它。我憎恶它。自私、愚蠢、歹毒。我知道它滋生出的恐惧——一种可怕的、却又事出有因的恐惧。我亲身领受过。这世上能跟它抗衡的东西，少而又少。这么许多感慨，您多包涵，斯科尔夫斯基？」
「这使我们成了兄弟。」俄国人说得恳切而质朴。「您也——」

===Original===
Hugo crossed the room restlessly. “I don’t know. I am always losing my grip. I came to Washington with a purpose and I cannot screw myself to it unremittingly. These men seem—”
Skorvsky was thinking. “Your plan for them. What assistance would you need?”
“None.”

===Chinese===
雨果不安地在屋里走来走去。「我不知道。我总是攥不牢自己的劲头。我来华盛顿，是带着一个目的来的，可我没法把自己一刻不停地拧在这上头。这两个人似乎——」
斯科尔夫斯基在思索。「您为他们定的计划。您需要什么协助？」
「不需要。」

===Original===
“None!”
“Why should I need help? I—never mind. I need none.”
“You have your own organization?”

===Chinese===
「不需要！」
「我干吗要人帮忙？我——罢了。我不需要。」
「您有自己的组织？」

===Original===
“There is no one but me.”
Skorvsky shook his head. “I cannot—and yet—looking at you—I believe you can. I shall tell you. You will come with me tonight and meet my friends—those who are working earnestly for a new America, an America ruled by intelligence alone. Few outsiders enter our councils. We are all—nearly all—foreigners. Yet we are more American than the Maine fisherman, the Minnesota farmer. Behind us is a party that grows apace. This incident in New Jersey has added to it, as does every dense mumble of Congress, every scandalous metropolitan investigation. I shall telephone.”
Hugo allowed himself to be conducted half-dubiously. But what he found was superficially, at least, what he had dreamed for himself. The house to which he was taken was pretentious; the people in its salon were amiable and educated; there was no sign of the red flag, the ragged reformer, the anarchist. The women were gracious; the men witty. As he talked to them, one by one, he began to believe that here was the nucleus around which he could construct his imaginary empire. He became interested; he expanded.

===Chinese===
「除我而外，没有别人。」
斯科尔夫斯基摇了摇头。「我不敢信——可是——再看看您这个人——我相信您办得到。我来告诉您。今晚您跟我走，去见见我的朋友们——那些在切切实实地为一个新美国、一个单单凭智力来治理的美国出力的人。外人极少能进我们的议事圈子。我们全都是——差不多全是——外国人。可是，比起缅因的渔夫、明尼苏达的农夫，我们更算得上美国人。我们背后，是一个正飞速壮大的党。新泽西这件事给它添了力量，正如国会每一次昏聩的咕哝、每一场轰动都市的丑闻调查一样。我去打电话。」
雨果半信半疑地由着他领了去。可他所见到的东西——至少在表面上是——正是他为自己梦想过的东西。他被带去的那栋房子气派堂皇；客厅里的人们和蔼而有教养；没有红旗，没有衣衫褴褛的改革家，没有无政府主义者的影子。女人们雍容大方；男人们妙语如珠。同他们一个一个谈过之后，他开始相信：这正是那个核心，他可以绕着它，构筑自己想象中的帝国。他来了兴致；他舒展了开来。

===Original===
It was late in the night when Skorvsky raised his voice slightly, so that everyone would listen, and made an announcement: “Friends, I have had the honour to introduce Mr. Danner to you. Now I have the greater honour of telling you his purpose and pledge. Tomorrow night he will go to New Jersey”—the silence became absolute—“and two nights later he will bring to us in person from their cells Davidoff and Pletzky.”
A quick, pregnant pause was followed by excitement. They took Hugo by the hand, some of them applauded, one or two cheered, they shouldered near him, they asked questions and expressed doubts. It was broad daylight before they dispersed. Hugo walked to his house, listening to a long rhapsody from Skorvsky.
“We will make you a great man if you succeed,” Skorvsky said. “Good night, comrade.”

===Chinese===
夜深时分，斯科尔夫斯基把嗓音略略一抬，好教人人都听得见，宣布道：「朋友们，我已有幸向各位引见了丹纳先生。现在，我更有幸奉告各位他的目的与誓言。明晚，他就要动身去新泽西」——全场霎时鸦雀无声——「再过两夜，他将亲自把他们从牢房里带到我们中间：达维多夫和普列茨基。」
短促而意味深长的一阵静默过后，是翻腾的兴奋。他们挨个儿握雨果的手，有几个人鼓起掌来，一两个欢呼出声；他们朝他身边挤过来，抛出问题，也道出疑心。待到众人散去，天已大亮。雨果步行回家，一路听着斯科尔夫斯基那长篇大论的狂想曲。
「您若成功，我们会把您造就成一个伟人。」斯科尔夫斯基说。「晚安，同志。」

===Original===
“Good night.” Hugo went into the hall and up to his bedroom. He sat on his bed. A dullness overcame him. He had never been patronized quite in the same way as he had that night; it exerted at once a corrosive and a lethargic influence. He undressed slowly, dropping his shoes on the floor. Splendid people they were, he thought. A smaller voice suggested to him that he did not really care to go to New Jersey for the prisoners. They would be hard to locate. There would be a sensation and a mystery again. Still, he had found a purpose.
His telephone rang. He reached automatically from the bed. The room was bright with sunshine, which meant that it was late in the day. His brain took reluctant hold on consciousness. “Hello?”
“Hello? Danner, my friend—”

===Chinese===
「晚安。」雨果走进门厅，上楼进了卧室。他坐在床沿。一阵沉滞压了上来。他还从来没有被人像那一夜那样抬举过；那滋味立刻生出一种蚀人的、令人萎靡的影响。他慢腾腾地脱衣裳，把鞋撂在地板上。他们都是些出众的人物，他想。一个更细弱的声音向他进言：他其实并不那么想去新泽西救那两个囚犯。他们会很难找。又该有一场轰动、一桩疑案了。话虽如此，他总算找到了一个目的。
电话铃响了。他从床上机械地伸出手去。房间里阳光灿亮，这就是说，天色已经不早。他的脑子不情愿地攥住了意识。「喂？」
「喂？丹纳，我的朋友——」

===Original===
“Oh, hello, Skorvsky—”
“May I come up? It is important.”
“Sure. I’m still in bed. But come on.”

===Chinese===
「哦，喂，斯科尔夫斯基——」
「我能上来吗？事情要紧。」
「当然。我还躺在床上呢。上来吧。」

===Original===
Hugo was under the shower bath when his visitor arrived. He invited Skorvsky to share his breakfast, but was impatiently refused. “Things have happened since last night, Comrade Danner. For one, I saw the chief.”
“Chief?”
“You have not met him as yet. We conferred about your scheme. He—I regret to say—opposed it.”

===Chinese===
客人到的当口，雨果正在淋浴。他请斯科尔夫斯基共进早餐，却被不耐烦地回绝了。「昨夜以来出了些事，丹纳同志。头一件，我见了首领。」
「首领？」
「您还没有跟他见过面。我们磋商了您的计划。他——我很遗憾——反对。」

===Original===
Hugo nodded. “I’m not surprised. I’ll tell you what to do. You take me to him—and I’ll prove conclusively that it will be successful. Then, perhaps, he will agree to sanction it. Every time I think of those two poor devils—snatched from a mob—waiting there in the dark for the electric chair—it makes my blood boil.”
“Quite,” Skorvsky agreed. “But you do not understand. It is not that he doubts your ability—if you failed it would not be important. He fears you might accomplish it. I assured him you would. I have faith in you.”
“He’s afraid I would do it? That doesn’t make sense, Skorvsky.”

===Chinese===
雨果点点头。「我不意外。这么说吧。你带我去见他——我会一锤定音地证明这事必成。到那时候，也许他就肯批准了。一想到那两个可怜虫——从暴民手里夺下来的——正在黑地里等着上电椅——我就血往上涌。」
「可不是嘛。」斯科尔夫斯基附和道。「可您不明白。不是他怀疑您的能力——您纵然失手，也无关紧要。他是怕您办得成。我向他保证了您办得成。我对您有信心。」
「他怕我办得成？这说不通啊，斯科尔夫斯基。」

===Original===
“It does, I regret to say.” His expressive face stirred with discomfort. “We were too hasty, too precipitate. I see his reason now. We cannot afford as a group to be branded as jail-breakers.”
“That’s—weak,” Hugo said.
Skorvsky cleared his throat. “There are other matters. Since Davidoff and Pletzky were jailed, the party has grown by leaps and bounds. Money has poured in—”

===Chinese===
「很遗憾，说得通。」他那张富于表情的脸因局促而抽动。「我们太急了，太冒失了。如今我明白他的道理了。作为一个团体，我们担不起落下劫狱者同党的名声。」
「这——太软弱了。」雨果说。
斯科尔夫斯基清了清嗓子。「还有别的事。自打达维多夫和普列茨基下狱，我们党正一日千里地壮大。钱像潮水一样涌进来——」

===Original===
“Ah,” Hugo said softly, “money.”
Skorvsky raged. “Go ahead. Be sarcastic. To free those men would cost us a million dollars, perhaps.”
“Too bad.”

===Chinese===
「啊，」雨果轻声说，「钱。」
斯科尔夫斯基勃然大怒。「说吧。挖苦个够。救那两个人，兴许要花掉我们一百万。」
「真可惜。」

===Original===
“With a million—the million their electrocution will bring from the outraged—we can accomplish more than saving two paltry lives. We must be hard, we must think ahead.”
“In thinking ahead, Skorvsky, do you not think of the closing of a switch and the burning of human flesh?”
“For every cause there must be martyrs. Their names will live eternally.”

===Chinese===
「有了这一百万——他们一上电椅、义愤填膺的人们就会捐来的那一百万——我们能办成的事，比救两条区区性命多得多。我们必须心硬，我们必须往前想。」
「往前想的时候，斯科尔夫斯基，您想没想过合上电闸、烧焦人肉的滋味？」
「每一种事业都必须有殉道者。他们的名字将万世流传。」

===Original===
“And they themselves—?”
“Bah! You are impractical.”
“Perhaps.” Hugo ate a slice of toast with outward calm. “I was hoping for a government that—did not weigh people against dollars—”

===Chinese===
「那他们两位自己呢——？」
「呸！您太不切实际。」
「也许吧。」雨果外表平静地吃了一片吐司。「我原指望有一个不把人搁在天平上、同美元称量的政府——」

===Original===
“Nor do we!”
“No?”
Skorvsky leaped to his feet. “Fool! Dreamer! Preposterous idealist! I must be going.”
Hugo sighed. “Suppose I went ahead?”

===Chinese===
「我们也不称量！」
「不称量？」
斯科尔夫斯基跳了起来。「傻子！梦想家！荒唐透顶的理想主义者！我该走了。」
雨果叹了口气。「要是我偏要干下去呢？」

===Original===
“One thing!” The Russian turned with a livid face. “One thing the chief bade me tell you. If those men escape—you die.”
“Oh,” Hugo said. He stared through the window. “And supposing I were to offer your chief a million—or nearly a million—for the privilege of freeing them?”
Skorvsky’s face returned to its look of transfiguration, the look that had accompanied his noblest words of the night before. “You would do that, comrade?” he whispered. “You would give us—give the cause—a million? Never since the days of our Saviour has a man like you walked on this—”
Hugo stood up suddenly. “Get out of here!” His voice was a cosmic menace. “Get out of here, you dirty swine. Get out of here before I break you to matchwood, before I rip out your guts and stuff them back through your filthy, lying throat. Get out, oh, God, get out!”

===Chinese===
「有一句话！」俄国人转过身来，脸色铁青。「首领吩咐我转告您一件事。那两个人要是跑了——您就得死。」
「哦。」雨果说。他望着窗外。「那要是我出一百万——或者将近一百万——向您那位首领买下放人的特权呢？」
斯科尔夫斯基的脸上，又回到了那种圣容发显似的神色，正是前一夜伴着他最崇高的话语出现过的那种神色。「您当真愿意，同志？」他喃喃道。「您愿意给我们——给这事业——一百万？自从救世主在世的日子以来，还没有一个像您这样的人走过这——」
雨果霍地站起身。「滚出去！」他的声音，是一场宇宙规模的威吓。「滚出去，你这头肮脏的猪。趁我还没把你劈成引火柴，趁我还没掏出你的肠子、从你那条肮脏的、专会撒谎的喉咙里塞回去，滚出去。滚，哦，上帝啊，滚出去！」

## XXII / 第二十二章

===Original===
Hugo realized at last that there was no place in his world for him. Tides and tempest, volcanoes and lightning, all other majestic vehemences of the universe had a purpose, but he had none. Either because he was all those forces unnaturally locked in the body of a man, or because he was a giant compelled to stoop and pander to live at all among his feeble fellows, his anachronism was complete.

That much he perceived calmly. His tragedy lay in the lie he had told to his father: great deeds were always imminent and none of them could be accomplished because they involved humanity, humanity protecting its diseases, its pettiness, its miserable convictions and conventions, with the essence of itself—life. Life not misty and fecund for the future, but life clawing at the dollar in the hour, the security of platitudes, the relief of visible facts, the hope in rationalization, the needs of skin, belly, and womb.

Beyond that, he could see destiny by interpreting his limited career. Through a sort of ontogenetic recapitulation he had survived his savage childhood, his barbaric youth, and the Greeces, Romes, Egypts, and Babylons of his early manhood, emerging into a present that was endowed with as much aspiration and engaged with the same futility as was his contemporary microcosm. No life span could observe anything but material progress, for so mean and inalterable is the gauge of man that his races topple before his soul expands, and the eventualities of his growth in space and time must remain a problem for thousands and tens of thousands of years.

===Chinese===
雨果终于明白，这世上终究没有他的容身之地。潮汐与风暴，火山与雷电，宇宙间其余一切雄伟的暴烈之力都各有用途，唯独他没有。或是因为他正是这一切力量，被不自然地锁进了一具人的躯壳；或是因为他是个巨人，要在孱弱的同类中间活下去，便不得不俯身屈就、曲意逢迎——总之，他的不合时宜已是至矣尽矣。

这一层，他是心平气和地看清的。他的悲剧在于他对父亲撒下的那个谎：伟大的功业永远近在眉睫，却一件也做不成，因为桩桩件件都牵涉人类——人类以自己的本质、以生命本身，护着自己的病痛、自己的褊狭、自己那可悲的信念与成规。这生命不是为着未来而朦胧丰饶的生命，而是当下便攫住美元不放的生命，是贪恋陈词滥调的安稳、眼见为实的宽慰、自我开脱里的指望、以及皮肤肚腹与子宫之所需的生命。

再往深一层看，凭着自己这段有限的经历，他已能解读命运。循着一种个体发育重演般的路径，他挨过了蒙昧的童年、蛮荒的青年，也挨过了壮年初期的那些希腊、罗马、埃及与巴比伦，终于走进当今之世——这个当今之世怀着不亚于他同时代小宇宙的向往，也耽于同样徒劳的营生。区区一生，除了物质的进步什么也看不见；因为人的量尺是那样卑微而不可移易，他的各族类总在灵魂舒展之前便已倾覆，而他在空间与时间中生长的种种结局，势必要留给千千万万年去悬而未决。

===Original===
Searching still further, he appreciated that no single man could force a change upon his unwilling fellows. At most he might inculcate an idea in a few and live to see its gradual spreading. Even then he could have no assurance of its contortions to the desire for wealth and power or of the consequences of those contortions.

Finally, to build, one must first destroy, and he questioned his right to select unaided the objects for destruction. He looked at the Capitol in Washington and pondered the effect of issuing an ultimatum and thereafter bringing down the great dome like Samson. He thought of the churches and their bewildering, stupefying effect on masses who were mulcted by their own fellows, equally bewildered, equally stupefied. Suppose through a thousand nights he ravaged the churches, wrecking every structure in the land, laying waste property, making the loud, unattended volume of worship an impossibility, taking away the purple-robed gods of his forbears? Suppose he sank the navy, annihilated the army, set up a despotism? No matter how efficiently and well he ruled, the millions would hate him, plot against him, attempt his life; and every essential agent would be a hypocritical sycophant seeking selfish ends.

He reached the last of his conclusions sitting beside a river whither he had walked to think. An immense loathing for the world rose up in him. At its apex a locomotive whistled in the distance, thundered inarticulately, and rounded a bend. It came very near the place where Hugo reclined, black, smoking, and noisy, drivers churning along the rails, a train of passenger cars behind. Hugo could see the dots that were people's heads. People! Human beings! How he hated them! The train was very near. Suddenly all his muscles were unsprung. He threw himself to his feet and rushed toward the train, with a passionate desire to get his fingers around the sliding piston, to upend the locomotive and to throw the ordered machinery into a blackened, blazing, bloody tangle of ruin.

===Chinese===
再往深处搜寻，他领悟到：没有哪一个人能强迫不甘不愿的同类接受变革。充其量，他或许能把一个观念灌输给寥寥几人，并活着看到它渐渐传布。即便到那时，他也无从担保这观念在财富与权势的欲望面前会如何扭曲变形，更无从担保那些扭曲会生出什么后果。

最后一条：要建设，必先破坏，而他怀疑自己是否有权独断独拣破坏的对象。他曾凝望华盛顿的国会大厦，思量先发出一道最后通牒、再像参孙那样拽塌那座大穹顶，会是何等光景。他也想到教堂，想到它们如何令芸芸大众目眩神迷、麻木痴騃——这些大众，正被他们同样目眩神迷、同样痴騃的同胞敲诈盘剥。假使在一千个夜晚里他接连洗劫教堂，捣毁国中每一座建筑，夷尽财产，叫那喧天而不经省察的礼拜之声从此绝响，夺走他祖先那些紫袍的神祇，又当如何？假使他击沉海军、荡平陆军、自立一个独裁之政呢？任凭他治国何等干练、何等贤明，千百万民众依旧会恨他、密谋算计他、行刺害他性命；而每一处要津的代理人，都将是营私自图的伪君子与谄媚之徒。

他坐在一条河边——他是特意走到那里去思索的——最后的结论便是在那里得出的。一股对世界的巨大憎恶从他胸中升起。恰在憎恶攀至顶点的一刻，一列机车在远方鸣笛，轰隆出一串含混的怒吼，拐过一道弯。它驶到离雨果倚卧之处极近的地方，黑黢黢、喷着烟、闹嚷嚷，动轮在铁轨上滚滚翻搅，身后拖着一长列客车。雨果望得见那些小点，那是人们的人头。人！人类！他恨他们恨到了极处！火车已近在眼前。蓦然，他周身的肌肉一齐绷开。他霍地跳起身，朝火车猛冲过去，怀着狂烈的欲念，想把手指抠进那滑动的活塞，把机车掀个底朝天，把那整套井然有序的机器摔作一团焦黑、烈焰、血污交缠的残骸。

===Original===
His lips uttered a wild cry; he jumped across the river and ran two prodigious steps. Then he stopped. The train went on unharmed. Hugo shuddered.

If the world did not want him, he would leave the world. Perhaps he was a menace to it. Perhaps he should kill himself. But his burning, sickened heart refused once more to give up. Frenzy departed, then numbness. In its place came a fresh hope, new determination. Hugo Danner would do his utmost until the end. Meanwhile, he would remove himself some distance from the civilization that had tortured him. He would go away and find a new dream.

The sound of the locomotive was dead in the distance. He crossed the river on a bridge and went back to his house. He felt strong again and glad—glad because he had won an obscure victory, glad because the farce of his quest in political government had ended with no tragic dénouement.

They were electrocuting Davidoff and Pletzky that day. The news scarcely interested Hugo. The part he had very nearly played in the affair seemed like the folly of a dimly remembered acquaintance. The relief of resigning that impossible purpose overwhelmed him. He dismissed his servants, closed his house, and boarded a train. When the locomotive pounded through the station, he suffered a momentary pang. He sat in a seat with people all around him. He was tranquil and almost content.

===Chinese===
他唇间迸出一声狂呼；他一跃跨过河去，迈出两步惊天的大步。随即他收住了。火车安然驶过，毫发无伤。雨果浑身一颤。

这世界既然容不下他，他便离开这个世界。或许他对它是个祸患。或许他该杀了自己。可他那颗灼痛的、作呕的心，又一次拒绝放弃。狂热退了，麻木也退了。顶替上来的是一线新生的希望、一股崭新的决心。雨果·丹纳要竭尽全力，直到终局。眼下，他且从这折磨过他的文明旁边退开一段路。他要远走他方，去寻一个新的梦。

机车的声响已在远方死寂。他从一座桥上过了河，回到自己的宅子。他又觉得自己有力了，而且欢喜——欢喜他赢了一场无名的胜利，欢喜他问政求治的这一场追寻虽成闹剧，收场却终究没有酿成悲剧的结局。

那一天，他们正要对达维多夫和普列茨基处以电刑。这消息几乎引不起雨果的兴趣。他在这桩事里险些扮下的角色，恍如一位记忆模糊的旧相识所干的蠢事。卸却那桩不可能的使命，如释重负之感将他整个淹没。他辞退仆人，闭锁宅门，登上了一列火车。机车轰然撞过车站时，他心口掠过一阵短短的刺痛。他拣了个座位坐下，四围全是人。他心境澄定，几乎可以说是心安意足了。

## XXIII / 第二十三章

===Original===
Hugo had no friends. One single individual whom he loved, whom he could have taken fully into his confidence, might, in a measure, have resolved his whole life. Yet so intense was the pressure that had conditioned him that he invariably retreated before the rare opportunities for such confidences. He had known many persons well: his father and mother, Anna Blake, Lefty Foresman, Charlotte, Iris, Tom Shayne, Roseanne, even Skorvsky﻿—but none of them had known him. His friendlessness was responsible for a melancholy yearning to remain with his kind. Having already determined to go away, he sought for a kind of compromise.

He did not want to be in New York, or Washington, or any other city; the landscape of America was haunted for him. He would leave it, but he would not open himself to the cruel longing for his own language, the sight of familiar customs and manners. From his hotel in New York he made excursions to various steamship agencies and travel bureaus. He had seen many lands, and his Wanderlust demanded novelty. For days he was undecided.

It was a chance group of photographs in a Sunday newspaper that excited his first real interest. One of the pictures was of a man﻿—erect, white-haired, tanned, clear-eyed﻿—Professor Daniel Hardin﻿—a procession of letters﻿—head of the new expedition to Yucatan. The other pictures were of ruined temples, unpiled stone causeways, jungle. He thought instantly that he would like to attach himself to the party.
===Chinese===
雨果没有朋友。倘若曾有过单独一个人，一个他爱着的、能让他推心置腹的人，他的一生或许多少能由此得解。然而塑造出他的那股压力实在太深重，以致每当这种交心的稀有机会当真出现，他总是退避三舍。他熟识过许多人：父亲与母亲、安娜·布莱克、左撇子·福斯曼、夏洛特、艾瑞丝、汤姆·谢恩、罗珊妮，甚至还有斯科尔夫斯基——但他们当中没有一个真正认识过他。正因为举目无友，他才生出一种忧郁的渴望，渴望仍旧留在自己的同类中间。既已决意远走，他便开始寻一条折中的路子。

他不愿待在纽约，不愿待在华盛顿，也不愿待在任何别的城市；美国的山川景物于他已是阴魂不散之地。他要离开它，却又不肯任由自己向那残酷的渴念敞开——对故土语言的思慕，对熟悉风物人情的眷顾。他从纽约下榻的旅馆出发，一趟趟走访各家轮船公司与旅行社。他已见过许多国土，他那一腔云游的热望（Wanderlust）渴求新奇。一连数日，他拿不定主意。

是一份星期日报纸上偶然登出的一组照片，头一回真正勾起了他的兴趣。其中一张照的是个男人——身姿挺拔，一头白发，古铜色的皮肤，目光澄澈——丹尼尔·哈丁（Daniel Hardin）教授——名衔排成一长串——尤卡坦（Yucatan）新远征队的领队。其余几张是倾颓的神庙、石块坍散的古堤道、丛林。他当即想到，自己愿意加入这支队伍。

===Original===
Many factors combined to make the withdrawal offered by an expedition ideal. The more Hugo thought about it, the more excited he became. The very nascency of a fresh objective was accompanied by and crowded with new hints for himself and his problems. The expedition would take him away from his tribulations, and it would not entirely cut him off from his kind: Professor Hardin had both the face and the fame of a distinguished man.

A thought that had been in the archives of his mind for many months came sharply into relief: of all human beings alive, the scientists were the only ones who retained imagination, ideals, and a sincere interest in the larger world. It was to them he should give his allegiance, not to the statesmen, not to industry or commerce or war. Hugo felt that in one quick glimpse he had made a long step forward.

Another concept, far more fantastic and in a way even more intriguing, dawned in his mind as he read accounts of the Maya ruins which were to be excavated. The world was cluttered with these great lumps of incredible architecture. Walls had been builded by primitive man, temples, hanging gardens, obelisks, pyramids, palaces, bridges, terraces, roads﻿—all of them gigantic and all of them defying the penetration of archæology to find the manner of their creation. Was it not possible﻿—Hugo’s heart skipped a beat when it occurred to him﻿—that in their strange combination of ignorance and brilliance the ancients had stumbled upon the secret of human strength﻿—his secret! Had not those antique and migratory peoples carried with them the formula which could be poured into the veins of slaves, making them stronger than engines? And was it not conceivable that, as their civilizations crumbled, the secret was lost, together with so many other formulæ of knowledge?
===Chinese===
种种因素凑在一起，使一场远征所能提供的退隐成了再理想不过的去处。雨果越想越兴奋。一个新目标方才萌生，种种新的启示便已随之而来、簇拥而至——关于他自己，也关于他的难题。远征会把他从种种磨难中带开，又不至于叫他同自己的同类彻底断绝：哈丁教授既有名士之仪，又有名士之望。

一个在他心库深处搁了几个月的念头，此刻赫然凸现：当今活着的人里头，唯有科学家还保有想象力、保有理想、保有一份对更广阔世界的真诚兴味。他该输诚效力的正是他们，而不是政客，不是实业、商业或战争。雨果觉得，就在这倏忽一瞥之间，他向前跨出了一大步。

读着那些讲述行将发掘的玛雅（Maya）废墟的报道时，另一个念头又在他心头破晓——这念头荒诞得多，某种意义上也撩人得多。世界上到处堆着这类不可思议的庞然巨构。原始人筑起过城墙、神庙、空中花园、方尖碑、金字塔、宫殿、桥梁、梯台、道路——它们无一不巨大无朋，又无一不叫考古学钻探不透，探不出它们究竟是如何造就的。有没有这种可能——想到此处，雨果的心跳漏了一拍——那些古人在愚昧与英明的奇特交缠里，曾歪打正着地撞上过人类力量的秘密——他的秘密！那些古老而迁徙不定的民族，岂不曾随身携着那道方子，可以灌进奴隶的血脉，使他们比机器还强？而随着他们的文明崩塌，这秘密连同其余多少知识的方子一并失传，岂不也是可以想见的么？

===Original===
He could imagine plumed and painted priests with prayer and sacrifice cutting open the veins of prehistoric mothers and pouring in the magic potion. When the babies grew, they could raise up the pyramids, walls, and temples; they could do it rapidly and easily. A great enigma was thus resolved. He set out immediately to locate Professor Hardin and with difficulty arranged an interview with him.

Preparations for the expedition were being carried on in an ordinary New York business office. A secretary announced Hugo and he was conducted before the professor. Daniel Hardin was no dusty pedagogue. His knowledge was profound and academic, his books were authoritative, but in himself he belonged to the type of man certain to succeed, whatever his choice of occupation. Much of his life had been spent in field work﻿—arduous toil in bizarre lands where life depended sometimes on tact and sometimes on military strategy. He appraised Hugo shrewdly before he spoke.

“What can I do for you, Mr. Danner?”

Hugo came directly to the point. “I should like to join your Yucatan expedition.”
===Chinese===
他想象得出那些插翎涂彩的祭司，如何一面祷告、一面献祭，剖开史前母亲的血管，把魔液灌将进去。待婴儿长大成人，他们便能扛起金字塔、城墙与神庙；而且干得又快又省力。一大谜团就此解开。他随即动身寻访哈丁教授，费了些周折才安排下一次会面。

远征队的筹备事宜正在纽约一间寻常的商务写字楼里进行。一位秘书通报了雨果来见，把他领到教授面前。丹尼尔·哈丁不是那种积满尘灰的学究。他学问渊博而精深，著作具有权威，但他本人属于无论择何为业都注定成功的那一类人。他大半生都耗在田野工作里——在种种奇邦异域辛苦操劳，在那里，性命有时系于手腕的圆融，有时系于军事的韬略。开口之前，他先把雨果精明地打量了一番。

「丹纳先生，我能为您效什么劳？」

雨果开门见山。「我想加入您的尤卡坦远征队。」

===Original===
Professor Hardin smiled. “I’m sorry. We’re full up.”

“I’d be glad to go in any capacity﻿—”

“Have you special qualifications? Knowledge of the language? Of archæology?”

“No.”
===Chinese===
哈丁教授微微一笑。「抱歉，名额满了。」

「我愿意以任何身份前往——」

「您有什么专长吗？懂那里的语言吗？懂考古学吗？」

「不懂。」

===Original===
The professor picked up a tray of letters. “These letters﻿—more than three hundred﻿—are all from young men﻿—and women﻿—who would like to join my expedition.”

“I think I should be useful,” Hugo said, and then he played his trump, “and I should be willing to contribute, for the favor of being included, a sum of fifty thousand dollars.”

Professor Hardin whistled. Then his eyes narrowed. “What’s your object, young man? Treasure?”
===Chinese===
教授拿起一盘信件。「这些信——三百多封——全是从想加入我远征队的年轻人那儿来的——也有姑娘。」

「我想我会派得上用场，」雨果说，接着亮出了王牌，「而且，为蒙收录之惠，我愿捐一笔款子——五万美元。」

哈丁教授吹了声口哨。随即他眯起眼睛。「您图的是什么，年轻人？财宝？」

===Original===
“No. A life﻿—let us say﻿—with ample means at my disposal and no definite purpose.”

“Boredom, then.” He smiled. “A lot of these other young men are independently wealthy, and bored. I must say, I feel sorry for your generation. But﻿—no﻿—I can’t accept. We are already adequately financed.”

Hugo smiled in response. “Then﻿—perhaps﻿—I could organize my own party and camp near you.”
===Chinese===
「不是。是一种——这么说吧——手头宽绰、却无一定目标的人生。」

「那就是闲得发慌了。」他笑了。「这些年轻人里有好些个都是家财自主，又都闲得发慌。我得说，我真替你们这一代惋惜。不过——不行——我不能收。我们的经费已经很充足了。」

雨果报以一笑。「那么——或许——我可以自己组一支队伍，在你们近旁扎营。」

===Original===
“That would hamper me.”

“Then﻿—a hundred thousand dollars.”

“Good Lord. You are determined.”
===Chinese===
「那会碍我的事。」

「那么——十万美元。」

「老天爷。您这是铁了心了。」

===Original===
“I have decided. I am familiar with the jungle. I am an athlete. I speak a little Spanish﻿—enough to boss a labor gang. I propose to assist you in that way, as well as financially. I will make any contract with you that you desire﻿—and attach no strings whatever to my money.”

Professor Hardin pondered for a long time. His eyes twinkled when he replied. “You won’t believe it, but I don’t give a damn for your money. Not that it wouldn’t assist us. But﻿—the fact is﻿—I could use a man like you. Anybody could. I’ll take you﻿—and you can keep your money.”

“There will be a check in the mail tomorrow,” Hugo answered.
===Chinese===
「我主意已定。我熟悉丛林。我是个运动健将。我会一点西班牙语——支使一支劳工队尽够了。我打算出力也出钱，从这两方面协助您。您愿意立什么样的契约我都依——而且我的钱不带任何附带条件。」

哈丁教授沉吟了好一阵。答话时，他眼里闪着光。「您不会相信的，可我对您的钱毫不在乎。倒不是说它帮不上忙。可是——实情是——我正用得上您这样一个人。谁都用得上。我收下您——钱您自己留着吧。」

「明天邮件里会有一张支票。」雨果答道。

===Original===
The professor stood. “We’re hoping to get away in three weeks. You’ll leave your address with my secretary and I’ll send a list of the things you’ll want for your kit.” He held out his hand and Hugo shook it. When he had gone, the professor looked over the rooftops and swore gleefully to himself.

Hugo discovered, after the ship sailed, that everyone called Professor Hardin “Dan” and they used Hugo’s first name from the second day out. Dan Hardin was too busy to be very friendly with any of the members of his party during the voyage, but they themselves fraternized continually. There were deck games and card games; there were long and erudite arguments about the people whom they were going to study. What was the Mayan time cycle and did it correspond to the Egyptian Sothic cycle or the Greek Metonic cycle. Where did the Mayans get their jade? Did they come from Asia over Bering Strait or were they a colony of Atlantis? When they knew so much about engineering, why did they not use the keystone arch and the wheel? Why was their civilization decadent, finished when the conquistadores discovered it? How old were they﻿—four thousand years or twelve thousand years? There were innumerable other debates to which Hugo listened like a man newborn.

The cold Atlantic winds were transformed overnight to the balm of the Gulf stream. Presently they passed the West Indies, which lay on the water like marine jewels. Ages turned back through the days of buccaneering to the more remote times. In the port of Xantl a rickety wharf, a single white man, a zinc bar, and a storehouse filled with chicle blocks marked off the realm of the twentieth century. The ship anchored. During the next year it would make two voyages back to the homeland for supplies. But the explorers would not emerge from the jungle in that time.
===Chinese===
教授站起身。「我们指望三个星期后动身。您把地址留给我的秘书，我会寄一份行装清单给您。」他伸出手来，雨果握了。雨果走后，教授越过屋顶望向远方，独自乐不可支地咒骂了一声。

船起锚之后雨果才发现，人人都管哈丁教授叫「丹」，而出海第二天起，大家也叫起雨果的名字来。航行途中丹·哈丁忙得很，同队里谁都谈不上多么亲热，队员们彼此之间却过从甚欢。甲板上有游戏，有牌局；还有一场场冗长而掉书袋的争论，争的是他们即将研究的那个民族。玛雅人的时间周期究竟是什么，它与埃及的索提斯周期、希腊的默冬周期对不对得上？玛雅人的玉是从哪儿来的？他们是从亚洲取道白令海峡而来，还是亚特兰蒂斯（Atlantis）的一支殖民地？他们既然那样通晓工程，为什么不用拱心石拱、不用轮子？为什么他们的文明早已衰颓凋敝，在西班牙征服者（conquistadores）发现它时便已终了？他们究竟多古老——四千年，还是一万两千年？此外还有数不清的辩题，雨果听着，恰似一个初生的婴儿。

大西洋的寒风一夜之间化成了墨西哥湾流的温煦。不多时他们驶过西印度群岛，群岛卧在水上，宛如海中的珠玉。岁月从海盗横行的日子一路倒卷回去，倒回更邈远的时代。在桑特尔（Xantl）港，一座摇摇欲坠的码头、一个白人、一口白铁皮的酒吧、一座堆满糖胶树胶块的货栈，便划出了二十世纪天地的疆界。船抛了锚。来年之内，它还要两次航回本土运送补给。但探险家们在那段时日里不会再从丛林里出来。

===Original===
An antiquated, wood-burning locomotive, which rocked along over treacherous rails, carried them inland. The scientists became silent and pensive. In another car the Maya Indians who were to do the manual labor chattered incessantly in their explosive tongue. At the last sunbaked stop they disembarked, slept through an insect-droning night, and entered the jungle. For three weeks they hacked and hewed their way forward; the vegetation closed behind them, cutting off the universe as completely as the submerging waves of the sea. It was hot, difficult work, to which Hugo lent himself with an energy that astounded even Hardin, who had judged him valuable.

One day, when the high mountains loomed into view, Hugo caught his first glimpse of Uctotol, the Sacred City. A creeper on the hillside fell before his machete, then another﻿—a hole in the green wall﻿—and there it stood, shining white, huge, desolate, still as the grave. His arm hung in midair. Over him passed the mystic feeling of familiarity, that fugitive sense of recognition which springs so readily into a belief in immortality. It seemed to him during that staggering instant that he knew every contour of those great structures, that he had run in the streets, lived, loved, died there﻿—that he could almost remember the names and faces of its inhabitants, dead for thousands of years﻿—that he could nearly recall the language and the music﻿—that destiny itself had arranged a homecoming. The vision died. He gave a great shout. The others rushed to his side and found him trembling and pointing.

Tons of verdure were cut down and pushed aside. A hacienda was constructed and a camp for the laborers. Then the shovels and picks were broken from their boxes; the scientists arranged their paraphernalia, and the work began, interrupted frequently by the exultant shouts that marked a new finding. No one regretted Hugo. He made his men work magically; his example was a challenge. He could do more than any of them, and his hair and eyes, black as their own, his granite face, stern and indefatigable, gave him a natural dominion over them.
===Chinese===
一辆老式烧木柴的机车，沿着险诈的铁轨一路摇晃，载着他们驶入内陆。科学家们沉默下来，各怀心思。另一节车厢里，充作苦力的玛雅印第安人用他们爆豆般的语言喋喋不休。在最后一个烈日炙烤的停靠站，他们下了车，在虫声营营的一夜里睡过，便进了丛林。整整三个星期，他们劈斩砍伐着向前开路；草木在他们身后重新合拢，把宇宙隔绝得干干净净，就像没顶的海浪一般彻底。这是又热又难的活计，雨果却拿出一股精力投入其中，连早已断定他大有用的哈丁都为之咋舌。

一天，高山在天际隐隐现出身形，雨果第一次望见了乌克托托尔（Uctotol）——圣城。山坡上一株藤蔓在他的砍刀前倒下，接着又是一株——绿墙上豁开一个洞——它就立在那里，白得发亮，巨大，荒凉，静得像坟茔。他的手臂僵在半空。一种神秘的熟稔之感从他身上漫过——那转瞬即逝的相认之感，它总是这样轻易地一跃而化为对不朽的信仰。在那目眩神驰的一瞬，他仿佛认得那些宏大建筑的每一道轮廓，仿佛自己曾在那些街衢间奔跑过、生活过、爱过、死过——仿佛他几乎记得那些居民的名姓与眉目，虽然他们已死去数千年——仿佛他几乎追忆得起那里的语言与音乐——仿佛是命运亲自安排了这趟还乡。幻象熄灭了。他放声大喊。众人奔到他身旁，只见他浑身发抖，抬手直指。

成吨的草木被砍倒、推开。一座宅院盖了起来，还给劳工们搭了营盘。随后铁锹和镐头从箱子里撬了出来；科学家们布开各自的器物，工作开始了，又不时被标志新发现的欢呼所打断。谁也不曾后悔带上雨果。他使他手下的人干起活来如有神助；他这个人本身便是一记挑战。他比他们谁都干得多，而他那同他们一般乌黑的头发和眼睛，他那张花岗岩般严峻而不倦的脸，给了他一种与生俱来的统驭之权。

===Original===
All this﻿—the dark, starlit, plushy nights with their hypnotic silences, the vivid days of toil, the patient and single-minded men﻿—was respite to Hugo. It salved his tribulations. It brought him to a gradual assurance that any work with such men would be sufficient for him. He was going backward into the world instead of forward; that did not matter. He stood on the frontier of human knowledge. He was a factor in its preparation, and if what they carried back with them was no more than history, if it cast no new light on existing wants and perplexities, it still served a splendid purpose. Months rolled by unheeded; Hugo gathered friends among these men﻿—and the greatest of those friends was Daniel Hardin.

In their isolation and occasional loneliness each of them little by little stripped his past for the others. Only Hugo remained silent about himself until his reticence was conspicuous. He might never have spoken, except for the accident.
